\kappa_{\mathrm{F}}\Gamma(\alpha+1)\mathrm{d}s|z|^{-\alpha-d}\mathrm{d}z
for the
intensity of the Poisson measure
N
appearing in (
6
).

Let us emphasize that the limit law depends on
\mathrm{G}
only through
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
.
For example, when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
, it does not depend on
\kappa_{\mathrm{G}}>0
.

Finally, let us present a P.D.E. version of this result.

Corollary 24
.

Adopt the same assumptions and notations as in Theorem
23
. For each
t\geq 0
,
set
f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}v)=\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\in\mathrm{d}y,\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\in\mathrm{d}v)
,

\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}y)=\int_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}v)
and
f_{t}(\mathrm{d}y)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X_{t}^{*}\in\mathrm{d}y)
.
We know from Remark
21
that
(f^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a weak solution to (
19
),
while Proposition
16
tells us that
(f_{t})_{t\geq 0}
solves (
18
).
For a.e.
t\geq 0
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t}
tends to
f_{t}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
for
the weak convergence of probability measures on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proof.

Theorem
23
implies that
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{t}
(which is the law of
\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t}
) weakly goes to
f_{t}

for any
t\geq 0
such that
\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)=0
. Indeed,

X_{t}^{*}:\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})\to{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
is continuous for the
{\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{1}}
-topology
at any
w\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
such that
\Delta X^{*}_{t}(w)=0
,
so that
\Pi_{t}:\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}))\to\mathcal{P}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
defined by
\Pi_{t}(q)=q(X^{*}_{t}\in\cdot)
is continuous
at any
q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}))
such that
q(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)=0
.
For any
q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}))
, the set
\{t\geq 0:q(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)>0\}

is classically Lebesgue-null.
∎

3
Preliminaries: properties of the excursion measures

In this section, we establish a few properties of the excursion measures
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
, for
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and of the stable process. First, we make precise the paragraph around (
7
).

Lemma 25
.

For
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
an ISP
α,0
and
Z^{1}_{t}=Z_{t}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}
, the process

(Y_{t})_{t\geq 0}=(Z_{t}^{1}-\inf_{s\in[0,t]}Z_{s}^{1})_{t\geq 0}
is Markov, possesses a local time

(\xi_{t})_{t\geq 0}
at
0
, and this local time is uniquely defined if we impose the condition

\mathbb{E}[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\mathrm{d}\xi_{t}]=1
. Its right-continuous inverse

(\gamma_{u}=\inf\{s\geq 0:\xi_{s}>u\})_{u\geq 0}

is a
(1/2)
-stable
\mathbb{R}_{+}
-valued subordinator.
We introduce
\mathrm{J}=\{u\geq 0,\>\Delta\gamma_{u}>0\}
and, for
u\in\mathrm{J}
,

e_{u}=(Z_{(\gamma_{u-}+s)\wedge\gamma_{u}}-Z_{\gamma_{u-}})_{s\geq 0}
.
Almost surely,
e_{u}\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
\ell(e_{u})=\Delta\gamma_{u}
for all
u\in\mathrm{J}
.

Proof.

It is classical, see Bertoin
[
9
, Chapter VI, pages 156-157]
,
that
(Y_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is Markov and possesses a local time

(\xi_{t})_{t\geq 0}
at
0
,
i.e.
a continuous nondecreasing
additive functional whose support equals

\mathcal{Z}_{Y}={}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\{t\geq 0:Y_{t}=0\}}
. It is unique up to a multiplicative constant and we impose the condition

\mathbb{E}[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\mathrm{d}\xi_{t}]=1
. Since
(Z_{t}^{1})_{t\geq 0}
is a
symmetric stable process, the process

(\gamma_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is a
(1/2)
-stable subordinator, see Bertoin
[
9
, Section VIII, page 218]
.

It is also classical, see for instance Blumenthal and Getoor
[
13
, equation 2.4 page 58]
, that

\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{Y}=\cup_{u\in\mathrm{J}}(\gamma_{u-},\gamma_{u})

and that for any
u\in\mathrm{J}
,
Y_{\gamma_{u}}=0
. This implies that for
u\in\mathrm{J}
,

Z^{1}_{\gamma_{u}}=\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u}]}Z_{s}^{1}
and thus

Z_{\gamma_{u-}}^{1}\geq\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u-}]}Z_{s}^{1}\geq\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u}]}Z_{s}^{1}=Z^{1}_{\gamma_{u}}
.

Let us now show that for all
u\in\mathrm{J}
, all
t\in(\gamma_{u-},\gamma_{u})
, we have
Z^{1}_{t}>Z^{1}_{\gamma_{u-}}
.
Since
(\gamma_{u-},\gamma_{u})\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{Y}
, we have

\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u})}Z_{s}^{1}=\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u-}]}Z_{s}^{1}

and for all
t\in(\gamma_{u-},\gamma_{u})
,
Z^{1}_{t}>\inf_{s\in[0,t]}Z_{s}^{1}=\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u-}]}Z_{s}^{1}
.
But
Z^{1}_{\gamma_{u-}}=\inf_{s\in[0,\gamma_{u-}]}Z_{s}^{1}
, because
Y_{\gamma_{u-}}=0
:
by e.g.
[
6
, Lemma A.5]
or
[
28
, Section 2]
, this
follows from the fact that for the process
(Z_{t}^{1})_{t\geq 0}
,
the point
0
is regular for both
(0,\infty)
and
(-\infty,0)
, meaning that for

T_{+}=\inf\{t\geq 0,\>Z_{t}^{1}>0\}
and
T_{-}=\inf\{t\geq 0,\>Z_{t}^{1}<0\}
,
we have
\mathbb{P}_{0}(T_{+}=T_{-}=0)=1
.

We now fix
u\in\mathrm{J}
and show that
e_{u}\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
\ell(e_{u})=\Delta\gamma_{u}
.
We have
e_{u}(0)=0
by definition. We have seen that
Z_{t}^{1}>Z_{\gamma_{u-}}^{1}
for any

t\in(\gamma_{u-},\gamma_{u})
, implying that
e_{u}(s)=Z_{\gamma_{u-}+s}-Z_{\gamma_{u-}}\in\mathbb{H}

for all
s\in(0,\Delta\gamma_{u})
.
Finally,
e_{u}(\Delta\gamma_{u})\notin\mathbb{H}
, since
e_{u}^{1}(\Delta\gamma_{u})=Z^{1}_{\gamma_{u}}-Z^{1}_{\gamma_{u-}}\leq 0
.
∎

Let us now show that the excursion measures possess a scaling property.

Lemma 26
.

For
\lambda>0
, let
\Phi_{\lambda}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
be defined by
\Phi_{\lambda}(e)(t)=\lambda^{1/\alpha}e(t/\lambda)
.
It holds that

\Phi_{\lambda}\#\mathfrak{n}_{*}=\lambda^{1/2}\mathfrak{n}_{*}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\Phi_{\lambda}\#\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}=\lambda^{\beta/\alpha}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}\quad\text{for all $\beta>0$}.

Proof.

We start with the case where
\beta=*
. We recall that
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
was defined in the paragraph around (
7
),
starting from an ISP
α,0

(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
. By the scaling property of the stable
process,
(Z^{\lambda}_{t}=\lambda^{1/\alpha}Z_{t/\lambda})_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.
For any
a>0
,
(a\xi_{t/\lambda})_{t\geq 0}
is a local time of the
first coordinate of
(Z^{\lambda}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
reflected on its infimum, as in Lemma
25
.
But
(\xi^{\lambda}_{t}=\lambda^{1/2}\xi_{t/\lambda})_{t\geq 0}

is the only choice such that, recalling that
\gamma_{u}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\xi_{t}>u\}
and observing
that
\gamma^{\lambda}_{u}:=\lambda\gamma_{u/\lambda^{1/2}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\lambda^{1/2}\xi_{t/\lambda}>u\}
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\mathrm{d}\xi^{\lambda}_{t}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\gamma^{\lambda}_{u}}\mathrm{d}u\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\gamma_{u}}\mathrm{d}u\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\mathrm{d}\xi_{t}\Big]=1.

We used the substitution
\xi^{\lambda}_{t}=u
for the first equality, that

(\gamma_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is
(1/2)
-stable for the second one,
and the substitution
u=\xi_{t}
for the third one. Hence the triples

((Z_{t})_{t\geq 0},(\xi_{t})_{t\geq 0},(\gamma_{u})_{u\geq 0})
and

((Z_{t}^{\lambda})_{t\geq 0},(\xi_{t}^{\lambda})_{t\geq 0},(\gamma_{u}^{\lambda})_{u\geq 0})

have the same law. Setting now
\mathrm{J}^{\lambda}=\{u\geq 0,\>\Delta\gamma_{u}^{\lambda}>0\}=\{\lambda^{1/2}u:u\in\mathrm{J}\}

and, for
u\in\mathrm{J}^{\lambda}
,

e_{u}^{\lambda}=\Big(Z^{\lambda}_{(\gamma^{\lambda}_{u-}+s)\wedge\gamma_{u}^{\lambda}}-Z^{\lambda}_{\gamma^{\lambda}_{u-}}\Big)_{s\geq 0},

we conclude that
\Pi_{*}^{\lambda}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}^{\lambda}}\delta_{(u,e^{\lambda}_{u})}
has the same law as

\Pi_{*}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}
: it is a Poisson measure on

\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)
.

On the other hand, for
u=\lambda^{1/2}v\in\mathrm{J}^{\lambda}
, we have

e_{u}^{\lambda}=\Phi_{\lambda}(e_{v})
. Thus
\Pi_{*}^{\lambda}=\sum_{v\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(\lambda^{1/2}v,\Phi_{\lambda}(e_{v}))}
,
of which the intensity is
\lambda^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}u(\Phi_{\lambda}\#\mathfrak{n}_{*})(\mathrm{d}e)
.
Hence
\lambda^{-1/2}\Phi_{\lambda}\#\mathfrak{n}_{*}=\mathfrak{n}_{*}
as desired.

We carry on with the case where
\beta>0
, which is simpler. For a measurable
\varphi:\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
we simply
write, recalling (
8
) and using the scaling property of the stable process,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(\Phi_{\lambda}(e))\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathbb{H}}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{E}_{x}[\varphi(\Phi_{\lambda}(Z_{\cdot\land\ell(Z)}))]\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{H}}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{E}_{\lambda^{1/\alpha}x}[\varphi(Z_{\cdot\land\ell(Z)})]\mathrm{d}x.

Using the change of variables
y=\lambda^{1/\alpha}x
, we conclude that

\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(\Phi_{\lambda}(e))\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)=\lambda^{\beta/\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{H}}|y|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{E}_{y}[\varphi(Z_{\cdot\land\ell(Z)})]\mathrm{d}y=\lambda^{\beta/\alpha}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(e)\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)

as desired.
∎

We then give some tail estimates of the excursion measure
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
.

Lemma 27
.

Recall that

\ell(e)=\inf\{t>0,\>e(t)\notin{\mathbb{H}}\}
and
M(e)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e)]}|e(t)|

and set
M_{1}(e)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e))}e_{1}(t)
for all

e\in\mathcal{E}
, where
e_{1}(t)=e(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}
. There are some constants
c_{*},d_{*},e_{*}\in(0,\infty)

such that for all
t>0
, all
m>0
,

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>t)=c_{*}t^{-1/2},\quad\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m)=d_{*}m^{-\alpha/2}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M_{1}>m)=e_{*}m^{-\alpha/2}.

(24)

Moreover, (
9
) holds true when
\beta=*
.

Proof.

Using the notations of Lemma
26
, we have
\ell(\Phi_{\lambda}(e))=\lambda\ell(e)
,

M(\Phi_{\lambda}(e))=\lambda^{1/\alpha}M(e)
and
M_{1}(\Phi_{\lambda}(e))=\lambda^{1/\alpha}M_{1}(e)
.
By Lemma
26
, for any
\lambda>0
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>t)=\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\lambda\ell>t)}{\lambda^{1/2}},\quad\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m)=\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\lambda^{1/\alpha}M>m)}{\lambda^{1/2}},\quad\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M_{1}>m)=\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\lambda^{1/\alpha}M_{1}>m)}{\lambda^{1/2}}.

Choosing
\lambda=t
and
\lambda=m^{\alpha}
, we find (
24
),
with
c_{*}=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>1)
,
d_{*}=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>1)
and
e_{*}=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M_{1}>1)
.
First,
c_{*}>0
, because otherwise, we would have
\ell(e)=0
for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
.
Similarly,
e_{*}>0
(whence
d_{*}>0
), because otherwise, we would have
M_{1}(e)=0
for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
.
Next, if
c_{*}=\infty
, the process
\gamma_{u}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell(e)\Pi_{*}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)

(this formula follows from
the construction of
\Pi_{*}
,
see the paragraph around (
7
)) explodes immediately, which is not possible.
Moreover,
e_{*}<d_{*}
and
d_{*}
is finite: otherwise, we would have
\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>A)=\infty
for all
A>0
;
hence a.s., for any
A\in\mathbb{N}
, there would be infinitely many

u\in\mathrm{J}\cap[0,1]
such that
M(e_{u})>A
,
making explode
Z
during
[0,\gamma_{1}]
.
Finally,

\int_{\mathcal{E}}[\ell(e)\land 1+M(e)\land 1]\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>t)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m)\mathrm{d}m,

which is finite by (
24
): (
9
) holds true when
\beta=*
.
∎

We will need the following property concerning the entrance law of
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
.

Lemma 28
.

For
t>0
, let
k_{t}=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(e(t)\in\cdot,\ell(e)>t)
. There
are some constants
c_{0},c_{1}\in(0,\infty)
such that
for all
\varphi\in C_{b}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathbb{H}})
,

\lim_{t\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\varphi(a)a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=c_{0}\varphi(0)\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\varphi(a)|a|^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=c_{1}\varphi(0).

Proof.

We divide the proof in two steps.

Step 1.
Here we show that there exists a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for any
x>0
,

\mathfrak{n}_{*}\big(|e(1)|>x,\ell(e)>1\big)\leq C(x\lor 3)^{-\alpha}\log(x\lor 3).

(25)

First recall the definition of
\Pi_{*}
, see the paragraph around (
7
) and Lemma
25
:
consider an ISP
α,0

(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
, the right-continuous inverse

(\gamma_{u})_{u\geq 0}
of its local time at
\partial\mathbb{H}

and set
e_{u}=(Z_{(\gamma_{u-}+s)\wedge\gamma_{u}}-Z_{\gamma_{u-}})_{s\geq 0}

for
u\in\mathrm{J}=\{u\geq 0,\>\Delta\gamma_{u}>0\}
. Then
\Pi_{*}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}

is a Poisson measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)
. Let us set

\sigma=\inf\{u\in\mathrm{J},\>\ell(e_{u})>1\}
. Then, see for instance
Revuz and Yor
[
62
, Chapter XII, Lemma 1.13]
, we have

\mathfrak{n}_{*}\big(|e(1)|>x,\ell(e)>1\big)=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell(e)>1)\mathbb{P}\big(|Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}+1}-Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}}|>x\big).

(26)

Let us now introduce

\tilde{\gamma}_{u}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell(e){\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)\leq 1\}}\Pi_{*}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)
,
which is a subordinator whose Lévy measure is

\nu(\mathrm{d}r):=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell(e)\in\mathrm{d}r,\ell(e)\leq 1)=\frac{c_{*}}{2}r^{-3/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{r\leq 1\}}\mathrm{d}r

by Lemma
27
. Therefore,
\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\tilde{\gamma}_{u}}]=e^{c_{\theta}u}
for any
\theta>0
,
any
u\geq 0
, where
c_{\theta}=\frac{c_{*}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(e^{\theta r}-1)r^{-3/2}\mathrm{d}r
.

Now, recalling the definition of
\sigma
and that

\gamma_{u}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell(e)\Pi_{*}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)
,
we observe that
\gamma_{\sigma-}=\tilde{\gamma}_{\sigma}
. Moreover,
\sigma
is independent of
(\tilde{\gamma}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
because the two Poisson measures

{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>1\}}\Pi_{*}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)

and
{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)\leq 1\}}\Pi_{*}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)
are independent, and
\sigma
is a functional of the first
one whereas
(\tilde{\gamma}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is a functional of the second one. Finally,
\sigma
is
exponentially distributed with parameter
\lambda:=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell(e)>1)
. All in all,

\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\gamma_{\sigma-}}]=\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\tilde{\gamma}_{\sigma}}]=\lambda\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta\tilde{\gamma}_{u}}]e^{-\lambda u}\mathrm{d}u=\lambda\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{c_{\theta}u-\lambda u}\mathrm{d}u.

Since
\lim_{0}c_{\theta}=0
, there is
\theta_{*}\in(0,\alpha)
such that

\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta_{*}\gamma_{\sigma-}}]<\infty
. We write, for
x>1
and
t\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}+1}-Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}}|>x\big)\leq\mathbb{P}(\gamma_{\sigma-}>t)+\mathbb{P}(Z_{2t}^{*}>x/2).

where
Z_{t}^{*}=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|Z_{s}|
. By scale invariance of the stable process, we have

\mathbb{P}(Z_{t}^{*}>x)=\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}^{*}>x/t^{1/\alpha})
. Moreover, there is

M>0
such that
\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}^{*}>z)\leq Mz^{-\alpha}
, see for instance
Bertoin
[
9
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
in dimension one (which is enough). We conclude that

\mathbb{P}\big(|Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}+1}-Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}}|>x\big)\leq e^{-\theta_{*}t}\mathbb{E}[e^{\theta_{*}\tau_{\sigma-}}]+2^{\alpha+1}Mtx^{-\alpha},

If
x>3>e^{\theta_{*}/\alpha}
, we choose
t=\alpha\log(x)/\theta_{*}\geq 1
and find, for some constant
C>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}+1}-Z_{\gamma_{\sigma-}}|>x\big)\leq Cx^{-\alpha}(1+\log x)\leq 2Cx^{-\alpha}\log x.

Recalling (
26
), this shows (
25
) when
x>3
. Since
\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>1)<\infty
,
the case
x\leq 3
is obvious.

Step 2.

By Lemma
26
, for any
\lambda>0
, since
\ell(\Phi_{\lambda}(e))=\lambda\ell(e)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}}\varphi(a)a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(e(t))(e_{1}(t))^{\alpha/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>t\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/2}}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(\lambda^{1/\alpha}e(t/\lambda))(\lambda^{1/\alpha}e_{1}(t/\lambda))^{\alpha/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>t/\lambda\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e).

Choosing
\lambda=t
, we find

\int_{\mathbb{H}}\varphi(a)a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(t^{1/\alpha}e(1))[e_{1}(1)]^{\alpha/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>1\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathbb{H}}\varphi(t^{1/\alpha}a)a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{1}(\mathrm{d}a)\to c_{0}\varphi(0)

as
t\to 0
, where
c_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{H}}a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{1}(\mathrm{d}a)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}[e_{1}(1)]^{\alpha/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>1\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)
. We have
c_{0}>0
, since
\ell(e)>1
implies
e_{1}(1)>0
and since
\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>1)>0

by Lemma
27
. We next write

c_{0}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(e_{1}(1)>x^{2/\alpha},\ell(e)>1)\mathrm{d}x,

which is finite by (
25
). The very same arguments
show that
\lim_{t\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\varphi(a)|a|^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=c_{1}\varphi(0)
for any
\varphi\in C_{b}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathbb{H}})
, where

c_{1}:=\int_{\mathbb{H}}|a|^{\alpha/2}k_{1}(\mathrm{d}a)\in(0,\infty)
.
∎

The following result describes the strong Markov property of the excursion measures.

Lemma 29
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
.
Recall that
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
, an ISP
α,x
.
Endow
\mathcal{E}
with its canonical filtration
\mathcal{G}_{t}=\sigma(X_{s},s\in[0,t])
, where
X_{s}(e)=e(s)

is the canonical process. Consider
\rho:\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{\infty\}
a
(\mathcal{G}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
-stopping time, that is,
for all
t\geq 0
,
\{e\in\mathcal{E}:\rho(e)\leq t\}\in\mathcal{G}_{t}
. If
\beta=*
, assume moreover that

\rho(e)>0
for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
.
For any pair
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
of
measurable functions from
\mathcal{E}
into
\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\psi_{1}[(e(s\land\rho(e)))_{s\geq 0}]

\displaystyle\psi_{2}[(e((\rho(e)+u)\land\ell(e)))_{u\geq 0}]{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\rho(e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\psi_{1}[(e(s\land\rho(e)))_{s\geq 0}]\mathbb{E}_{e(\rho(e))}[\psi_{2}[(Z_{u\land\ell(Z)})_{u\geq 0}]]{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\rho(e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e).

Proof.

Recalling the definition (
8
) of
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
, this formula is directly inherited from the strong
Markov property of the stable process
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
.
When
\beta=*
, the proof is exactly the same as when
d=1
,
see e.g. Blumenthal
[
14
, Theorem 3.28 page 102]
.
∎

We next give some estimates concerning the stable process.

Lemma 30
.

Recall that
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
, an ISP
α,x
.
There are some constants
c,C\in(0,\infty)
such that
for any
x\in\mathbb{H}
, denoting by
x_{1}=x\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}
and
recalling that
\ell(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin{\mathbb{H}}\}
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)\sim c\;x_{1}^{\alpha/2}\quad\hbox{ as $|x|\to 0$}\quad\hbox{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|\land 1\Big]\leq C(x_{1}^{\alpha/2}\land 1).

Proof.

The estimate concerning
\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)
can be found in
Bertoin
[
9
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 2 page 219]
,
because by symmetry,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)=\mathbb{P}_{0}(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}Z_{t}^{1}<x_{1})
.
For the second estimate, we fix
x\in\mathbb{H}
and use Lemmas
27
and
29
.
We introduce the stopping time
\rho(e)=\inf\{t\geq 0:e_{1}(t)>x_{1}\}
,
where
e_{1}(t)=e(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}
.
Observe that
\rho
is
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e. positive (because
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
is carried by
\mathcal{E}_{0}
) and
that for any
m>0
,

\{\rho(e)<\ell(e)\}\cap\Big\{\sup_{t\in[\rho(e),\ell(e)]}|e(t)-e(\rho(e))|>m\Big\}\subset\{M(e)>m/2\}..

Indeed, if
|e(\rho(e))|>m/2
, then clearly
M(e)>m/2
, while if
|e(\rho(e))|\leq m/2
then

M(e)\geq\sup_{t\in[\rho(e),\ell(e)]}|e(t)|\geq\sup_{t\in[\rho(e),\ell(e)]}|e(t)-e(\rho(e))|-m/2>m/2
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m/2)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\rho(e)\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\sup_{t\in[\rho(e),\ell(e)]}|e(t)-e(\rho(e))|>m\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\rho(e)\}}\mathbb{P}_{e(\rho(e))}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|>m\Big)\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)

by Lemma
29
. But for all
e\in\mathcal{E}
such that
\ell(e)>\rho(e)
, we have
e_{1}(\rho(e))>x_{1}
, whence clearly

\mathbb{P}_{e(\rho(e))}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|>m\Big)\geq\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|>m\Big).

Next,

\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\rho(e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M_{1}>x_{1})
, where

M_{1}(e)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e))}e_{1}(t)
. All this gives

\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|>m\Big)\leq\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m/2)}{\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M_{1}>x_{1})}=\frac{2^{\alpha/2}d_{*}x_{1}^{\alpha/2}}{e_{*}m^{\alpha/2}}

by Lemma
27
. We conclude that

\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|\land 1\Big]=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|>m\Big)\mathrm{d}m\leq Cx_{1}^{\alpha/2},

which completes the proof since the left hand side is of course also bounded by
1
.
∎

We can now compute some tail distributions under the excursion measure
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
.

Lemma 31
.

Fix
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
. Recall that for
e\in\mathcal{E}
,

\ell(e)=\inf\{t>0,\>e(t)\notin{\mathbb{H}}\}
and
M(e)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e)]}|e(t)|
.
There are
c_{\beta},d_{\beta}\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
t>0
, all
m>0
,

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\ell>t)=c_{\beta}t^{-\beta/\alpha}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m)=d_{\beta}m^{-\beta}.

(27)

Moreover, (
9
) holds true when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
. Finally,
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\ell>1)=\infty
when

\beta\geq\alpha/2
.

Proof.

Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
27
, we find

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\ell>t)=\lambda^{-\beta/\alpha}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\ell>t/\lambda)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(M>m)=\lambda^{-\beta/\alpha}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(M>m/\lambda^{1/\alpha}).

Choosing
\lambda=t
and
\lambda=m^{\alpha}
, we get (
27
)
with
c_{\beta}=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\ell>1)
and
d_{\beta}=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(M>1)
. Recalling (
8
),

c_{\beta}=\int_{\mathbb{H}}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)\mathrm{d}x

is positive and finite, since
\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)\leq C(1\land x_{1}^{\alpha/2})
by Lemma
30
and since

\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
.
Similarly,
d_{\beta}=\int_{\mathbb{H}}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{P}_{x}(M(Z)>1)\mathrm{d}x
is positive and finite, because

\mathbb{P}_{x}(M(Z)>1)\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}|\land 1\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\Big(|x|+\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|\Big)\land 1\Big],

which is controlled thanks to Lemma
30
by
|x|\land 1+C(x_{1}^{\alpha/2}\land 1)\leq C(|x|^{\alpha/2}\land 1)
.

Moreover, (
9
) holds true since

\int_{\mathcal{E}}[\ell(e)\land 1+M(e)\land 1]\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell>t)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m)\mathrm{d}m<\infty

by (
27
). Finally, if
\beta\geq\alpha/2
,

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\ell>1)=\int_{\mathbb{H}}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)\mathrm{d}x

is infinite, since
\mathbb{P}_{x}(\ell(Z)>1)\sim cx_{1}^{\alpha/2}
as
|x|\to 0
.
∎

We now show that

\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
does not instantaneously leave a ball tangent to
\partial\mathbb{H}
at
0
.

Lemma 32
.

Fix
r>0
and set
\ell_{r}(e)=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\notin{B}_{d}(r\mathbf{e}_{1},r)\}
. Then
\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell_{r}=0)=0
.

Proof.

We have
\mathbb{H}\setminus B_{d}(r\mathbf{e}_{1},r)=\{x\in\mathbb{H}:2rx_{1}\leq|x|^{2}\}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{H}:rx_{1}<|x|^{2}\}=:D_{r}
,
so that
\ell_{r}(e)\geq\rho_{r}(e)
, where
\rho_{r}(e)=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\in D_{r}\}
. It thus suffices
that
\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r}=0)=0
. Since
\ell>0
on
\mathcal{E}
, it suffices that

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r}=0,\ell>\delta)=0
for all
\delta>0
and, by
scaling (see Lemma
26
), we may assume that
\delta=2
.
We would like to apply the Markov property (Lemma
29
) at time
\rho_{r}

but this would require that
\rho_{r}>0
, which is precisely what we want to check.

For
n\geq 1
, we set
D_{r,n}=\{x\in\mathbb{H}:rx_{1}<|x|^{2}-1/n\}
and
\rho_{r,n}(e)=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\in D_{r,n}\}
.
It suffices to check that

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\liminf_{n}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r,n}<\eta,\ell>2)=0.

(28)

Indeed, for each
\eta>0
,
\{\rho_{r}<\eta\}\subset\liminf_{n}\{\rho_{r,n}<\eta\}
, because
\rho_{r}(e)<\eta
implies that there is
t\in[0,\eta)

such that
e(t)\in D_{r}
, so that there is
n_{0}\geq 1
such that
e(t)\in\cap_{n\geq n_{0}}D_{r,n}
,
whence
\rho_{r,n}(e)<\eta
for all
n\geq n_{0}
.
Consequently,

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r}<\eta,\ell>2)\leq\liminf_{n}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r,n}<\eta,\ell>2)
. Thus

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r}=0,\ell>2)\leq\lim_{\eta\to 0}\liminf_{n}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r,n}<\eta,\ell>2)
.

Recall that
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
is carried by
\mathcal{E}_{0}=\{e\in\mathcal{E},e(0)=0\}
.
For each
n\geq 1
, each
e\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
, there is

\varepsilon>0
such that for all
t\in(0,\varepsilon]
,
e_{1}(t)>0
and
|e(t)|^{2}<1/n
,
implying that
\rho_{r,n}(e)\geq\varepsilon>0
. Thus we may apply Lemma
29
to write, if
\eta\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r,n}<\eta,\ell>2)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\rho_{r,n}(e)<\ell(e),\rho_{r,n}(e)<\eta\}}\mathbb{P}_{e(\rho_{r,n}(e))}(\ell(Z)>2-\rho_{r,n}(e))\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\rho_{r,n}(e)<\eta\land\ell(e)\}}\mathbb{P}_{e(\rho_{r,n}(e))}(\ell(Z)>1)\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e).

Recalling Lemma
30
and using that
e(\rho_{r,n}(e))\in D_{r,n}\subset D_{r}
,
we conclude that

\mathbb{P}_{e(\rho_{r,n}(e))}(\ell(Z)>1)\leq C[e_{1}(\rho_{r,n}(e))\land 1]^{\alpha/2}\leq C[|e(\rho_{r,n}(e))|^{2}\land 1]^{\alpha/2}=C[|e(\rho_{r,n}(e))|^{\alpha}\land 1],

the constant
C
being allowed to vary and to depend on
r
. Thus if
\rho_{r,n}(e)<\eta\land\ell(e)
,

\mathbb{P}_{e(\rho_{r,n}(e))}(\ell(Z)>1)\leq C\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\ell(e)]}|e(t)|^{\alpha}\land 1\Big).

Consequently,

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\rho_{r,n}<\eta,\ell>2)\leq C\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\ell(e)]}|e(t)|^{\alpha}\land 1\Big)\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e).

This last quantity does not depend on
n\geq 1
and tends to
0
as
\eta\to 0
by dominated convergence, since

\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\ell(e)]}|e(t)|^{\alpha}\to 0
for all
e\in\mathcal{E}_{0}
and since

\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e)]}|e(t)|^{\alpha}\land 1\Big)\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}((M(e))^{\alpha}\land 1)\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m^{1/\alpha})\mathrm{d}m,

which is finite since
\mathfrak{n}_{*}(M>m^{1/\alpha})=d_{*}m^{-1/2}

by Lemma
27
. We have proved (
28
).
∎

Finally, the following result is almost immediate.

Lemma 33
.

Suppose Assumption
1
, fix
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and recall that
Z
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
, an
ISP_{\alpha,x}
.
Then
\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tilde{\ell}(Z)=0)=1
, where
\tilde{\ell}(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}
.

Proof.

The process
Y_{t}=(Z_{t}-x)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}
is a one-dimensional symmetric
\alpha
-stable process
issued from
0
,
and by convexity of
\mathcal{D}
,
\tilde{\ell}(Z)\leq\inf\{t>0:Y_{t}\leq 0\}=:\rho(Y)
.
Using Bertoin
[
9
, Theorem 5 p 222]
,
\liminf_{t\to 0}t^{-1/\alpha}Y_{t}=-\infty
,
which implies that
\rho(Y)=0
.
∎

4
A crucial estimate

The goal of this section is to establish the following Lipschitz estimate, which is necessary to show that
the S.D.E. (
14
) defining the boundary process
(b_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is well-posed,
with a continuous dependence in the initial condition.

Proposition 34
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and suppose Assumption
1
.
There is a constant
C>0
such that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all

A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
,

\Delta(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}):=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big||g_{x}(A,e)-g_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},e)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\leq C(|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||).

The proof of this proposition is long and tedious, and relies on fine estimates on the excursion
measure
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
, as well as on a geometric inequality which will be proved in Appendix
A
. However,
this long section (as well as the whole Appendix
A
) can be omitted when
\mathcal{D}
is a Euclidean ball,
in which case this inequality is quite straightforward to obtain.

Proof of Proposition
34
when
\mathcal{D}=B_{d}(0,1)
.

Let us observe that
for
x\in\partial B_{d}(0,1)
and
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, we have

\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(A,y)\in{B}_{d}(0,1)\}={B}_{d}(\mathbf{e}_{1},1)
. Indeed, since
|x|=1
and

\mathbf{n}_{x}=-x
, we have
|x+Ay|^{2}<1
if and only if
|Ay|^{2}+2x\cdot Ay<0
if and only if

|y|^{2}-2\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot y<0
(because
|Ay|=|y|
and
x\cdot Ay=-A\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot Ay=-\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot y
)
if and only if
|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}<1
. Thus

\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=\inf\{t>0:h_{x}(A,e(t))\notin{B}_{d}(0,1)\}=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\notin{B}_{d}(\mathbf{e}_{1},1)\}=:\tilde{\ell}(e),

and
g_{x}(A,e)=(h_{x}(A,e(\tilde{\ell}(e)-)),h_{x}(A,e(\tilde{\ell}(e)))]\cap\partial B_{d}(0,1)=x+A\tilde{g}(e)
, where

\tilde{g}(e)=(e(\tilde{\ell}(e)-),e(\tilde{\ell}(e))]\cap\partial B_{d}(\mathbf{e}_{1},1).

As a consequence,
g_{x}(A,e)-g_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},e)=x-x^{\prime}+(A-A^{\prime})\tilde{g}(e)
, so that

\Big||g_{x}(A,e)-g_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},e)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq|(A-A^{\prime})\tilde{g}(e)|\leq||A-A^{\prime}|||\tilde{g}(e)|.

Since
|\tilde{g}(e)|\leq M(e)\land 2
, we find

\Delta(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq||A-A^{\prime}||\int_{\mathcal{E}}(M(e)\land 2)\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
and
the conclusion follows from (
9
).
∎

We will frequently use the following lemma.

Lemma 35
.

Grant Assumption
1
.
For
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, let
\mathcal{D}_{x,A}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}\}
.

(i) For all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, all
y\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}
,
|y|\leq\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})
.

(ii) There is
C>0
such that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, all
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
,

{\rm Vol}_{d}(\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}

where
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}=|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||
, and for all
z\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,

\displaystyle d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})=d(h_{x}(A,z),\partial\mathcal{D})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}},

\displaystyle d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})=d(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z),\partial\mathcal{D})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}.

Proof.

For (i), take
y\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}
. Then
|y|=|Ay|=|h_{x}(A,y)-x|\leq\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})
,
since
h_{x}(A,y)\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
.

For (ii), take
z\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
i.e.

h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
but

h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
. Thus

d(h_{x}(A,z),\partial\mathcal{D})\lor d(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z),\partial\mathcal{D})\leq|h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)-h_{x}(A,z)|\leq|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||\,|z|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}},

where
C=1\lor\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})
, since
|z|\leq\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})
by (i).
Since
\partial\mathcal{D}
is Lebesgue-null, we deduce that

\mathrm{Vol}_{d}(\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{0<d(h_{x}(A,z),\mathcal{D})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\}}\mathrm{d}z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{0<d(u,\mathcal{D})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\}}\mathrm{d}u,

since
h_{x}(A,\cdot)
is an isometry. Substituting
u=\Phi^{-1}(v)
with
\Phi
defined in
Lemma
78
, using that the Jacobian is bounded and that

\kappa^{-1}d(v,{B}_{d}(0,1))\leq d(\Phi^{-1}(v),\mathcal{D})\leq\kappa d(v,{B}_{d}(0,1))
, we get

\mathrm{Vol}_{d}(\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{0<d(v,{B}_{d}(0,1))\leq\kappa C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\}}\mathrm{d}v=C\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma\int_{1}^{1+\kappa C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}}r^{d-1}\mathrm{d}r,

which is smaller than
C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
as desired since
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
is uniformly bounded
(recall that
x,x^{\prime}
lie at the boundary of a bounded domain and that
A,A^{\prime}
are isometries).
∎

4.1
Joint law of the undershoot and overshoot

For
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
h_{x}(A,y)\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
, we set

\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=\Lambda\Big(h_{x}(A,y),h_{x}(A,z)\Big).

(29)

When
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,
\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=(h_{x}(A,y),h_{x}(A,z)]\cap\partial\mathcal{D}
.
To motivate this section, let us start with the following observation,
that immediately follows from (
12
) and (
29
).

Remark 36
.

For any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, any
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, any
e\in\mathcal{E}
, recalling that
h_{x}(A,y)=x+Ay
,
that
\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=\inf\{t>0:h_{x}(A,e(t))\notin\mathcal{D}\}

and introducing
\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e)=e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)-)
and
\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e)=e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))
, it holds that

g_{x}(A,e)=\bar{g}_{x}(A,\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e)).

Observe that
h_{x}(A,\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e))
(resp.
h_{x}(A,\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e))
) is the position of
h_{x}(A,e)

just before (resp. just after) exiting
\mathcal{D}
. As
g_{x}(A,e)
is a deterministic function of

(\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e))
, a first step towards the proof of Proposition
34
is to obtain
estimates on the law of
(\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e))
under
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
. For
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we introduce
\delta(y)=d(y,\partial\mathcal{D})
.

Proposition 37
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and grant Assumption
1
.
There is a constant
C
such that for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,

\displaystyle q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle):=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e)\in\mathrm{d}y,\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e)\in\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C[\delta(h_{x}(A,y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z\!+\!{\bm{1}}_{\{\beta\neq*\}}\frac{C}{|z|^{d+\beta}}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\in\mathbb{H},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}z,

where
\mathrm{d}y
and
\mathrm{d}z
stand for the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\delta_{0}
for the Dirac mass at
0
.

We believe that these bounds are sharp when
\beta=*

as they are derived from sharp estimates on the Green function
of isotropic stable processes in
C^{1,1}
domains. Moreover, we also believe that this inequality is an equality
when
\beta=*
and when
\mathcal{D}
is a ball.
The following result is likely to be well-known, although we found no precise reference.

Lemma 38
.

Grant Assumption
1
.
Recall that
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
, an ISP
α,x
. There is a constant
C>0

such that for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
, setting
\tilde{\ell}(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-}\in\mathrm{d}y,Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}\in\mathrm{d}z)\leq C{\bm{1}}_{\{y\in\mathcal{D},z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}K(x,y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z,

where

K(x,y,z)=|z-y|^{-d-\alpha}|x-y|^{\alpha-d}\min\Big(\Big[\frac{\delta(x)\delta(y)}{|x-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2},1\Big).

Observe that
\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e)=y
and
\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e)=z
means that the undershoot (resp. overshoot) of
h_{x}(A,e)
equals

h_{x}(A,y)
(resp.
h_{x}(A,z)
). Observe also that since
h_{x}(A,\cdot)
is an isometry, for
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}

an ISP
α,a
,
the process
(h_{x}(A,Z_{t}))_{t\geq 0}
is an ISP
{}_{\alpha,h_{x}(A,a)}
.
Admitting (
2
) and Lemma
38
, we thus find that when
\beta=*
, informally,

\displaystyle q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)=

\displaystyle a_{*}\lim_{r\to 0}r^{-\alpha/2}\mathbb{P}_{r\mathbf{e}_{1}}\Big(\mathfrak{u}(x,A,Z)\in\mathrm{d}y,\mathfrak{o}(x,A,Z)\in\mathrm{d}z\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\lim_{r\to 0}r^{-\alpha/2}K(h_{x}(A,r\mathbf{e}_{1}),h_{x}(A,y),h_{x}(A,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}|z-y|^{-d-\alpha}|y|^{-d}[\delta(h_{x}(A,y))]^{\alpha/2}.

However, even if we assume (
2
), such an argument is not so easy to make rigorous,
in particular because the undershoot/overshoot is
not a continuous function of the path.

Proof of Lemma
38
.

By Chen
[
30
, Theorem 2.4]

(with much less assumptions on the domain)
we know that there is a constant
C>0
such that for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
,

G(x,\mathrm{d}y):=\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\int_{0}^{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}{\bm{1}}_{\{Z_{t}\in\mathrm{d}y\}}\mathrm{d}t\Big]\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha-d}\min\Big(\Big[\frac{\delta(x)\delta(y)}{|x-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2},1\Big){\bm{1}}_{\{y\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y.

We next apply a classical method, see e.g. Bertoin
[
9
, Section III, page 76]
.
For
x\in\mathcal{D}
and
f:(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
bounded, we write, using the Poisson measure
N
defining
the stable process
Z
, see (
6
), as well as the compensation formula,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}[f(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)})]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(Z_{s-},Z_{s-}+z){\bm{1}}_{\{\tilde{\ell}(Z)\geq s\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{Z_{s-}+z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}N(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(Z_{s},Z_{s}+z){\bm{1}}_{\{\tilde{\ell}(Z)\geq s\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{Z_{s}+z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\int_{0}^{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(Z_{s},Z_{s}+z){\bm{1}}_{\{Z_{s}+z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(y,y+z){\bm{1}}_{\{y+z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}G(x,\mathrm{d}y).

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}[f(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)})]\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(y,y+z)|x-y|^{\alpha-d}\min\Big(\Big[\frac{\delta(x)\delta(y)}{|x-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2}\!\!\!,1\Big){\bm{1}}_{\{y+z\notin\mathcal{D},y\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(y,z)|x-y|^{\alpha-d}\min\Big(\Big[\frac{\delta(x)\delta(y)}{|x-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2}\!\!\!,1\Big){\bm{1}}_{\{z\notin\mathcal{D},y\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}},

whence the result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
37
.

We fix
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and use the short notation
{h_{x}(y)=h_{x}(A,y)}
,

\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)=\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)
,
\mathfrak{u}(x,e)=\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e)
and
\mathfrak{o}(x,e)=\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e)
.
We set
\mathcal{D}_{x}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D}\}
.

Case
\beta=*
.
Our goal is to prove that for any measurable
F:(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle q_{x}(F):=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}F(y,z)q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)\leq C\int_{\mathcal{D}_{x}\times\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}}F(y,z)G(x,y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z,

(30)

\displaystyle\hbox{where}\qquad G(x,y,z)=|y-z|^{-d-\alpha}|y|^{-d}[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}.

By density, it is sufficient to prove (
30
) when
F\in C_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{x}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}^{c},\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
provided we can show that

q_{x}((\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cup(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}))=0.

(31)

Fix any measurable
F:(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
By Remark
3
,
\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)>0
for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e
, so that

q_{x}(F)=\lim_{t\to 0}q_{x,t}(F),\quad\hbox{where}\quad q_{x,t}(F)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}F(\mathfrak{u}(x,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,e)){\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)>t\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e).

We now apply the Markov property at time
t>0
, see Lemma
29
, to find

q_{x,t}(F)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\mathbb{E}_{e(t)}[F(\mathfrak{u}(x,Z),\mathfrak{o}(x,Z))]{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)>t\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e),

Since
Z
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{a}
, an ISP
α,a
and since
h_{x}
is an isometry,

\tilde{Z}=h_{x}(Z)
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{a}
, an ISP
{}_{\alpha,h_{x}(a)}
. Moreover,

\tilde{\ell}(\tilde{Z}):=\inf\{t>0:\tilde{Z}_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}=\bar{\ell}_{x}(Z)
,

\mathfrak{u}(x,Z)=h_{x}^{-1}(\tilde{Z}_{\tilde{\ell}(\tilde{Z})-})
and
\mathfrak{o}(x,Z)=h_{x}^{-1}(\tilde{Z}_{\tilde{\ell}(\tilde{Z})})
,
whence

\mathbb{E}_{a}[F(\mathfrak{u}(x,Z),\mathfrak{o}(x,Z))]\!=\!\mathbb{E}_{a}[F(h_{x}^{-1}(\tilde{Z}_{\tilde{\ell}(\tilde{Z})-}),h_{x}^{-1}(\tilde{Z}_{\tilde{\ell}(\tilde{Z})})))]\!=\!\mathbb{E}_{h_{x}(a)}[F(h_{x}^{-1}(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-}),h_{x}^{-1}(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}))]

and thus, by Lemma
38
,

\mathbb{E}_{a}[F(\mathfrak{u}(x,Z),\mathfrak{o}(x,Z))]\leq C\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}F(h_{x}^{-1}(y),h_{x}^{-1}(z)){\bm{1}}_{\{y\in\mathcal{D},z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}K(h_{x}(a),y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

(32)

Consequently,

\displaystyle q_{x,t}(F)\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathcal{E}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}F(h_{x}^{-1}(y),h_{x}^{-1}(z)){\bm{1}}_{\{y\in\mathcal{D},z\notin\mathcal{D}\}}K(h_{x}(e(t)),y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)>t\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e).

Performing the substitution
y\mapsto h_{x}(y),z\mapsto h_{x}(z)
, we find

\displaystyle q_{x,t}(F)\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathcal{E}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}F(y,z){\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}K(h_{x}(e(t)),h_{x}(y),h_{x}(z))\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)>t\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathcal{E}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}F(y,z){\bm{1}}_{\{y\in\mathcal{D}_{x},z\notin\mathcal{D}_{x}\}}K(h_{x}(e(t)),h_{x}(y),h_{x}(z))\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>t,e(t)\in\mathcal{D}_{x}\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e),

since
\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)>t
implies that
\ell(e)>t
and
h_{x}(e(t))\in\mathcal{D}
.
Introducing the measure
k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=n_{*}(e(t)\in\mathrm{d}a,\ell(e)>t)
, which is carried by
\mathbb{H}
,
we get

q_{x,t}(F)\leq C\int_{\mathbb{H}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}F(y,z){\bm{1}}_{\{y\in\mathcal{D}_{x},z\notin\mathcal{D}_{x},a\in\mathcal{D}_{x}\}}K(h_{x}(a),h_{x}(y),h_{x}(z))\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}zk_{t}(\mathrm{d}a).

For all
a\in\mathcal{D}_{x}
, it holds that
\delta(h_{x}(a))=d(a,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x})\leq a_{1}
, where
a_{1}:=a\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}>0
:
since
\mathcal{D}_{x}\subset\mathbb{H}
,
a-a_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}\in\partial\mathbb{H}
cannot belong to
\mathcal{D}_{x}
,
so that
d(a,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x})\leq|a-(a-a_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1})|=a_{1}
.
Consequently, recalling the expression of
K
and that
h_{x}
is an isometry,

\displaystyle K(h_{x}(a),h_{x}(y),h_{x}(z))\leq a_{1}^{\alpha/2}H(a,x,y,z),

\displaystyle\hbox{where}\quad H(a,x,y,z)=a_{1}^{-\alpha/2}|z-y|^{-d-\alpha}|a-y|^{\alpha-d}\Big(\Big[\frac{a_{1}\delta(h_{x}(y))}{|a-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2}\land 1\Big).

Moreover,
a\in\mathcal{D}_{x}
implies that
|a|\leq
diam
(\mathcal{D})=:D
, see Lemma
35
-(i).
All in all,

\displaystyle q_{x,t}(F)\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)}f(a)a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a),\quad\hbox{where}\quad f(a)=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{x}\times\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}}F(y,z)H(a,x,y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

Choosing
F={\bm{1}}_{(\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cup(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x})}
, we have
f\equiv 0

(since
\partial\mathcal{D}_{x}
is Lebesgue-null), whence

q_{x}((\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cup(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}))=\lim_{t\to 0}q_{x,t}(F)=0,

and we have checked (
31
). Consider next
F\in C_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{x}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}^{c},\mathbb{R}_{+})
and assume
we can show that

\displaystyle\hbox{$f$ is bounded on $\mathbb{H}\cap{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)$}\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{a\to 0}f(a)=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{x}\times\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}}F(y,z)G(x,y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

(33)

Then by Lemma
28
, which tells us that
a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\to c_{0}\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}a)

weakly as
t\to 0
,

q_{x}(F)=\lim_{t\to 0}q_{x,t}(F)\leq C\lim_{t\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap\bar{B}_{d}(0,D)}f(a)a_{1}^{\alpha/2}k_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)=c_{0}C\int_{\mathcal{D}_{x}\times\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}}F(y,z)G(x,y,z)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z,

which shows (
30
).

It remains to prove (
33
). Since
F\in C_{c}(\mathcal{D}_{x}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}^{c},\mathbb{R}_{+})
, there exists

\varepsilon>0
such that
F(y,z)=0
as soon as
|y-z|\leq\varepsilon
or
|z|\geq 1/\varepsilon
, whence

F(y,z)H(a,x,y,z)\leq||F||_{\infty}{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|<1/\varepsilon\}}a_{1}^{-\alpha/2}\varepsilon^{-d-\alpha}|a-y|^{\alpha-d}\Big(\frac{a_{1}^{\alpha/2}[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|a-y|^{\alpha}}\land 1\Big).

But
\delta(h_{x}(y))\leq|h_{x}(a)-h_{x}(y)|+\delta(h_{x}(a))\leq|a-y|+a_{1}
, whence, for some
C

depending on
F
,

\displaystyle F(y,z)H(a,x,y,z)\leq

\displaystyle C{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|<1/\varepsilon\}}\Big(|a-y|^{\alpha/2-d}+\frac{a_{1}^{\alpha/2}}{|a-y|^{d}}\land\frac{1}{a_{1}^{\alpha/2}|a-y|^{d-\alpha}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|<1/\varepsilon\}}|y-a|^{\alpha/2-d},

(34)

since
u\land v\leq\sqrt{uv}
.
Consequently, for all
a\in\mathbb{H}\cap{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)
, recalling the definition of
f
and
that
\mathcal{D}_{x}\subset B_{d}(0,D)

by Lemma
35
-(i), we find

\displaystyle f(a)\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,D)}|y-a|^{\alpha/2-d}\mathrm{d}y\int_{B_{d}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}z=C\int_{{B}_{d}(-a,D)}|u|^{\alpha/2-d}\mathrm{d}u\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,2D)}|u|^{\alpha/2-d}\mathrm{d}u,

so that
f
is bounded on
\mathbb{H}\cap{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)
. Next, we clearly have

\lim_{a\to 0}H(a,x,y,z)=G(x,y,z)
for each fixed
y,z\in\mathcal{D}_{x}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}^{c}
.
Hence to establish the limit in (
33
), we only have to check the uniform integrability
property
\lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_{a\in\mathbb{H}\cap{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)}R_{M}(a)=0
, where

R_{M}(a)=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{x}\times\mathcal{D}_{x}^{c}}F(y,z)H(a,x,y,z){\bm{1}}_{\{F(y,z)H(a,x,y,z)\geq M\}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

By (
34
) and since
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x}\subset{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)
, for all
a\in\mathbb{H}\cap{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muB}_{d}(0,D)
,
substituting
u=y-a

R_{M}(a)\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,D)}|y-a|^{\alpha/2-d}{\bm{1}}_{\{|y-a|^{\alpha/2-d}\geq M/C\}}\mathrm{d}y\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,2D)}|u|^{\alpha/2-d}{\bm{1}}_{\{|u|^{\alpha/2-d}\geq M/C\}}\mathrm{d}u,

which does not depend on
a
and tends to
0
as
M\to\infty
.

Case
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
.
We write
q_{x}=q_{x}^{1}+q_{x}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle q_{x}^{1}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(h_{x}(e(0))\in\mathcal{D},\mathfrak{u}(x,e)\in\mathrm{d}y,\mathfrak{o}(x,e)\in\mathrm{d}z),

\displaystyle q_{x}^{2}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(h_{x}(e(0))\notin\mathcal{D},\mathfrak{u}(x,e)\in\mathrm{d}y,\mathfrak{o}(x,e)\in\mathrm{d}z).

We clearly have

\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)=0
when
h_{x}(e(0))\notin\mathcal{D}
and, recalling the definitions of
\mathfrak{u}
and
\mathfrak{o}
and
the convention that
e(0-)=0
,

q_{x}^{2}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(h_{x}(e(0))\notin\mathcal{D},0\in\mathrm{d}y,e(0)\in\mathrm{d}z)=\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}y)\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(h_{x}(e(0))\notin\mathcal{D},e(0)\in\mathrm{d}z).

By definition of
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
, see (
8
), and since
\mathbb{P}_{a}(Z(0)\in\mathrm{d}z)=\delta_{a}(\mathrm{d}z)
, this gives

q_{x}^{2}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)=\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}y)\int_{a\in\mathbb{H}}|a|^{-d-\beta}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\delta_{a}(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}a=\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}y)|z|^{-d-\beta}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\in\mathbb{H},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}z.

By definition of
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
and by (
32
),

\displaystyle q^{1}_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)=

\displaystyle\int_{a\in\mathbb{H}}|a|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{P}_{a}(h_{x}(Z(0))\in\mathcal{D},\mathfrak{u}(x,Z)\in\mathrm{d}y,\mathfrak{o}(x,Z)\in\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{a\in\mathbb{H}}|a|^{-d-\beta}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(a)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathbb{P}_{a}(\mathfrak{u}(x,Z)\in\mathrm{d}y,\mathfrak{o}(x,Z)\in\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{a\in\mathbb{H}}|a|^{-d-\beta}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(a)\in\mathcal{D}\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}K(h_{x}(a),h_{x}(y),h_{x}(z))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}|z-y|^{-d-\alpha}m(x,y)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z,

where

m(x,y)=\int_{a\in\mathbb{H}}|a|^{-d-\beta}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(a)\in\mathcal{D}\}}|a-y|^{\alpha-d}\Big(\Big[\frac{\delta(h_{x}(a))\delta(h_{x}(y))}{|a-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2}\land 1\Big)\mathrm{d}a.

It only remains to show that for some constant
C>0
, for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D}
,

m(x,y)\leq C|y|^{-d}[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}.

(35)

We write
m(x,y)=m_{1}(x,y)+m_{2}(x,y)
, where
m_{1}
(resp.
m_{2}
) corresponds to the integral on
a\in\mathbb{H}\setminus B_{d}(y,|y|/2)
(resp.
a\in B_{d}(y,|y|/2)
).

First,
a\notin B_{d}(y,|y|/2)
implies that

|y-a|\geq|y|/2
, and
h_{x}(a)\in\mathcal{D}
implies that
|a|\leq
diam
(\mathcal{D})=:D
, see Lemma
35
-(i).
Moreover,
\delta(h_{x}(a))\leq|h_{x}(a)-x|=|a|
, since
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. Thus

\displaystyle m_{1}(x,y)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{B_{d}(0,D)\cap B_{d}(y,|y|/2)^{c}}|a|^{-d-\beta}|a-y|^{-d}[\delta(h_{x}(a))\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}|y|^{-d}\int_{B_{d}(0,D)}|a|^{-d-\beta+\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}a.

Since
\beta<\alpha/2
, we conclude that
m_{1}(x,y)\leq C[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}|y|^{-d}
.

Next,
a\in B_{d}(y,|y|/2)
implies that
|a|>|y|/2
. Using that
\delta(h_{x}(a))\leq\delta(h_{x}(y))+|y-a|
,

\displaystyle m_{2}(x,y)\leq

\displaystyle C|y|^{-d-\beta}\int_{B_{d}(y,|y|/2)}|a-y|^{\alpha-d}\Big(\Big[\frac{\delta(h_{x}(y))(\delta(h_{x}(y))+|a-y|)}{|a-y|^{2}}\Big]^{\alpha/2}\land 1\Big)\mathrm{d}a.

But

\displaystyle\frac{\delta(h_{x}(y))(\delta(h_{x}(y))+|a-y|)}{|a-y|^{2}}\land 1\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(\delta(h_{x}(y)))^{2}}{|a-y|^{2}}\land 1+\frac{\delta(h_{x}(y))}{|a-y|}\land 1\leq 2\frac{\delta(h_{x}(y))}{|a-y|},

so that

m_{2}(x,y)\leq C|y|^{-d-\beta}[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}\int_{B_{d}(y,|y|/2)}|a-y|^{\alpha/2-d}\mathrm{d}a=C|y|^{\alpha/2-d-\beta}[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}.

This last quantity is bounded by
C|y|^{-d}[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}
, because

\alpha/2>\beta
and because
|y|\leq
diam
(\mathcal{D})
, see Lemma
35
-(i).
∎

4.2
A geometric inequality

Recall that
\delta(y)=d(y,\partial\mathcal{D})
and that
\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=\Lambda(h_{x}(A,y),h_{x}(A,z))
.

Proposition 39
.

Grant Assumption
1
.
There is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
{x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
,
setting
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}=|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||
,

(i) for all
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

such that
h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))}\Big),

(ii) for all
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

such that
h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}
,

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\Big),

(iii) for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,0,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},0,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}},

(iv) for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,0,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},0,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C.

The proof of this proposition is handled in Appendix
A
, to which we refer for some illustrations.
The shape of these upperbounds is of course motivated by the estimates we have on the joint law of the
undershoot/overshoot, see Proposition
37
. However, these bounds look quite sharp.
Let us also mention Remark
82
, where we show that the strong convexity assumption of
\mathcal{D}

cannot be removed, although it is likely that some more tricky upperbounds should hold without such an assumption.

4.3
Bounding a few integrals

We will need the following bounds.

Proposition 40
.

Fix
\beta\in(0,1)
and grant Assumption
1
.
There is
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
,
setting
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}=|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||
,

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\Big)\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(A,y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z\leq C,

(36)

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\!\!\!\frac{[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z))]^{\alpha/2}[\delta(h_{x}(A,y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}},

(37)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\leq C,

(38)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{1}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\mathrm{d}z\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}.

(39)

We will use the following inequality.

Lemma 41
.

There is a constant
c_{d,\alpha}>0
such that
for
x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\hbox{if $|x|=1<|z|$,}\qquad\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{(1-|a|^{2})^{\alpha/2}}{|a-z|^{d+\alpha}|a-x|^{d}}\mathrm{d}a\leq\frac{c_{d,\alpha}}{|z-x|^{d}(|z|^{2}-1)^{\alpha/2}}.

Proof.

This inequality may actually be an equality and a more straightforward proof could likely be
provided.
Let
Z
be some ISP
α,0
and set
\tau(Z)=\inf\{t>0,|Z_{t}|>1\}
.
By Blumenthal, Getoor and Ray
[
12
, Theorem A]
,
there is
c_{1,\alpha,d}
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

with
|x|<1
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(Z_{\tau(Z)}\in\mathrm{d}z)=\frac{c_{1,\alpha,d}(1-|x|^{2})^{\alpha/2}}{|z-x|^{d}(|z|^{2}-1)^{\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|>1\}}\mathrm{d}z.

By Kyprianou, Rivero and Satitkanitkul
[
49
, Corollary 1.4-(ii)]
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
|x|<1
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}(Z_{\tau(Z)-}\in\mathrm{d}a,Z_{\tau(Z)}\in\mathrm{d}z)=\frac{c_{2,\alpha,d}\,\varphi(\zeta(x,a))}{|x-a|^{d-\alpha}|z-a|^{d+\alpha}}{\bm{1}}_{\{|a|<1<|z|\}}\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}z,

where
\varphi(u)=\int_{0}^{u}(v+1)^{-d/2}v^{\alpha/2-1}\mathrm{d}v
and

\zeta(x,a)=\frac{(1-|x|^{2})(1-|a|^{2})}{|x-a|^{2}}
.
Hence if
|x|<1<|z|
,

\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{\varphi(\zeta(x,a))}{(1-|x|^{2})^{\alpha/2}|x-a|^{d-\alpha}|z-a|^{d+\alpha}}\mathrm{d}a=\frac{c_{1,\alpha,d}}{c_{2,\alpha,d}|z-x|^{d}(|z|^{2}-1)^{\alpha/2}}.

(40)

Fix now
x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
|x|=1<|z|
, and consider
x_{n}=\frac{n-1}{n}x
.
Since
\varphi(u)\sim_{0}(2/\alpha)u^{\alpha/2}
,

\text{for all $a\in B_{d}(0,1)$,}\quad\lim_{n}\frac{\varphi(\zeta(x_{n},a))}{(1-|x_{n}|^{2})^{\alpha/2}|x_{n}-a|^{d-\alpha}|z-a|^{d+\alpha}}=\frac{2(1-|a|^{2})^{\alpha/2}}{\alpha|x-a|^{d}|z-a|^{d+\alpha}}.

The conclusion follows from (
40
) applied to
x_{n}
and from the Fatou lemma.
∎

We can now give the

Proof of Proposition
40
.

We divide the proof in several steps.

Step 1.
There is
C>0
such that for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,

z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,

f_{x}(z):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(A,y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C}{|z|^{d}[\delta(h_{x}(A,z))]^{\alpha/2}}.

Indeed, we substitute
u=h_{x}(A,y)
,
v=h_{x}(A,z)
and write

f_{x}(z)=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{[\delta(u)]^{\alpha/2}}{|v-u|^{d+\alpha}|u-x|^{d}}\mathrm{d}u.

We now use the diffeomorphism
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\Phi(\mathcal{D})=B_{d}(0,1)

introduced in Lemma
78
and substitute

a=\Phi(u)
. The Jacobian is bounded, and we get

f_{x}(z)\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{[\delta(\Phi^{-1}(a))]^{\alpha/2}}{|v-\Phi^{-1}(a)|^{d+\alpha}|\Phi^{-1}(a)-x|^{d}}\mathrm{d}a.

But, see Lemma
78
, we have, for
a\in B_{d}(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\delta(\Phi^{-1}(a))\leq\kappa d(a,\partial B_{d}(0,1))=\kappa(1-|a|)\leq\kappa(1-|a|^{2}),

\displaystyle|v-\Phi^{-1}(a)|\geq\kappa^{-1}|\Phi(v)-a|\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad|\Phi^{-1}(a)-x|\geq\kappa^{-1}|a-\Phi(x)|.

Hence

f_{x}(z)\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{(1-|a|^{2})^{\alpha/2}}{|a-\Phi(v)|^{d+\alpha}|a-\Phi(x)|^{d}}\mathrm{d}a\leq\frac{C}{|\Phi(v)-\Phi(x)|^{d}(|\Phi(v)|^{2}-1)^{\alpha/2}}

because
|\Phi(v)|>1=|\Phi(x)|
, see Lemma
41
.
To conclude the step, it suffices to notice that

\displaystyle|\Phi(v)|^{2}-1\geq|\Phi(v)|-1=d(\Phi(v),\partial B_{d}(0,1))\geq\kappa^{-1}d(v,\partial\mathcal{D})=\kappa^{-1}\delta(h_{x}(A,z)),

\displaystyle|\Phi(v)-\Phi(x)|\geq\kappa^{-1}|v-x|=\kappa^{-1}|h_{x}(A,z)-x|=\kappa^{-1}|z|.

Step 2.
We now bound the first term (with
|z|\land 1
) in (
36
). Calling it
I_{1}(x)
,
we see that

I_{1}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|z|\land 1)f_{x}(z){\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}z\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|z|\land 1}{|z|^{d}[\delta(h_{x}(A,z))]^{\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}z.

As in Step 1, we substitute
v=h_{x}(A,z)
(whence
|z|=|v-x|
) and then
b=\Phi(v)
to write

\displaystyle I_{1}(x)\leq C\int_{\mathcal{D}^{c}}\frac{|v-x|\land 1}{|v-x|^{d}[\delta(v)]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}v\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)^{c}}\frac{|\Phi^{-1}(b)-x|\land 1}{|\Phi^{-1}(b)-x|^{d}[\delta(\Phi^{-1}(b))]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}b.

By Lemma
78
, we have
\kappa^{-1}|b-\Phi(x)|\leq|\Phi^{-1}(b)-x|\leq\kappa|b-\Phi(x)|
, as well as

\delta(\Phi^{-1}(b))\geq\kappa^{-1}d(b,\partial B_{d}(0,1))=\kappa(|b|-1)
. Thus

I_{1}(x)\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)^{c}}\frac{|b-\Phi(x)|\land 1}{|b-\Phi(x)|^{d}(|b|-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}b=C\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{1}^{\infty}r^{d-1}\frac{|r\sigma-\Phi(x)|\land 1}{|r\sigma-\Phi(x)|^{d}(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma.

By rotational invariance and since
|\Phi(x)|=1
, we have
I_{1}(x)\leq CJ_{1}
, where

J_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{1}^{\infty}r^{d-1}\frac{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\land 1}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d}(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma,

and it only remains to check that
J_{1}
is finite. We write
J_{1}\leq J_{11}+J_{12}
, where

\displaystyle J_{11}=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{2}^{\infty}r^{d-1}\frac{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\land 1}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d}(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{2}^{\infty}\frac{r^{d-1}}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d}(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma,

\displaystyle J_{12}=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{1}^{2}r^{d-1}\frac{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\land 1}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d}(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d-1}(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma.

For
r\geq 2
, we have
|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq r-1\geq\frac{r}{2}
, whence

J_{11}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\int_{2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r^{1+\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\sigma<\infty.

For
r\in[1,2]
, we have
|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq r-1
and
|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|

(because
|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}=1+r^{2}-2r\sigma_{1}\geq 2r(1-\sigma_{1})\geq 2(1-\sigma_{1})=|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}
), so that

|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq\frac{r-1+|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|}{2}
. Hence

J_{12}\leq C\int_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{(r-1+|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|)^{d-1}}\leq C\int_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\Big(1+\log\frac{1}{r-1}\Big)<\infty.

Step 3.
We now show that for all
\theta\in(0,1)
, there is
C_{\theta}>0
such that
for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,

K_{\theta}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{|u-x|^{d-1}[\delta(u)]^{\theta}}\leq C_{\theta}.

We substitute
a=\Phi(u)
and find, similarly as in Steps 1 and 2,

K_{\theta}(x)\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{\mathrm{d}a}{|\Phi^{-1}(a)-x|^{d-1}[\delta(\Phi^{-1}(a))]^{\theta}}\leq C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{\mathrm{d}a}{|a-\Phi(x)|^{d-1}(1-|a|)^{\theta}}.

By rotational invariance and since
|\Phi(x)|=1
,
K_{\theta}(x)\leq CL_{\theta}
, where

L_{\theta}=C\int_{{B}_{d}(0,1)}\frac{\mathrm{d}a}{|a-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d-1}(1-|a|)^{\theta}}=C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\frac{r^{d-1}\mathrm{d}\sigma\mathrm{d}r}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d-1}(1-r)^{\theta}}.

But for
r\in(0,1)
,

|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq 1-r
and
|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq r|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|
(because
|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}=1+r^{2}-2r\sigma_{1}\geq 2r(1-\sigma_{1})=r|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}\geq r^{2}|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}
), so that

|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq\frac{1-r+r|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|}{2}
and thus

\frac{r}{|r\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|}\leq\frac{2r}{1-r+r|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|}=\frac{2}{\frac{1-r}{r}+|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|}\leq\frac{2}{1-r+|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|}.

Consequently,

L_{\theta}\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(1-r)^{\theta}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{(1-r+|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|)^{d-1}}\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(1-r)^{\theta}}\Big(1+\log\frac{1}{1-r}\Big)<\infty.

Step 4.
We next bound the second term in (
36
). Naming it
I_{2}(x)
, we have

I_{2}(x)=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\frac{|y-z|\land 1}{|y|^{d-1}|y-z|^{d+\alpha}[\delta(h_{x}(A,y))]^{1-\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

We substitute
u=h_{x}(A,y)
,
v=h_{x}(A,z)
to find

I_{2}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathcal{D}^{c}}\frac{|u-v|\land 1}{|u-x|^{d-1}|u-v|^{d+\alpha}[\delta(u)]^{1-\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}u.

But for
u\in\mathcal{D}
, we have
\mathcal{D}^{c}\subset B_{d}(u,\delta(u))^{c}
(because

\delta(u)=d(u,\mathcal{D}^{c})
). Moreover,

\int_{B_{d}(u,\delta(u))^{c}}\frac{|u-v|\land 1}{|u-v|^{d+\alpha}}\mathrm{d}v=\int_{|w|>\delta(u)}\frac{|w|\land 1}{|w|^{d+\alpha}}\mathrm{d}w=:\rho_{\alpha}(u).

We thus have

I_{2}(x)\leq\int_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{\rho_{\alpha}(u)}{|u-x|^{d-1}[\delta(u)]^{1-\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}u.

Recall that
\delta(u)
is bounded (for
u\in\mathcal{D}
) by the diameter of
\mathcal{D}
.
One easily checks that
\rho_{\alpha}(u)\leq C
if
\alpha<1
, that
\rho_{\alpha}(u)\leq C(1+\log(1+\frac{1}{\delta(u)}))\leq(\delta(u))^{-1/4}
if
\alpha=1
, and that
\rho_{\alpha}(u)\leq C(\delta(u))^{1-\alpha}
if
\alpha\in(1,2)
, so that in any case,

\frac{\rho_{\alpha}(u)}{|u-x|^{d-1}[\delta(u)]^{1-\alpha/2}}\leq\frac{C}{|u-x|^{d-1}[\delta(u)]^{\theta}}\quad\text{whence}\quad I_{2}(x)\leq CK_{\theta}(x),

with
\theta=1-\alpha/2
if
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\theta=3/4
if
\alpha=1
and
\theta=\alpha/2

if
\alpha\in(1,2)
.
We always have
\theta\in(0,1)
, whence
\sup_{x\in\partial\mathcal{D}}I_{2}(x)<\infty
by Step 3.
Recalling Step 2, we have checked (
36
).

Step 5.
Calling
I_{3}(x,x^{\prime})
the left hand side of (
37
) and recalling Step 1, we have

\displaystyle I_{3}(x,x^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z))]^{\alpha/2}f_{x}(z){\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z|^{d}[\delta(h_{x}(A,z))]^{\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}z.

We now use the notation of Lemma
79
and observe that
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}

if and only if
z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, while
\delta(h_{x}(A,z))=d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})
. Hence

I_{3}(x,x^{\prime})\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})]^{\alpha/2}}{|z|^{d}[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})]^{\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\}}\mathrm{d}z=I_{31}(x,x^{\prime})+I_{32}(x,x^{\prime}),

I_{31}(x,x^{\prime})
(resp.
I_{32}(x,x^{\prime})
) being the integral on
B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
(resp.
B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})^{c}
).
Using the functions
\psi_{x,A},\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}:B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})\to\mathbb{R}
introduced in Lemma
79
,
we may write

\displaystyle I_{31}(x,x^{\prime})=

\displaystyle C\int_{B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\frac{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})]^{\alpha/2}}{|z|^{d}[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})]^{\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(z_{2},\dots,z_{d})<z_{1}\leq\psi_{x,A}(z_{2},\dots,z_{d})\}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}{\bm{1}}_{\{\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)<\psi_{x,A}(v)\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d}}\int_{\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)}^{\psi_{x,A}(v)}\frac{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})]^{\alpha/2}}{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}z_{1},

where we have set
z=(z_{1},v)
and we used that
|z|\geq|v|
. But, see Lemma
79
, we have

d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})\leq z_{1}-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)
and

d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\geq\frac{1}{2}(\psi_{x,A}(v)-z_{1})
, so that

I_{31}(x,x^{\prime})\leq C\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}{\bm{1}}_{\{\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)<\psi_{x,A}(v)\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d}}\int_{\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)}^{\psi_{x,A}(v)}\frac{[z_{1}-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)]^{\alpha/2}}{[\psi_{x,A}(v)-z_{1}]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}.

Setting
c_{\alpha}=\int_{0}^{1}(\frac{u}{1-u})^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}u
, we have

\int_{a}^{b}(\frac{z_{1}-a}{b-z_{1}})^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}z_{1}=c_{\alpha}(b-a)
for all
0\leq a\leq b
: use
the change of variables
u=\frac{z_{1}-a}{b-a}
. Consequently, see Lemma
79
,

\displaystyle I_{31}(x,x^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}|\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)-\psi_{x,A}(v)|\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d}}\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\frac{|v|^{2}\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d}}\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}.

Next,

\displaystyle I_{32}(x,x^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})]^{\alpha/2}}{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})]^{\alpha/2}}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\}}\mathrm{d}z..

For
z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, we have

d(z,\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\lor d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
, see Lemma
35
. Hence

\displaystyle I_{32}(x,x^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{{\bm{1}}_{\{d(z,\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\in(0,C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}]\}}}{[d(z,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{{\bm{1}}_{\{d(h_{x}(A,z),\mathcal{D})\in(0,C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}]\}}}{[d(h_{x}(A,z),\partial\mathcal{D})]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}z.

We substitute
u=h_{x}(A,z)
and then
a=\Phi(u)
. The Jacobian of
\Phi
being bounded, see Lemma
78
,

\displaystyle I_{32}(x,x^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{{\bm{1}}_{\{d(u,\mathcal{D})\in(0,C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}]\}}}{[d(u,\partial\mathcal{D})]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{{\bm{1}}_{\{d(\Phi^{-1}(a),\mathcal{D})\in(0,C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}]\}}}{[d(\Phi^{-1}(a),\partial\mathcal{D})]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}a.

But, see Lemma
78
,
\kappa^{-1}d(a,{B}_{d}(0,1))\leq d(\Phi^{-1}(a),\mathcal{D})\leq\kappa d(a,{B}_{d}(0,1))
, whence

\displaystyle I_{32}(x,x^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{{\bm{1}}_{\{d(a,{B}_{d}(0,1))\in(0,C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}]\}}}{[d(a,{B}_{d}(0,1))]^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{1}^{1+C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}}\frac{r^{d-1}\mathrm{d}r}{(r-1)^{\alpha/2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\int_{1}^{1+C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}r}{(r-1)^{\alpha/2}},

because
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
is uniformly bounded. Finally, we conclude that

I_{32}(x,x^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{\alpha/2}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}^{1-\alpha/2}=C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}.

We have proved that
I_{3}(x,x^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
,
i.e.
(
37
).

Step 6.
Let
I_{4}(x)
be the left hand side of (
38
) and recall that

\mathcal{D}_{x,A}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}\}
.
Using the function
\psi_{x,A}:B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
introduced in Lemma
79
,

I_{4}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{H}}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\notin\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})^{c}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}+\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}{\bm{1}}_{\{z_{1}<\psi_{x,A}(z_{2},\dots,z_{d})\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}.

Hence, writing
z=(z_{1},v)
and using that
|z|\geq|v|
,

I_{4}(x)\leq C+\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\beta}}\int_{0}^{\psi_{x,A}(v)}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq C+\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\frac{\psi_{x,A}(v)\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\beta}}.

By Lemma
79
, there is
C>0
, which does not depend on
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
such that
|\psi_{x,A}(v)|\leq C|v|^{2}
:

I_{4}(x)\leq C+C\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\beta-2}}.

The conclusion follows, since
d+\beta-2<d-1
.

Step 7.
We call
I_{5}(x,x^{\prime})
the left hand side of (
39
). Using the functions

\psi_{x,A},\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}:B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
introduced in Lemma
79
,

I_{5}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{H}}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\leq I_{51}(x,x^{\prime})+I_{52}(x,x^{\prime}),

where

\displaystyle I_{51}(x,x^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})^{c}}{\bm{1}}_{\{z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\leq C{\rm Vol}_{d}(\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}),

\displaystyle I_{52}(x,x^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\!\!\frac{{\bm{1}}_{\{\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(z_{2},\dots,z_{d})\leq z_{1}<\psi_{x,A}(z_{2},\dots,z_{d})\}}\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\leq C\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\!\!\frac{|\psi_{x,A}(v)-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)|\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\beta}}.

We have written
z=(z_{1},v)
, used that
|z|\geq|v|
and integrated in
z_{1}\in[\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v),\psi_{x,A}(v))
.
Using now Lemmas
35
and
79
, we find

I_{5}(x,x^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}+C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\beta-2}}\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}.

∎

4.4
Conclusion

We can now give the

Proof of Proposition
34
.

We fix

\alpha\in(0,2)
,
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
, as well as

x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
.
We use the shortened notation

h_{x}(y)=h_{x}(A,y)
,
g_{x}(e)=g_{x}(A,e)
,

\mathfrak{u}(x,e)=\mathfrak{u}(x,A,e)
,
\mathfrak{o}(x,e)=\mathfrak{o}(x,A,e)
,
\bar{g}_{x}(y,z)=\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)
and
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}=|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||
.

Step 1.
By Remark
36
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Delta(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,e))-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e),\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e).

We recall that
\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)=\inf\{t>0:h_{x}(e(t))\notin\mathcal{D}\}
,

\mathfrak{u}(x,e)=e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)-)
and
\mathfrak{o}(x,e)=e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(e))
. We distinguish
four possibilities:

\bullet

\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)=\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)
and
\delta(h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\leq\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e))
,
in which case
\mathfrak{u}(x,e)=\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e)
,
\mathfrak{o}(x,e)=\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e)
,
and
h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D}
and

h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D}
;

\bullet

\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)=\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)
and
\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e))\leq\delta(h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))
,
in which case
\mathfrak{u}(x,e)=\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e)
,
\mathfrak{o}(x,e)=\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e)
,
and
h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e))\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e))\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))\notin\mathcal{D}
and

h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))\notin\mathcal{D}
;

\bullet

\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)<\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)
, in which case

h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\!\in\!\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\!\in\!\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\!\notin\!\mathcal{D}
,

h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\!\in\!\mathcal{D}
;

\bullet

\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)<\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)
, in which case

h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e))\!\in\!\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e))\!\in\!\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))\!\notin\!\mathcal{D}
,

h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))\!\in\!\mathcal{D}
.

Thus
\Delta(x,x^{\prime})\leq\Delta_{1}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})+\Delta_{1}(x^{\prime},x,A^{\prime},A)+\Delta_{2}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})+\Delta_{2}(x^{\prime},x,A^{\prime},A)
, where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}(x,x^{\prime}\!,A,A^{\prime})\!=\!

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\!\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\!-\!\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,e))|\!-\!|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{\delta(h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\leq\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(y,z)\!-\!\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(y,z)|\!-\!|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{\delta(h_{x}(y))\leq\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(y))\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z),

with
q_{x}
defined in Proposition
37
, and

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,e))-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e),\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)>\bar{\ell}_{x}(e),h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e).

We write
\Delta_{2}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq\Delta_{21}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})+\Delta_{22}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})
, where

\displaystyle\Delta_{21}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e),\mathfrak{o}(x,e))-h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z),

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{22}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big|h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x^{\prime},e),\mathfrak{o}(x^{\prime},e))\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)>\bar{\ell}_{x}(e),h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e).

By the Markov property (see Lemma
29
)
with the stopping time
\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)
(which is indeed positive
when
\beta=*
, see Remark
32
) and since

\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)<\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)
implies
\bar{\ell}_{x}(e)<\ell(e)
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{22}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathfrak{o}(x,e)}\Big[\Big|h_{x^{\prime}}(Z_{0})-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(Z)-},Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(Z)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(e)>\bar{\ell}_{x}(e),h_{x}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{u}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{o}(x,e))\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big|h_{x^{\prime}}(Z_{0})-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(Z)-},Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(Z)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z).

Step 2.
By Proposition
37
, we see that

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{\delta(h_{x}(y))\leq\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(y))\}}
