\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+C{\bm{1}}_{\{\beta\neq*\}}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(0,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(0,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{1}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\mathrm{d}z.

Using next Proposition
39
-(i)-(iii), we find

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(y))}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+C{\bm{1}}_{\{\beta\neq*\}}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{H}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\notin\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{1}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\mathrm{d}z.

Thus
\Delta_{1}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}

by (
36
) and (
38
).

Step 3.
By Proposition
37
, we get

\displaystyle\Delta_{21}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\!\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+C{\bm{1}}_{\{\beta\neq*\}}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(0,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{1}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\mathrm{d}z.

We next use Proposition
39
-(ii)-(iv) to obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{21}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\!\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(y))}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+C{\bm{1}}_{\{\beta\neq*\}}\int_{\mathbb{H}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{1}{|z|^{d+\beta}}\mathrm{d}z.

We conclude from (
36
) and (
39
) that

\Delta_{21}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
.

Step 4.
To treat
\Delta_{22}
, let us first prove that there is a constant
C

(not depending on
x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
) such that
for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}
,

f(z):=\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big|h_{x^{\prime}}(Z_{0})-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(Z)-},Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x^{\prime}}(Z)})\Big|\Big]\leq C[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(z))]^{\alpha/2}.

(41)

Since
h_{x^{\prime}}
is an affine isometry,
h_{x^{\prime}}(Z)
has the same law as
Z
(with the suitable
initial condition), so that we can write, recalling (
29
),

f(z)=\mathbb{E}_{h_{x^{\prime}}(z)}\Big[\Big|Z_{0}-\Lambda(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)})\Big|\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{h_{x^{\prime}}(z)}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|\land\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})\Big],

where
\tilde{\ell}(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}
. Consider now
u\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that

\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(z))=|h_{x^{\prime}}(z)-u|
, as well as the half-space
\mathbb{H}_{u}
tangent to
\partial\mathcal{D}
at
u

and containing
\mathcal{D}
(recall that
\mathcal{D}
is convex).
It holds that
\hat{\ell}_{u}(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin{\mathbb{H}}_{u}\}\geq\tilde{\ell}(Z)
, whence

f(z)\leq\mathbb{E}_{h_{x^{\prime}}(z)}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\hat{\ell}_{u}(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|\land\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})\Big]=\mathbb{E}_{\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(z))\mathbf{e}_{1}}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(Z)]}|Z_{t}-Z_{0}|\land\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{D})\Big]

by isometry-invariance of the stable process (recall that
\ell(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin{\mathbb{H}}\}
)
and since
h_{x^{\prime}}(z)-u
is orthogonal to
\partial\mathbb{H}_{u}
.
We then deduce (
41
) from Lemma
30
. With the very same arguments,
we have the following (to be used in other proofs): for any
y\in\mathcal{D}
,
with
u\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that
\delta(y)=|y-u|
and
\mathbb{H}_{u}
as above,

\mathbb{P}_{y}(\tilde{\ell}(Z)>1)\leq\mathbb{P}_{y}(\hat{\ell}_{u}(Z)>1)=\mathbb{P}_{\delta(y)\mathbf{e}_{1}}(\ell(Z)>1)\sim c(\delta(y))^{\alpha/2}\text{ as $\delta(y)\to 0$.}

(42)

We thus have

\displaystyle\Delta_{22}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(z))]^{\alpha/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}q_{x}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(z))]^{\alpha/2}\frac{[\delta(h_{x}(y))]^{\alpha/2}}{|z-y|^{d+\alpha}|y|^{d}}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(y)\in\mathcal{D},h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+C{\bm{1}}_{\{\beta\neq*\}}\int_{\mathbb{H}}[\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(z))]^{\alpha/2}{\bm{1}}_{\{h_{x}(z)\notin\mathcal{D},h_{x^{\prime}}(z)\in\mathcal{D}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\beta}}

by Proposition
37
. By (
37
) and (
39
), we end with
\Delta_{22}(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
.

Conclusion.
Gathering Steps 1 to 4, we find
\Delta(x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
as desired.
∎

5
The reflected process starting from the boundary

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
7
. We start with three lemmas.

Lemma 42
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
,
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and suppose Assumption
1
.
Consider a filtration
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
, a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure

\Pi_{\beta}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity measure
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
,
a càdlàg
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-adapted
\partial\mathcal{D}
-valued process
(b_{u})_{u\geq 0}
, a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-predictable
process
(a_{u})_{u\geq 0}
such that a.s., for all
u\geq 0
,
a_{u}\in\mathcal{I}_{b_{u-}}
and define
(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}

by (
15
). Then a.s.,
u\mapsto\tau_{u}
is strictly increasing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\lim_{u\to\infty}\tau_{u}=\infty
.

Proof.

By Remark
3
, we have

\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)\geq\ell_{r}(e)
for all
e\in\mathcal{E}
, all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
.
If
\beta=*
, we have
\ell_{r}(e)>0
for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
by Lemma
32
, so that

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\ell_{r}>0)=\infty
and the result follows.
If next
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
, we have
\ell_{r}(e)>0
for all
e\in\mathcal{E}_{r}:=\{e\in\mathcal{E}:e(0)\in B_{d}(r\mathbf{e}_{1},e)\}
. Since
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathcal{E}_{r})=\int_{\mathbb{H}\cap B_{d}(r\mathbf{e}_{1},e)}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathrm{d}x=\infty
,
recall (
8
),
the result follows.
∎

The second one deals with Poisson measures.

Lemma 43
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
. Consider a filtration
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
,
a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure

\Pi_{\beta}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity measure
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.
For any
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-predictable process
\Theta_{u}
such that a.s., for all
u\geq 0
,

\Theta_{u}
is a linear isometry of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
sending
\mathbf{e}_{1}
to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
, the random measure

\Pi^{\prime}_{\beta}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,\Theta_{u}e_{u})}
is again a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity measure
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.

Proof.

By rotational invariance of the stable process, the excursion measure
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
is invariant by
any linear isometry of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
sending
\mathbf{e}_{1}
to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
. Consequently,
the compensator (see Jacod and Shiryaev
[
47
, Theorem 1.8 page 66]
)
of the integer-valued random
measure (see
[
47
, Definition 1.13 page 68]
)
\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}
is again
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.
The conclusion follows from
[
47
, Theorem 4.8 page 104]
.
∎

The third one states some continuity results.

Lemma 44
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and suppose Assumption
1
.

(i) For any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, any
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, any
t>0
, we have
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\{e\in\mathcal{E}:\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=t\})=0
.

(ii) Let
x_{n}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A_{n}\in\mathcal{I}_{x_{n}}
such that
\lim_{n}x_{n}=x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and

\lim_{n}A_{n}=A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
. For
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
,
there is
n_{e}\geq 1
such that for all
n\geq n_{e}
,
\bar{\ell}_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e)=\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)
.

Proof.

For (i), we fix
t>0
and observe that for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all

z\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(y)\in\mathcal{D}\}
,

\mathbb{P}_{z}(\ell_{x}(A,Z)=t)=0
, where
Z
is an ISP
α,z
under
\mathbb{P}_{z}
:

\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,Z)
has a density under
\mathbb{P}_{z}
, as shown (with more generality and with some informative
formula) by Bogdan, Jastrzȩbski, Kassmann, Kijaczko and Popławski
[
18
, Theorem 1.3]
.

Since
t>0
,
\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=t
implies
that
e(0)\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
(because
e(0)\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c}
implies that
\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=0
).
Recalling (
8
), we conclude that if
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
,

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,\cdot)=t)=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{x,A}}|z|^{-d-\beta}\mathbb{P}_{z}(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,Z)=t)\mathrm{d}z=0.

If now
\beta=*
, we use the Markov property of
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
at time
t/2
,
see Lemma
29
, to write

\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,\cdot)=t)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>t/2\}}\mathbb{P}_{e(t/2)}(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,Z)=t/2)\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)=0.

We next check (ii).
We fix
x_{n}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A_{n}\in\mathcal{I}_{x_{n}}
such that
x_{n}\to x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
A_{n}\to A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
.
We set
\mathcal{D}_{x_{n},A_{n}}=\{y\in\mathbb{H}:h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},y)\in\mathcal{D}\}
and
\mathcal{D}_{x,A}=\{y\in\mathbb{H}:h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}\}
.
We recall that
\bar{\ell}_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e)=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\notin\mathcal{D}_{x_{n},A_{n}}\}
and

\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\notin\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\}
.

(a) If
z\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, then
\mathbb{P}_{z}(\inf_{t\in[0,\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,Z))}d(Z_{t},\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c})>0,Z_{\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,Z)}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}^{c})=1
.

By invariance of the stable process, it suffices that, setting

\tilde{\ell}(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}
,

\text{for all $z\in\mathcal{D}$,}\qquad p(z):=\mathbb{P}_{z}\Big(\inf_{t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(Z))}\delta(Z_{t})>0,Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}\Big)=1,

(43)

where
\delta(y)=d(y,\partial\mathcal{D})
.
Fix
z\in\mathcal{D}
. By Lemma
38
, the laws of
Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-}
and
Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}
have densities under

\mathbb{P}_{z}
, so that
\mathbb{P}_{z}
-a.s.,
Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}
and

Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-}\in\mathcal{D}
, whence there is
\varepsilon>0
such that

\inf_{t\in[\tilde{\ell}(Z)-\varepsilon,\tilde{\ell}(Z))}\delta(Z_{t})>0
.
Hence we only have to check that for all
\varepsilon>0
,

q_{\varepsilon}(z):=\mathbb{P}_{z}\Big(\tilde{\ell}(Z)>\varepsilon,\inf_{t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(Z)-\varepsilon]}\delta(Z_{t})=0\Big)=0.

For
k\geq 1
, set
\rho_{k}(Z)=\inf\{t\geq 0:\delta(Z_{t})\leq 1/k\}
: we need
that
\lim_{k}q_{k,\varepsilon}(z)=0
, where

q_{k,\varepsilon}(z)=\mathbb{P}_{z}\Big(\tilde{\ell}(Z)>\varepsilon,\inf_{t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(Z)-\varepsilon]}\delta(Z_{t})\leq 1/k\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}_{z}(\rho_{k}(Z)\leq\tilde{\ell}(Z)-\varepsilon).

On the event
\{\rho_{k}(Z)\leq\tilde{\ell}(Z)-\varepsilon\}
, we have
Z_{\rho_{k}(Z)}\in\mathcal{D}
, so that, applying the
strong Markov property at time
\rho_{k}(Z)
, we get

q_{k,\varepsilon}(z)\leq\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[{\bm{1}}_{\{Z_{\rho_{k}(Z)}\in\mathcal{D}\}}\mathbb{P}_{Z_{\rho_{k}(Z)}}(\tilde{\ell}(Z)\geq\varepsilon)\Big].

By (
42
) and a scaling argument, there is
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{y}(\tilde{\ell}(Z)\geq\varepsilon)\leq c_{\varepsilon}[\delta(y)]^{\alpha/2}
for
y\in\mathcal{D}
. Since

\delta(Z_{\rho_{k}(Z)})\leq 1/k
on
\{Z_{\rho_{k}(Z)}\in\mathcal{D}\}
, we conclude that

q_{k,\varepsilon}(z)\leq c_{\varepsilon}k^{-\alpha/2}\to 0
as desired.

(b) We conclude when
\beta=*
. Let us first mention that as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|d(y,\mathcal{D}_{x_{n},A_{n}}^{c})-d(y,\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c})|=\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|d(h_{x}(A,y),\mathcal{D}^{c})-d(h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},y),\mathcal{D}^{c})|\to 0,

(44)

since
|d(h_{x}(A,y),\mathcal{D}^{c})-d(h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},y),\mathcal{D}^{c})|\leq|h_{x}(A,y)-h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},y)|\leq|x-x_{n}|+||A-A_{n}|||y|

and since
|d(h_{x}(A,y),\mathcal{D}^{c})-d(h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},y),\mathcal{D}^{c})|=0
if
|y|\geq\text{diam}(\mathcal{D})
.

By Remark
3
,

\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)\geq\ell_{r}(e)
,
\bar{\ell}_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e)\geq\ell_{r}(e)
and by Lemma
32
,

\ell_{r}>0

\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
By (a) and the Markov property applied at time
\ell_{r}(e)>0
, see Lemma
29
,
we see that for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
,

\bullet
either
e(\ell_{r}(e))\notin{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}
,

\bullet
or

e(\ell_{r}(e))\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
and
\inf_{t\in[\ell_{r}(e),\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))}d(e(t),\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c})>0

and
e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}^{c}
.

We used that for
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
,
e(\ell_{r}(e))\notin\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
because the law of
e(\ell_{r}(e))
under
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
has a density (as in
Proposition
37
).

If first
e(\ell_{r}(e))\notin{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}
, then
\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=\ell_{r}(e)

and
\bar{\ell}_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e)=\ell_{r}(e)
for all
n
large enough, since

h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e(\ell_{r}(e)))\to h_{x}(A,e(\ell_{r}(e)))\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
, which is open,
so that
e(\ell_{r}(e))\notin{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x_{n},A_{n}}
for
n
large enough.

If next
e(\ell_{r}(e))\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
and
\inf_{t\in[\ell_{r}(e),\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))}d(e(t),\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c})>0

and

e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}^{c}
, then for all
n
large enough,

e(\ell_{r}(e))\in\mathcal{D}_{x_{n},A_{n}}
(because
h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e(\ell_{r}(e)))\to h_{x}(A,e(\ell_{r}(e)))\in\mathcal{D}
which
is open),

\inf_{t\in[\ell_{r}(e),\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))}d(e(t),\mathcal{D}_{x_{n},A_{n}}^{c})>0

by (
44
)
and
e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x_{n},A_{n}}^{c}
(because
h_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)))\to h_{x}(A,e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}
which is open).
Thus
\bar{\ell}_{x_{n}}(A_{n},e)=\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)
for all
n
large enough.

(c) The case where
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
is easier. By (a) and (
8
),
we see that for
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
,

\bullet

either
e(0)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}
,

\bullet
or
e(0)\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
and

\inf_{t\in[0,\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))}d(e(t),\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c})>0
and

e(\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x,A}^{c}
.

We conclude as in (b).
∎

We now prove some well-posedness result for the boundary process.

Proposition 45
.

Fix

\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and suppose Assumption
1
. We consider a filtration

(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
\Pi_{\beta}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}

on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.

(a) If
d=2
, consider the Lipschitz family
(A_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
introduced in Lemma
77
-(i).
For any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
there is strong existence and uniqueness
of a càdlàg
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-adapted
\partial\mathcal{D}
-valued process
(b_{u}^{x})_{u\geq 0}
solving

b_{u}^{x}=x+\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[g_{b^{x}_{v-}}(A_{b^{x}_{v-}},e)-b^{x}_{v-}]\Pi_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e).

(45)

(b) If
d\geq 3
, we denote by

\varsigma
the uniform measure on
\partial\mathcal{D}
, that is, the (normalized) Hausdorff measure
of dimension
d-1
(in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
) restricted to
\partial\mathcal{D}
. We
consider for each
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
the family
(A^{z}_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
introduced in
Lemma
77
-(ii), such that
y\mapsto A^{z}_{y}
is locally Lipschitz on
\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
.
For any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, for
\varsigma
-a.e.
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
there is strong existence and uniqueness
of a càdlàg
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-adapted
\partial\mathcal{D}
-valued process
(b_{u}^{x,z})_{u\geq 0}
solving

b_{u}^{x,z}=x+\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[g_{b^{x,z}_{v-}}(A^{z}_{b^{x,z}_{v-}},e)-b^{x,z}_{v-}]\Pi_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e),

(46)

with moreover
\rho_{x,z}:=\lim_{k\to\infty}\rho_{x,z}^{k}=\infty
a.s., where

\rho_{x,z}^{k}:=\inf\{u\geq 0:|b_{u}^{x,z}-z|\leq 1/k\}
.

Point (b) might actually hold true for all
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{x\}
, but this seems difficult to prove.

Proof.

Point (a) classically follows from the Lipschitz estimate

\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big||g_{y}(A_{y},e)-g_{y^{\prime}}(A_{y^{\prime}},e)|-|y-y^{\prime}|\Big|\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\leq C|y-y^{\prime}|,

which is a consequence of Proposition
34
and Lemma
77
-(i). The resulting
process is indeed
\partial\mathcal{D}
-valued since
g_{y}(A_{y},e)\in\partial\mathcal{D}
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
e\in\mathcal{E}
.
We now turn to (b) and fix
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.

Step 1.

We fix
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. By Proposition
34
and Lemma
77
-(ii),
there exists, for any
k\geq 1
, a constant
C_{k}
such that
for any
y,y^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus B_{d}(z,1/k)
,

\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big||g_{y}(A_{y}^{z},e)-g_{y^{\prime}}(A_{y^{\prime}}^{z},e)|-|y-y^{\prime}|\Big|\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\leq C_{k}|y-y^{\prime}|.

(47)

Moreover,
g_{y}(A_{y}^{z},e)\in\partial\mathcal{D}
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
e\in\mathcal{E}
.
We classically deduce the strong existence and uniqueness of a càdlàg
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-adapted

\partial\mathcal{D}
-valued process

b^{x,z}
solving (
46
) on
[0,\rho_{x,z}^{k}]
. Letting
k\to\infty
, we conclude the strong
existence and uniqueness of a càdlàg
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-adapted

\partial\mathcal{D}
-valued process

b^{x,z}
solving (
46
) on
I_{x,z}=\cup_{k\geq 1}[0,\rho_{x,z}^{k}]
.

Step 2.
For
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, we introduce
\mathcal{R}_{x,z}:=\overline{\{b^{x,z}_{u},u\in I_{x,z}\}}
.
Then
\{\rho_{x,z}<\infty\}\subset\{z\in\mathcal{R}_{x,z}\}
.

Indeed, recall that
\rho_{x,z}=\lim_{k}\rho_{x,z}^{k}
. On the event
\{\rho_{x,z}<\infty\}
, we have

\bullet
either
I_{x,z}=[0,\rho_{x,z})
if the sequence
k\mapsto\rho_{x,z}^{k}

is not eventually constant,
in which case
b^{x,z}_{\rho_{x,z}-}=z
(because
|b^{x,z}_{\rho^{k}_{x,z}}-z|\leq 1/k
),

\bullet
or
I_{x,z}=[0,\rho_{x,z}]
if the sequence
k\mapsto\rho_{x,z}^{k}
is
eventually constant,
i.e.
if
b^{x,z}_{\rho_{x,z}^{k}}=z
for all
k
large enough,
in which case
b^{x,z}_{\rho_{x,z}}=z
.

Step 3.
For all
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, for
\varsigma
-a.e.
z^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
\mathbb{P}(z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{x,z})=0
.

Using (
9
) and that
|g_{y}(A,e)-y|\leq M(e)\land\text{diam }\mathcal{D}
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
,

e\in\mathcal{E}
,
we see that

\text{for all $T>0$,}\qquad\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}\cap I_{x,z}\cap[0,T]}|\Delta b^{x,z}_{u}|\Big]\leq T\int_{\mathcal{E}}(M(e)\land\text{diam }\mathcal{D})<\infty.

This implies, see Lépingle
[
54
, Theorem 1]
, that
(b^{x,z}_{u})_{u\in I_{x,z}}
a.s.
has finite variation
on any finite time interval. By McKean
[
57
, Page 570]
, this implies that
the Hausdorff dimension of
\mathcal{R}_{x,z}

is a.s. smaller than
1
(McKean gives the result for
\{b^{x,z}_{u},u\in I_{x,z}\}
,
but taking the closure
only adds a countable number of points, namely
\{b^{x,z}_{u-},u\in\mathrm{J}\cap I_{x,z}\}
). Since

\varsigma
is the Hausdorff measure
of dimension
d-1\geq 2
restricted to
\partial\mathcal{D}

we conclude that a.s.,
\varsigma(\mathcal{R}_{x,z})=0
, whence

\int_{\partial\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{P}(z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{x,z})\varsigma(\mathrm{d}z^{\prime})=\mathbb{E}[\varsigma(\mathcal{R}_{x,z})]=0
.

Step 4.
For
z,z^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
set
\rho^{k}_{x,z,z^{\prime}}=\inf\{t\geq 0:|b^{x,z}_{t}-z|\leq 1/k
or
|b^{x,z}_{t}-z^{\prime}|\leq 1/k\}
and

I_{x,z,z^{\prime}}=\cup_{k\geq 1}[0,\rho^{k}_{x,z,z^{\prime}}]
.
The processes

(b^{x,z}_{u})_{u\in I_{x,z,z^{\prime}}}
and
(b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{u})_{u\in I_{x,z^{\prime},z}}

have the same law (when extended to the time-interval
\mathbb{R}_{+}
by sending them to a
cemetery point after their lifetime).

Indeed, for all
u\in[0,\rho^{k}_{x,z^{\prime},z}]
, we have

\displaystyle b_{u}^{x,z^{\prime}}\!=\!x+\!\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[g_{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{v-}}(A^{z^{\prime}}_{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{v-}},e)-b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{v-}]\Pi_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)\!=\!x+\!\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[g_{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{v-}}(A^{z}_{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{v-}},e)-b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{v-}]\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e),

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u}^{\prime})},\quad\text{with}\quad e_{u}^{\prime}=\Theta_{u}e_{u}\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta_{u}=(A^{z}_{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{u-}})^{-1}A^{z^{\prime}}_{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{u-}}.

But
(\Theta_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-predictable and
a.s., for any
u\geq 0
,
\Theta_{u}
is a linear isometry sending

\mathbf{e}_{1}
to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
. By Lemma
43
,
\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}
is a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
with intensity
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.
Consequently,
b^{x,z^{\prime}}
solves (
46
) during
[0,\rho^{k}_{x,z^{\prime},z}]
,
i.e.
until it enters

\bar{B}_{d}(z,1/k)\cup\bar{B}_{d}(z^{\prime},1/k)
, with the Poisson measure

\Pi^{\prime}_{\beta}
. Since
b^{x,z}
also solves (
46
) during
[0,\rho^{k}_{x,z,z^{\prime}}]
,
i.e.
until it enters

\bar{B}_{d}(z,1/k)\cup\bar{B}_{d}(z^{\prime},1/k)
, with the Poisson measure

\Pi_{\beta}
and since this S.D.E. is well-posed, see Step 1, we conclude that

(b^{x,z}_{u})_{u\in[0,\rho^{k}_{x,z,z^{\prime}}]}
and
(b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{u})_{u\in[0,\rho^{k}_{x,z^{\prime},z}]}

share the same law. The conclusion follows.

Step 5.
For
\varsigma\otimes\varsigma
-a.e.
(z,z^{\prime})\in\partial\mathcal{D}^{2}
,

\mathcal{R}_{x,z}
and
\mathcal{R}_{x,z^{\prime}}
have the same law.

By Step 4, we know that for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\{b^{x,z}_{u}:u\in I_{x,z,z^{\prime}}\}}

and
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\{b^{x,z^{\prime}}_{u}:u\in I_{x,z^{\prime},z}\}}
have the same law.
It thus suffices to verify that for
\varsigma\otimes\varsigma
-a.e.
(z,z^{\prime})\in\partial\mathcal{D}^{2}
,
we both have
I_{x,z,z^{\prime}}=I_{x,z}
a.s. (
i.e.

z^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{R}_{x,z}
a.s.) and
I_{x,z^{\prime},z}=I_{x,z^{\prime}}
a.s.
(
i.e.

z\notin\mathcal{R}_{x,z^{\prime}}
a.s.). This follows from Step 3.

Step 6.
By Step 2, it suffices to check that for
\varsigma
-a.e.
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,

\mathbb{P}(z\in\mathcal{R}_{x,z})=0
.
But for
\varsigma\otimes\varsigma
-a.e.
(z,z^{\prime})\in(\partial\mathcal{D})^{2}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(z\in\mathcal{R}_{x,z})=\mathbb{P}(z\in\mathcal{R}_{x,z^{\prime}})=0
by Steps 5 and 3.
∎

We are now ready to give the

Proof of Theorem
7
.

We only treat the case
d\geq 3
. The case
d=2
is easier, as we can use Proposition
45
-(a)
instead of Proposition
45
-(b).
We fix

\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and suppose Assumption
1
. We also consider a filtration

(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
\Pi_{\beta}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}

on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity measure
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.

Step 1.
We prove (a). The existence, for any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, of an

(\alpha,\beta)
-stable process reflected in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
issued from
x
, see Definition
4
,
immediately follows from Proposition
45
: fix one value of
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that

(b_{u}:=b^{x,z}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
exists.
Then
(b_{u})_{u\geq 0}
solves (
14
) with the choice
a_{u}=A^{z}_{b^{x,z}_{u-}}

and it suffices to define
(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
by (
15
), to introduce

(L_{t}=\inf\{u\geq 0:\tau_{u}>t\})_{t\geq 0}
and to define
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
by (
16
).

The process
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
-valued. Indeed, recall from Remark
5
that we always have

t\in[\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}}]
.
If first
L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}
and
\tau_{L_{t}}>t
, then
R_{t}=h_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}}(t-\tau_{L_{t}-}))
, which belongs to
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}

because
t-\tau_{L_{t}-}\in[0,\Delta\tau_{L_{t}})=[0,\bar{\ell}_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e))
and by definition of
\bar{\ell}
;
if next
L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}
and
\tau_{L_{t}}=t
, then
R_{t}=b_{L_{t}}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, see Remark
6
-(f);
if finally
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
, then
R_{t}=b_{L_{t}}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, see Remark
6
-(a).

It remains to show that
R=(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a.s. càdlàg on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
We will use that for all
t\geq 0
,

|R_{t}-b_{L_{t-}}|=\left.\begin{cases}|h_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}}(t-\tau_{L_{t}-}))-b_{L_{t}-}|&\text{if }\tau_{L_{t}}>t,\\
|g_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}})-b_{L_{t}-}|&\text{if }L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}}=t,\\
|b_{L_{t}}-b_{L_{t}-}|&\text{if }L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}.\end{cases}\right\}\leq{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\}}M(e_{L_{t}}),

(48)

where
M(e)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e)]}|e(t)|
,
because
b_{L_{t}}=b_{L_{t}-}
if
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
, see Remark
6
-(a).

Let us now check that

\text{a.s., for all }u\geq 0,\lim_{v\to u,v\neq u}{\bm{1}}_{\{v\in\mathrm{J}\}}M(e_{v})=0.

(49)

The process
\hat{\tau}_{u}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell(e)\Pi_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)
being càdlàg,

\text{a.s., for all }u\geq 0,\lim_{v\to u,v\neq u}\Delta\hat{\tau}_{v}=0,\quad\text{i.e. }\lim_{v\to u,v\neq u}{\bm{1}}_{\{v\in\mathrm{J}\}}\ell(e_{v})=0.

(50)

For
T>0
and
\delta>0
, set
G_{T,\delta}=\sup_{v\in\mathrm{J}\cap[0,T]}M(e_{v}){\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e_{v})\leq\delta\}}
.
We have

\mathbb{E}[G_{T,\delta}\land 1]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{v\in\mathrm{J}\cap[0,T]}(M(e_{v})\land 1){\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e_{v})\leq\delta\}}\Big]=T\int_{\mathcal{E}}(M(e)\land 1){\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)\leq\delta\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\to 0\text{ as }\delta\to 0

by (
9
) and since
\ell>0

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
-a.e.
Since
G_{T,\delta}
is monotone in
\delta
, we conclude that
\lim_{\delta\to 0}G_{T,\delta}=0
a.s. for all
T>0

which, together with (
50
), implies (
49
).

We claim that

\displaystyle L_{\tau_{u}+h}>u\quad\text{for all $u\in\mathrm{J}$, all $h>0$},

\displaystyle L_{\tau_{u-}-h}<u\quad\text{for all $u\in\mathrm{J}$, all $h\in(0,\tau_{u-})$},

\displaystyle L_{t+h}\neq L_{t}\quad\text{for all $t\geq 0$ such that $L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}$ and all $h\in[-t,\infty)\setminus\{0\}$.}

The first claim follows from the fact that
L_{\tau_{u}+h}=\inf\{v>0:\tau_{v}>\tau_{u}+h\}
and that

(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is càd. Indeed, if
L_{\tau_{u}+h}\leq u
, then for any
v>u
,
\tau_{v}>\tau_{u}+h
,
which would contradict the right-continuity of
\tau
.
The second one uses that
L_{\tau_{u-}-h}=\inf\{v>0:\tau_{v}>\tau_{u-}-h\}
and that
(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is làg.
The last claim uses that for
t\geq 0
such that
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
, if e.g.
h>0
,

L_{t+h}=\inf\{v>0:\tau_{v}>t+h\}>\inf\{v>0:\tau_{v}>t\}
, because
\tau
is continuous at
L_{t}
and

\tau_{L_{t}}=t
.

We now have all the tools to check that
R
is càdlàg.

\bullet
Since
e_{u}
is càdlàg for each
u\in\mathrm{J}
and
since
R_{t}=h_{b_{u-}}(a_{u},e_{u}(t-\tau_{u-}))
for each
t\in[\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})
,
R
is clearly càdlàg on

\cup_{u\in\mathrm{J}}(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})
, càd at each
t\in\{\tau_{u-}:u\in\mathrm{J},\Delta\tau_{u}>0\}
and làg at each

t\in\{\tau_{u}:u\in\mathrm{J},\Delta\tau_{u}>0\}
.

\bullet
Let us check that
R
is càd at
t=\tau_{u}
for each
u\in\mathrm{J}
.
For
h>0
, by Remark
6
-(f) with
t=\tau_{u}
,

|R_{\tau_{u}+h}-R_{\tau_{u}}|=|R_{\tau_{u}+h}-b_{u}|\leq|R_{\tau_{u}+h}-b_{L_{\tau_{u}+h}-}|+|b_{L_{\tau_{u}+h}-}-b_{u}|=:\Delta_{u,h}^{1}+\Delta_{u,h}^{2}.

As seen above,
L_{\tau_{u}+h}>u
for all
h>0
.
Hence
\lim_{h\searrow 0}\Delta_{u,h}^{2}=0
because
b
is càd,
L
is continuous and
L_{\tau_{u}}=u
.
Next,
\Delta_{u,h}^{1}\leq{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{\tau_{u}+h}\in\mathrm{J}\}}M(e_{L_{\tau_{u}+h}})
by (
48
).
Thus
\lim_{h\searrow 0}\Delta_{u,h}^{1}=0
by (
49
), because
L_{\tau_{u}+h}\neq u
for all
h>0

and because
L
is continuous and
L_{\tau_{u}}=u
.

\bullet
We now verify that
R
is làg at
t=\tau_{u-}
for each
u\in\mathrm{J}
, and more
precisely that
\lim_{h\searrow 0}R_{\tau_{u-}-h}=b_{u-}
.
We write, for
h\in(0,\tau_{u-})
,

|R_{\tau_{u-}-h}-b_{u-}|\leq|R_{\tau_{u-}-h}-b_{(L_{\tau_{u-}-h})-}|+|b_{(L_{\tau_{u-}-h})-}-b_{u-}|=:\Delta_{u,h}^{3}+\Delta_{u,h}^{4}.

First,
\lim_{h\searrow 0}\Delta_{u,h}^{4}=0
because
b
is làg,
L
is continuous,
L_{\tau_{u-}-h}<u

and
L_{\tau_{u-}}=u
. Next,
\Delta_{u,h}^{3}\leq{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{\tau_{u-}-h}\in\mathrm{J}\}}M(e_{L_{\tau_{u-}-h}})
by (
48
).
Since
L_{\tau_{u-}-h}<u
for all
h\in[0,\tau_{u-})
and since
L
is continuous,

\lim_{h\searrow 0}\Delta_{u,h}^{3}=0
by (
49
).

\bullet
Let us show that
R
is continuous at each
t
such that
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
.
By Remark
6
-(a), for
h\in(-t,\infty)
,

|R_{t+h}-R_{t}|=|R_{t+h}-b_{L_{t}}|\leq|R_{t+h}-b_{L_{t+h}-}|+|b_{L_{t+h}-}-b_{L_{t}}|=:\Delta_{t,h}^{5}+\Delta_{t,h}^{6}.

Since
L
is continuous and
b
is continuous at
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
, we have

\lim_{h\to 0}\Delta_{t,h}^{6}=0
. Next,

\Delta_{t,h}^{5}\leq{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t+h}\in\mathrm{J}\}}M(e_{L_{t+h}})
by (
48
).
But
\lim_{h\to 0}{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t+h}\in\mathrm{J}\}}M(e_{L_{t+h}})=0
by (
49
), since
L_{t+h}\neq L_{t}
for all
h\neq 0

as recalled above.

Step 2.
Here we show (b),
i.e.
that for any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, any

(\alpha,\beta)
-stable process
(R^{\prime}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
reflected in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
issued from
x
, associated to some

(\mathcal{G}_{u}^{\prime})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}
and some processes
(b^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
,
(a^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
,

(\tau^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and
(L_{t}^{\prime})_{t\geq 0}
,
has the same law as the process of Step 1 (built with the same value of
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}

as in Step 1, with
some
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure

\Pi_{\beta}
and some processes
(b_{u})_{u\geq 0}
,
(a_{u}:=A^{z}_{b_{u-}})_{u\geq 0}
,

(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and
(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
).

Write
\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}^{\prime}}\delta_{(u,e_{u}^{\prime})}
and, setting
e_{u}^{\prime\prime}=\Theta_{u}e_{u}^{\prime}

with
\Theta_{u}=(A^{z}_{b^{\prime}_{u-}})^{-1}a_{u}^{\prime}

for all
u\in\mathrm{J}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle b_{u}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle x+\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big(g_{b^{\prime}_{v-}}(a^{\prime}_{v},e)-b_{v-}^{\prime}\Big)\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big(g_{b^{\prime}_{{v-}}}(A^{z}_{b^{\prime}_{v-}},e)-b^{\prime}_{v-}\Big)\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime\prime}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e),\;\;\hbox{where}\;\;\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime\prime}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}^{\prime}}\delta_{(u,e_{u}^{\prime\prime})}.

This follows from the fact that
g_{b^{\prime}_{v-}}(a^{\prime}_{v},e)=g_{b^{\prime}_{{v-}}}(A^{z}_{b^{\prime}_{v-}},\Theta_{u}e)
.
But
(\Theta_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
- predictable and a.s.,
for any
u\geq 0
,
\Theta_{u}
is a linear isometry sending

\mathbf{e}_{1}
to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
. By Lemma
43
,
\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime\prime}
is a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
with intensity
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
.
By strong uniqueness, see Proposition
45
-(b),

((b_{u}^{\prime})_{u\geq 0},\Pi^{\prime\prime}_{\beta})
has the same law as
((b_{u})_{u\geq 0},\Pi_{\beta})
.
Next, we have

\tau_{u}^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\bar{\ell}_{b^{\prime}_{v-}}(a^{\prime}_{v},e)\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\bar{\ell}_{b^{\prime}_{v-}}(A^{z}_{b^{\prime}_{v-}},e)\Pi_{\beta}^{\prime\prime}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)

and
L^{\prime}_{t}=\inf\{u\geq 0:\tau^{\prime}_{u}>t\}
,
so that
((b_{u}^{\prime})_{u\geq 0},(\tau^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 0},(L^{\prime}_{t})_{t\geq 0},\Pi^{\prime\prime}_{\beta})
has the same law as

((b_{u})_{u\geq 0},(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0},(L_{t})_{t\geq 0},\Pi_{\beta})
. Finally,

R_{t}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}h_{b^{\prime}_{L_{t}^{\prime}-}}(a^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}},e^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}}(t-\tau^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}-}))=h_{b^{\prime}_{L_{t}^{\prime}-}}(A^{z}_{b^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}-}},e^{\prime\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}}(t-\tau^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}-}))&\text{if }\tau^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}}>t,\\
g_{b^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}-}}(a^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}},e^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}})=g_{b^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}-}}(A^{z}_{b^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}-}},e^{\prime\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}})&\text{if }L^{\prime}_{t}\in\mathrm{J}^{\prime}\text{ and }\tau^{\prime}_{L^{\prime}_{t}}=t,\\
b^{\prime}_{L_{t}^{\prime}}&\text{if }L^{\prime}_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}^{\prime},\end{cases}

so that
((b_{u}^{\prime})_{u\geq 0},(\tau^{\prime}_{u})_{u\geq 0},(L^{\prime}_{t})_{t\geq 0},(R^{\prime}_{t})_{t\geq 0},\Pi^{\prime\prime}_{\beta})
and

((b_{u})_{u\geq 0},(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0},(L_{t})_{t\geq 0},(R_{t})_{t\geq 0},\Pi_{\beta})
have the same law. In particular,

(R^{\prime}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
as desired.

Step 3.
Here we prove point (c). We
call
\mathbb{Q}_{x}
the law of the
(\alpha,\beta)
-stable process
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
reflected in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
issued from
x
,
consider some sequences
t_{n}\geq 0
and
x_{n}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that
\lim_{n}t_{n}=t\geq 0
and
\lim_{n}x_{n}=x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
some bounded continuous function
\varphi:{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}
, and verify that

\lim_{n}\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t_{n}})]=\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]
, where we recall that

(X_{t}^{*})_{t\geq 0}
is the canonical process.

Step 3.1.
We fix a Poisson measure
\Pi_{\beta}
and

z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that Proposition
45
-(b) applies both to
x
and to every
x_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
.
We consider the solutions
(b_{u}:=b^{x,z}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and
(b^{n}_{u}:=b^{x_{n},z}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
to (
46
),
and we then build
((\tau_{u})_{u,\geq 0},(L_{t})_{t\geq 0},(R_{t})_{t\geq 0})

(resp.
((\tau_{u}^{n})_{u,\geq 0},(L_{t}^{n})_{t\geq 0},(R_{t}^{n})_{t\geq 0})
) as in Definition
4
, using

(a_{u}:=A^{z}_{b_{u-}})_{u\geq 0}
(resp.
(a_{u}^{n}:=A^{z}_{b^{n}_{u-}})_{u\geq 0}
) and the Poisson measure
\Pi_{\beta}
.
We will check that
\lim_{n}\mathbb{E}[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-R_{t}|]=0
and this will end the proof, since

(R^{n}_{s})_{s\geq 0}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}
and
(R_{s})_{s\geq 0}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{x}
.

Step 3.2.
We verify that for any
T>0
,
\sup_{[0,T)}(|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|+|a^{n}_{u}-a_{u}|)

tends to
0
in probability.

For
k\geq 1
, we introduce
\rho^{k}=\inf\{u\geq 0:|b_{u}-z|\leq 1/k\}
and

\rho^{k}_{n}=\inf\{u\geq 0:|b_{u}^{n}-z|\leq 1/k\}
. Using (
47
) and the Gronwall Lemma, one easily gets

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,T\land\rho^{k}\land\rho^{k}_{n})}|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|\Big]\leq|x_{n}-x|e^{C_{k}T}.

(51)

Since
y\mapsto A^{z}_{y}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus B_{d}(z,1/k)
, this gives,
for some other constant
C^{\prime}_{k}
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,T\land\rho^{k}\land\rho^{k}_{n})}|a^{n}_{u}-a_{u}|\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,T\land\rho^{k}\land\rho^{k}_{n})}|A^{z}_{b^{n}_{u-}}-A^{z}_{b_{u-}}|\Big]\leq C^{\prime}_{k}|x_{n}-x|e^{C_{k}T}.

(52)

For
\varepsilon>0
and
k\geq 1
, we write

p_{n}(\varepsilon):=\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{[0,T)}(|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|+|a^{n}_{u}-a_{n}|)>\varepsilon\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}(\rho^{k}\leq T)+p_{n,k}^{1}+p_{n,k}^{2}(\varepsilon),

where

\displaystyle p_{n,k}^{1}=\mathbb{P}(\rho^{k}>T,\rho^{2k}_{n}\leq T)\quad\text{and}\quad p_{n,k}^{2}(\varepsilon)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{[0,T\land\rho^{k}\land\rho^{2k}_{n})}(|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|+|a^{n}_{u}-a_{u}|)>\varepsilon\Big).

First,
\lim_{n}p_{n,k}^{2}(\varepsilon)=0
for each
k\geq 1
by (
51
)-(
52
). Next, we observe that

\rho^{k}>T,\rho^{2k}_{n}\leq T
implies that
\sup_{[0,T\land\rho^{k}\land\rho^{2k}_{n})}|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|>1/(2k)
, whence

p_{n,k}^{1}\leq p_{n,k}^{2}(1/2k)
and
\lim_{n}p_{n,k}^{1}=0
for each
k\geq 1
as well.
We thus find
\limsup_{n}p_{n}(\varepsilon)\leq\mathbb{P}(\rho^{k}\leq T)
. Since

\lim_{k}\rho^{k}=\infty
a.s. by Proposition
45
-(b), the conclusion follows.

Step 3.3.
We next check that for any
T>0
,
\sup_{[0,T]}|\tau^{n}_{u}-\tau_{u}|
tends to
0
in probability.
Recalling (
15
) and using the subadditivity of the function
r\mapsto r\land 1
on
[0,\infty)
, we write

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,T]}|\tau^{n}_{u}-\tau_{u}|\land 1\Big]\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\mathbb{E}[|\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{v-}}(a^{n}_{v},e)-\bar{\ell}_{b_{v-}}(a_{v},e)|\land 1]\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}v.

Recall that
\bar{\ell}_{y}(A,e)\leq\ell(e)
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
, and that

\int_{\mathcal{E}}(\ell(e)\land 1)\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)<\infty
, see (
9
). By dominated convergence,
it thus suffices to verify that for all
v\in[0,T]
, for
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
,

\lim_{n}\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{v-}}(a^{n}_{v},e)=\bar{\ell}_{b_{v-}}(a_{v},e)
in probability. This follows from Lemma
44
-(ii)
and Step 3.2.

Step 3.4.
By Lemma
42
, the generalized inverse
L_{t}=\inf\{u\geq 0:\tau_{u}>t\}
is a.s. finite
for all
t\geq 0
and continuous on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We thus classically deduce from Step 3.3 that for all
T>0
,
\sup_{[0,T]}|L^{n}_{t}-L_{t}|\to 0
in probability.

Step 3.5.
Here we prove that for all
t\geq 0
,
\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}-}=t)=\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}}=t)=0
and for all
n\geq 0
, all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}(L_{t}^{n}\in\mathrm{J},\tau^{n}_{L_{t}^{n}-}=t)=\mathbb{P}(L_{t}^{n}\in\mathrm{J},\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t}}=t)=0
.

We only study
L_{t},\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}}
, the case of
L_{t}^{n},\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t}-},\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t}}

being treated in the very same way. Since
L_{0}=0\notin\mathrm{J}
a.s., it suffices to treat the case where
t>0
. By Remark
5
, we always have
\tau_{L_{t}-}\leq t\leq\tau_{L_{t}}
and

L_{t}=u\in\mathrm{J}
if and only if
u\in\mathrm{J}
and
t\in[\tau_{u-},\tau_{u}]
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}-}=t)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u-}=t\leq\tau_{u}\}}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u-}=t\leq\tau_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u-}}(a_{u},e_{u})\}}\Big].

By the Poisson compensation formula,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}-}=t)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}=t\leq\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}=t\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big].

This last quantity equals
0
, since there is a.s. at most one
u\in[0,\infty)
such that
\tau_{u}=t

by Lemma
42
. Similarly (and using that
\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}-}=t)=0
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}}=t)=\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}-}<t=\tau_{L_{t}})=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}<t=\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big],

which equals
0
by by Lemma
44
-(i).

Step 3.6.
Here we prove that
I_{n}=\mathbb{E}[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-R_{t}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}]\to 0
.
Everywhere in this step we may use the dominated convergence theorem, since
R
and
R^{n}
take values in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}

which is bounded.

We have
R_{t}=b_{L_{t}}
on
\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}
, so that
I_{n}\leq I_{n,A}^{1}+I_{n,A}^{2}+I_{n,A}^{3}
, where,
setting
D=\text{diam}(\mathcal{D})
,

\displaystyle I_{n,A}^{1}=2D(\mathbb{P}(L_{t}>A)+\mathbb{P}(L^{n}_{t_{n}}>A)),\quad I_{n,A}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[|b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}-b_{L_{t}}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J},L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A\}}],

\displaystyle I_{n,A}^{3}=\mathbb{E}[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J},L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A\}}].

By Step 3.4 and since
L
is continuous,
L^{n}_{t_{n}}\to L_{t}
. Hence

\limsup_{n}I^{1}_{n,A}\leq 4D\mathbb{P}(L_{t}>A)
,
so that
\lim_{A\to\infty}\limsup_{n}I^{1}_{n,A}=0
. Next,

I^{2}_{n,A}\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,A]}|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|b_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}-b_{L_{t}}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}\Big].

The first term
tends to
0
as
n\to\infty
by Step 3.2, as well as the second one, since
L^{n}_{t_{n}}\to L_{t}

and since
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
implies that
b
is continuous at
L_{t}
.
Thus
\lim_{n}I^{2}_{n,A}=0
for all
A>0
.
We now recall that if
L^{n}_{t_{n}}\notin\mathrm{J}
, then
R^{n}_{t_{n}}=b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}=b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}
(see Remark
6
-(a))
and write

I_{n,A}^{3}=\mathbb{E}[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{n}_{t_{n}}\in\mathrm{J},L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J},L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A\}}]\leq 2DI_{n,A,\delta}^{31}+I_{n,A,\delta}^{32}+I_{n,A}^{33},

where

\displaystyle I_{n,A,\delta}^{31}=\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A,L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J},\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}-\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}>\delta),

\displaystyle I_{n,A,\delta}^{32}=\mathbb{E}\Big[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}-}}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A,L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J},0<\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t}}-\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}<\delta\}}\Big],

\displaystyle I_{n,A}^{33}=\mathbb{E}\Big[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A,L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J},L^{n}_{t_{n}}\in\mathrm{J},\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t}}=\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}\}}\Big].

Observe that
I_{n,A}^{33}=0
when
\beta=*
, since we have
\bar{\ell}_{y}(B,e)>0
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all

B\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
(see Remark
3
) and thus
\tau^{n}_{u}>\tau^{n}_{u-}
for all

u\in\mathrm{J}
, but this is not the case when
\beta\neq*
since
\bar{\ell}_{y}(B,e)=0
as soon as
h_{y}(B,e(0))\notin\mathcal{D}
.

We have
\lim_{n}I_{n,A,\delta}^{31}=0
for each
A>0
, each
\delta>0
, because
\tau^{n}
converges uniformly to

\tau
on
[0,A]
by Step 3.3, because
L^{n}_{t_{n}}\to L_{t}
, and because
\tau
is
continuous at
L_{t}
when
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
. Next,
we recall that when
L^{n}_{t_{n}}=u\in\mathrm{J}
, we have

|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}|\leq M(e_{u})\land D
, see (
48
). Since
L^{n}_{t_{n}}=u\in\mathrm{J}
if and only if

t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{u-},\tau^{n}_{u}]=[\tau^{n}_{u-},\tau^{n}_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u-}}(a^{n}_{u},e)]
,

\displaystyle I_{n,A,\delta}^{32}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}-}}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A,0<\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t}}-\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}-}<\delta\}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J},u\leq A}[M(e_{u})\land D]{\bm{1}}_{\{t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{u-},\tau^{n}_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u-}}(a^{n}_{u},e)],\ell_{b^{n}_{u-}}(a_{u}^{n},e_{u})\in(0,\delta)\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[M(e)\land D]{\bm{1}}_{\{t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{u},\tau^{n}_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u}}(a^{n}_{u},e)]\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u}}(a_{u}^{n},e_{u})\in(0,\delta)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[M(e)\land D]{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u}}(a_{u}^{n},e_{u})\in(0,\delta)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big].

By Step 3.2,
Lemma
44
-(ii) and dominated convergence (recall (
9
)), we conclude that

\limsup_{n}I_{n,A,\delta}^{32}\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[M(e)\land D]{\bm{1}}_{\{\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e_{u})\in(0,\delta)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big],

so that
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{n}I_{n,A,\delta}^{32}=0
for all
A>0
by dominated convergence again.
Finally, observe that
L^{n}_{t_{n}}\in\mathrm{J}
and
\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}=\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}-}}
implies that
t_{n}=\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}=\tau^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}-}}
and thus that
R^{n}_{t_{n}}=b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}
, see Remark
6
-(f). Thus

I_{n,A}^{33}\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[|b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}-b^{n}_{L^{n}_{t_{n}-}}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\leq A,L^{n}_{t_{n}}\leq A,L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}\Big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,A]}|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[|b_{L^{n}_{t_{n}}}-b_{L^{n}_{t_{n}-}}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}\Big].

Using Step 3.2, that
L^{n}_{t_{n}}\to L_{t}
by Step 3.4
and that
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
implies that
b
is continuous at
L_{t}
,
we conclude that
\lim_{n}I^{33}_{n,A}=0
for each
A>0
.
The step is complete.

Step 3.7.
We finally check that
J_{n}=\mathbb{E}[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-R_{t}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\}}]
tends to
0
.
Again, we may use everywhere the dominated convergence theorem, since
R
and
R^{n}
take values in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
.

By Step 3.5, we see that
\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\}=\{\tau_{L_{t}-}<t<\tau_{L_{t}}\}
up to some negligible event.
We write
J_{n}\leq DJ_{n}^{1}+J_{n}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle J_{n}^{1}=\mathbb{P}(L^{n}_{t_{n}}\neq L_{t},\tau_{L_{t}-}<t<\tau_{L_{t}})\quad\text{and}\quad J_{n}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[|R^{n}_{t_{n}}-R_{t}|{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{n}_{t_{n}}=L_{t},\tau_{L_{t}-}<t<\tau_{L_{t}}\}}\Big].

By Remark
5
, we know that for all
u\in\mathrm{J}
,
\{L^{n}_{t_{n}}=u\}=\{t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{u-},\tau^{n}_{u}]\}
. Thus

\{L^{n}_{t_{n}}=L_{t}\}=\{t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{L_{t}-},\tau^{n}_{L_{t}}]\}
, and

J_{n}^{1}=\mathbb{P}\Big(t_{n}\notin[\tau^{n}_{L_{t}-},\tau^{n}_{L_{t}}],t\in(\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}})\Big)\to 0,

because
t_{n}\to t
,
\tau^{n}_{L_{t}-}\to\tau_{L_{t}-}
and
\tau^{n}_{L_{t}}\to\tau_{L_{t}}
by Step 3.3.
We next write, recalling (
16
),

\displaystyle J_{n}^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\Big|h_{b^{n}_{u-}}(a^{n}_{u},e_{u}(t_{n}-\tau^{n}_{u-}))-h_{b_{u-}}(a_{u},e_{u}(t-\tau_{u-}))\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{u-},\tau^{n}_{u}]\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{t\in(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big|h_{b^{n}_{u}}(a^{n}_{u},e(t_{n}-\tau^{n}_{u}))-h_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e(t-\tau_{u}))\Big|{\bm{1}}_{\{t_{n}\in[\tau^{n}_{u},\tau^{n}_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u}}(a^{n}_{u},e)]\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt{\bm{1}}_{\{t\in(\tau_{u},\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)]\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big]

by the compensation formula and since
\tau^{n}_{u}=\tau^{n}_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{n}_{u}}(a^{n}_{u},e_{u})
and
\tau_{u}=\tau_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e_{u})
for all
u\in\mathrm{J}
. By dominated convergence and the following arguments, we conclude that

\lim_{n}J_{n}^{2}=0
:

\bullet

\mathbb{E}[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{E}}{\bm{1}}_{\{t\in(\tau_{u},\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e))\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u]=\mathbb{P}(t\in(\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}}))<\infty
by the compensation formula,

\bullet
for
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
, for all
u\geq 0
such that

t\in(\tau_{u},\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e))
and
t_{n}\in(\tau_{u}^{n},\tau_{u}^{n}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}^{n}}(a_{u}^{n},e))
,

|h_{b^{n}_{u}}(a^{n}_{u},e(t-\tau^{n}_{u}))-h_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e(t-\tau_{u}))|\leq|b^{n}_{u}-b_{u}|+||a^{n}_{u}-a_{u}|||e(t_{n}-\tau^{n}_{u})|+|e(t_{n}-\tau^{n}_{u})-e(t-\tau_{u})|,

which vanishes as
n\to\infty
, because

b^{n}_{u}\to b_{u}
and
a^{n}_{u}\to a_{u}
by Step 3.2,
\tau^{n}_{u}\to\tau_{u}
by Step 3.3,
and
e(t-\tau^{n}_{u})\to e(t-\tau_{u})
since

t-\tau_{u}>0
is not a jump time of
e
(for
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
-a.e.
e\in\mathcal{E}
).

Step 4.
We finally check (d).
Since

\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}))=\sigma(\{\{w\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}):w(t)\in A\}:t\geq 0,A\in\mathcal{B}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})\}),

it suffices, by a monotone class argument,
to verify that for all
0\leq t_{1}<\dots<t_{k}
,
for all
A\in\mathcal{B}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{k})
, the map
x\mapsto\mathbb{Q}_{x}[(X^{*}_{t_{1}},\dots,X^{*}_{t_{k}})\in A]

is measurable from
\partial\mathcal{D}
to
\mathbb{R}
. Since now
\{A\in\mathcal{B}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{k}):x\mapsto\mathbb{Q}_{x}[(X^{*}_{t_{1}},\dots,X^{*}_{t_{k}})\in A]

is measurable
\}
is a
\sigma
-field, we may assume that
A
is a closed
subset of
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{k}
. In such a case, we may find a sequence
\varphi_{\ell}\in C_{b}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{k})
decreasing to
{\bm{1}}_{A}
,
and it finally suffices to show that for any
\varphi\in C_{b}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{k})
,

x\mapsto\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t_{1}},\dots,X^{*}_{t_{k}})]
is measurable from
\partial\mathcal{D}
to
\mathbb{R}
.
But this map is actually continuous: this follows from Step 3, where we have seen that if
x_{n}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
satisfies

\lim_{n}x_{n}=x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, then it is possible to build
R^{x_{n}}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}
and

R^{x}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{x}
on the same probability space in such a way that for each
t\geq 0
,

R^{x_{n}}_{t}\to R^{x}_{t}
in probability.
∎

6
The reflected stable process starting from anywhere

Our goal is now to prove Theorem
9
.
We start with the Markov property,
of which the proof is fastidious. We do not follow Blumenthal’s
approach
[
14
, Chapter V, Section 2]
, which rely more on resolvents, and approximations
of the process. Our proof is closer in spirit to the one given by Salisbury
[
63
]
, although
it is more involved as we deal with excursions of Markov processes in domains. It basically relies on the
compensation formula and the Markov property of the excursion measure. The rotational
invariance of the isotropic stable process is also crucial.

Proposition 46
.

Fix
\beta\in\{*\}\cup(0,\alpha/2)
and suppose Assumption
1
.
The family
(\mathbb{Q}_{x})_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}
defines a Markov process on the
canonical filtered probability space of càdlàg
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
-valued processes.

Proof.

We denote by
\Omega^{*}=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
the canonical space endowed with the Skorokhod
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology, by
X^{*}=(X_{t}^{*})_{t\geq 0}
the canonical process and by

(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{*})_{t\geq 0}
the canonical filtration. We have to show that for any
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
,
any
t\geq 0
, any bounded measurable functions
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\psi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\psi_{2}((X_{s+t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]=\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\psi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}\big[\psi_{2}((X_{s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\big]\Big].

(53)

Step 1.
We first rephrase Definition
8
. We denote by
\mathbb{D}_{0}
the subset of

\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
of paths
z
such that
z(0)\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
and for
z\in\mathbb{D}_{0}
,
we set
\tilde{\ell}(z)=\inf\{r>0,\>z(r)\notin{\mathcal{D}}\}
. Then we define

\mathcal{C}:\mathbb{D}_{0}\to\partial\mathcal{D}
and
\Psi:\mathbb{D}_{0}\to\Omega^{*}
by

\mathcal{C}(z)=\Lambda(z(\tilde{\ell}(z)-),z(\tilde{\ell}(z)))\quad\text{and}\quad\Psi(z)=\big(z(t){\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(z)\}}+\mathcal{C}(z){\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq\tilde{\ell}(z)\}}\big)_{t\geq 0}.

(54)

Next, we introduce the stopping time
d_{0}^{*}=\inf\{s>0,\>X_{s}^{*}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}
.
Recall that under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
, the process
Z=(Z_{s})_{s\geq 0}
is an ISP
α,x
.
From Definition
8
, for any
x\in\mathcal{D}
, any bounded measurable

\varphi:\Omega^{*}\times\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0},(X_{s+d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]=\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\tilde{\varphi}(\Psi(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z))\Big],

(55)

\displaystyle\text{where}\quad\tilde{\varphi}(w,x)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}\big[\varphi(w,X^{*})\big]\quad\text{for all $w\in\Omega^{*}$, all $x\in\partial\mathcal{D}$.}

Step 2.
Here we show that (
53
) holds when
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
Consider, on some filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{G},(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0},\mathbb{P})
,
a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure
\Pi_{\beta}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}

on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{E}
with intensity
\mathrm{d}u\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)
, together with the processes

(a_{u}^{x},b_{u}^{x},\tau_{u}^{x})_{u\geq 0}
and
(L^{x}_{t},R^{x}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
from Definition
4
. By definition,
the law of
(R_{t}^{x})_{t\geq 0}
is
\mathbb{Q}_{x}
. For
t>0
, we also set
g_{t}^{x}=\tau_{L_{t}^{x}-}^{x}
and

d_{t}^{x}=\tau_{L_{t}^{x}}^{x}
.
To lighten the notations, we will most of the time drop the superscript
x
, except for
(R_{t}^{x})_{t\geq 0}
.

Step 2.1.
Let us verify that
with the convention that
(v,v)=\emptyset
for all
v\geq 0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad\mathcal{Z}:={}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\{t\geq 0:R^{x}_{t}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}},\quad\text{we have}\quad\mathcal{Z}^{c}=\cup_{u\in\mathrm{J}}(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{Z}=\{\tau_{u},\>u\geq 0\}\cup\{\tau_{u-},\>u>0\}.

(56)

Pick
t
in
\mathcal{O}:=\cup_{u\in\mathrm{J}}(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})
.
There exists
u\in\mathrm{J}
such that
\Delta\tau_{u}>0
and
t\in(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})
. By
Remark
5
,
L_{t}=u
and
t\in(\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}})
, so that

R_{t}^{x}\in\mathcal{D}
by Remark
6
-(b). Consequently,

\mathcal{O}\subset\{t\geq 0,R_{t}^{x}\in\mathcal{D}\}=\{t\geq 0,R_{t}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}^{c}
, whence

\mathcal{O}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{c}
since
\mathcal{O}
is open.
Now, we infer from Bertoin
[
10
, page 13]

that
\mathcal{O}^{c}=\{\tau_{u},\>u\geq 0\}\cup\{\tau_{u-},\>u>0\}
,
because
(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is a strictly increasing càdlàg path such that
\lim_{u\to\infty}\tau_{u}=\infty
,
see Lemma
42
. For all
u\geq 0
,
R_{\tau_{u}}=b_{u}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
: by Remark
6
with
t=\tau_{u}

(whence
L_{t}=u
by Remark
5
), we either have
u\notin\mathrm{J}
and thus
R_{t}=b_{u}

(see Remark
6
-(a))
or
u\in\mathrm{J}
and
t=\tau_{u}
and thus
R_{t}=b_{u}
(see Remark
6
-(f)). Consequently,

\{\tau_{u},\>u\geq 0\}\subset\mathcal{Z}
.
Hence for all
u>0
, all
h\in(0,u)
,
\tau_{u-h}\in\mathcal{Z}
, so that

\tau_{u-}=\lim_{h\searrow 0}\tau_{u-h}\in\mathcal{Z}
, since
\mathcal{Z}
is closed. All in all

\mathcal{O}^{c}=\{\tau_{u},\>u\geq 0\}\cup\{\tau_{u-},\>u>0\}\subset\mathcal{Z}
. Since
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{O}^{c}
(because we have seen that
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{c}
),
we conclude that
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{O}^{c}=\{\tau_{u},\>u\geq 0\}\cup\{\tau_{u-},\>u>0\}

and that
\mathcal{Z}^{c}=\mathcal{O}=\cup_{u\in\mathrm{J}}(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})
.
We have proved (
56
).

We next check that for all
t>0
,

g_{t}=\sup\{s<t,\>R_{s}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}\quad\text{and}\quad d_{t}=\inf\{s>t,\>R_{s}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}.

Indeed, recall that
g_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}-}
and observe that by (
56
)

\displaystyle\sup\{s<t,\>R_{s}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}=

\displaystyle\sup{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\{s<t,\>R_{s}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup(\mathcal{Z}\cap[0,t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup((\{\tau_{u}:u\geq 0\}\cup\{\tau_{u-}:u>0\})\cap[0,t)),

which equals
\tau_{L_{t}-}
since
t\in[\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}}]
by Remark
5
.
The other identity is checked similarly.

Setting now
d_{0}=\inf\{s>0:R_{s}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}
, we observe that
d_{0}=0
a.s.
(recall that
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
). This implies that
\mathbb{Q}_{x}(d_{0}^{*}=0)=1

when
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, a fact we will use later. Indeed, for any
t>0
,
d_{0}\leq d_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}}
, which tends to

\tau_{0}=0
as
t\to 0
since
\tau
is right continuous and since
\lim_{t\to 0}L_{t}=0
because

\tau
is strictly increasing (so that
L
is continuous).

By Remark
6
-(a)-(b)-(c)-(d)-(f) and since
\tau_{L_{t}}=t
when
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
(because

t\in[\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}}]
and
\Delta\tau_{L_{t}}=0
), we see that for all
t\geq 0
,

R_{t}^{x}=h_{b_{L_{t}}-}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}}(t-\tau_{L_{t}-})){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}+b_{L_{t}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}=t\}}.

(57)

Recalling that
d_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}}
, we deduce that
(R_{s\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0}
is
\mathcal{G}_{L_{t}}
-measurable.

Step 2.2.
We now show that for any
t\geq 0
, the law of
(R_{d_{t}+s}^{x})_{s\geq 0}
conditionally on

(R_{s\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0}
is
\mathbb{Q}_{R_{d_{t}}^{x}}
. To this end, we fix
t\geq 0
and we introduce

\Pi_{\beta}^{t}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}_{t}}\delta_{(u,e^{t}_{u})}\quad\text{where}\quad\mathrm{J}_{t}=\{u\geq 0,\>u+L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\}\quad\text{and}\quad e^{t}_{u}=e_{u+L_{t}}.

Since
L_{t}
is a
(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-stopping time, it comes that
\Pi_{\beta}^{t}
is a

(\mathcal{G}_{u+L_{t}})_{u\geq 0}
-Poisson measure distributed as
\Pi_{\beta}
and is independent of

\mathcal{G}_{L_{t}}
.
One easily checks that
(b_{u}^{t})_{u\geq 0}=(b_{L_{t}+u})_{u\geq 0}
and

(\tau_{u}^{t})_{u\geq 0}=(\tau_{L_{t}+u}-\tau_{L_{t}})_{u\geq 0}
solve (
14
) and (
15
) with the Poisson
measure
\Pi^{t}_{\beta}
, with
(a^{t}_{u}=a_{L_{t}+u})_{u\geq 0}
and with
b^{t}_{0}=b_{L_{t}}=R_{d_{t}}^{x}
(recall that

d_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}}
and use Remark
6
-(f)).
Next,

(L_{s}^{t})_{s\geq 0}=(L_{d_{t}+s}-L_{d_{t}})_{s\geq 0}
is the right-continuous inverse of
(\tau_{u}^{t})_{u\geq 0}
:
this is easily checked, recalling that
d_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}}
and using that

L_{d_{t}}=L_{t}
(because
L
is constant on
[\tau_{L_{t}-},\tau_{L_{t}}]
, see Remark
5
).
By (
57
),

R_{d_{t}+s}^{x}=\begin{cases}h_{b_{(L_{d_{t}+s})-}}(a_{L_{d_{t}+s}},e_{L_{d_{t}+s}}(d_{t}+s-\tau_{(L_{d_{t}+s})-}))&\text{if }\tau_{L_{d_{t}+s}}>d_{t}+s,\\
b_{L_{d_{t}+s}}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Since
L_{d_{t}}=L_{t}
, we find
b_{(L_{d_{t}+s})-}=b_{(L_{s}^{t})-}^{t}
,
a_{L_{d_{t}+s}}=a_{L_{s}^{t}}^{t}

and
\tau_{(L_{d_{t}+s})-}-d_{t}=\tau^{t}_{(L^{t}_{s})-}
, so that

R_{d_{t}+s}^{x}=\begin{cases}h_{b_{(L_{s}^{t})-}^{t}}(a_{L_{s}^{t}}^{t},e_{L_{s}^{t}}^{t}(s-\tau_{L_{s}^{t}}^{t}))&\text{if }\tau_{L_{s}^{t}}^{t}>s,\\
b_{L_{s}^{t}}^{t}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus, conditionally on
\mathcal{G}_{L_{t}}
,

(R_{d_{t}+s}^{x})_{s\geq 0}
is an
(\alpha,\beta)
-stable process reflected in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
,
driven by the Poisson measure
\Pi^{t}_{\beta}
and starting from
R^{x}_{d_{t}}
.
Hence by Theorem
7
-(b),
conditionally on
\mathcal{G}_{L_{t}}
,
(R_{d_{t}+s}^{x})_{s\geq 0}
is
\mathbb{Q}_{R_{d_{t}}^{x}}
-distributed.
This completes the step since

\sigma((R_{s\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\subset\mathcal{G}_{L_{t}}
by Step 2.1.

Step 2.3.
We show here that for any
t>0
, any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, any bounded measurable functions

\psi_{1},\psi_{2}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have (
53
). By a monotone class argument,
it suffices that for any bounded measurable functions

\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge g^{*}_{t}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((X_{(s+g^{*}_{t})\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((X_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{t}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge g^{*}_{t}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((X_{(s+g^{*}_{t})\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}\Big[\varphi_{3}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]\Big]

where
g^{*}_{t}=\sup\{s<t,\>X_{s}^{*}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}
and
d_{t}^{*}=\inf\{s>t,\>X_{s}^{*}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}
.
By definition, we have

\bm{\mathrm{I}}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((R_{d_{t}+s}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\Big].

Conditioning on
(R^{x}_{s\wedge d_{t}})_{s\geq 0}
and applying Step 2.2, we get

\bm{\mathrm{I}}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{d_{t}}^{x}}\big[\varphi_{4}((X_{s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\big]\Big].

We split this expectation according to whether
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
or
\tau_{L_{t}}>t
.
This covers all the cases, since
\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}}=t)=0

as seen in Step 3.5 of the proof of Theorem
7
.

First, when
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
, we have
d_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}}=t
so that

(R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0}=(R_{t})_{s\geq 0}
:

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{d_{t}}^{x}}\big[\varphi_{4}((X_{s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\big]{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((R_{t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{t}^{x}}\big[\varphi_{4}((X_{s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\big]{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}\Big].

Since
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
implies that
R_{t}^{x}=b_{L_{t}}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and since

\mathbb{Q}_{z}(d_{0}^{*}=0)=1
for all
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
(see Step 2.1)

\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{1}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{t}^{x}}\Big[\varphi_{3}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]{\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}\}}\Big].

(58)

To treat the case
\tau_{L_{t}}>t
, we introduce the function

\varphi_{5}:\Omega^{*}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}

defined by
\varphi_{5}(w,z)=\varphi_{3}(w)\mathbb{Q}_{z}[\varphi_{4}(X^{*})]
, so that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{d_{t}}^{x}}\big[\varphi_{4}((X_{s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\big]{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{5}((R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0},R_{d_{t}}^{x}){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}\Big].

We now aim to use the compensation formula. For a path
w\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and for
r\geq 0
,
we set
k_{r}w=(w(s\wedge r))_{s\geq 0}
and
\theta_{r}w=(w(r+s))_{s\geq 0}
. We set

\mathcal{A}=\{(b,a):b\in\partial\mathcal{D},a\in\mathcal{I}_{b}\}
and introduce
the function
\mathrm{H}:\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{E}\to\Omega^{*}
defined by

\mathrm{H}(b,a,e)=\Big(h_{b}(a,e(r)){\bm{1}}_{\{r<\bar{\ell}_{b}(a,e)\}}+g_{b}(a,e){\bm{1}}_{\{r\geq\bar{\ell}_{b}(a,e)\}}\Big)_{r\geq 0}.

From (
57
) and since
g_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}-}
and

d_{t}=\tau_{L_{t}}
, on the event
\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}
, we have

\displaystyle(R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0}=\mathrm{H}(b_{L_{t}-},a_{L_{t}},k_{t-\tau_{L_{t}-}}e_{L_{t}}),\quad(R_{(t+s)\wedge d_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0}=\mathrm{H}(b_{L_{t}-},a_{L_{t}},\theta_{t-\tau_{L_{t}-}}e_{L_{t}}),

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad R_{d_{t}}^{x}=g_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},\theta_{t-\tau_{L_{t}-}}e_{L_{t}}).

We now set

\tilde{\varphi}_{2}(b,a,e)=\varphi_{2}(\mathrm{H}(b,a,e))
and
\tilde{\varphi}_{5}(b,a,e)=\varphi_{5}(\mathrm{H}(b,a,e),g_{b}(a,e))
and we write

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge\tau_{L_{t}-}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\tilde{\varphi}_{2}(b_{L_{t}-},a_{L_{t}},k_{t-\tau_{L_{t}-}}e_{L_{t}})\tilde{\varphi}_{5}(b_{L_{t}-},a_{L_{t}},\theta_{t-\tau_{L_{t}-}}e_{L_{t}}){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge\tau_{u-}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\tilde{\varphi}_{2}(b_{u-},a_{u},k_{t-\tau_{u-}}e_{u})\tilde{\varphi}_{5}(b_{u-},a_{u},\theta_{t-\tau_{u-}}e_{u}){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u-}\leq t<\tau_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u-}}(a_{u},e_{u})\}}\bigg].

We used that
\tau_{L_{t}}>t
if and only if
L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}
and
\tau_{L_{t}-}\leq t<\tau_{L_{t}}
,
see Remark
5
, and that in such a case,

\tau_{L_{t}}=\tau_{L_{t}-}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}})
by (
15
).
Finally, we apply the compensation formula, which is licit because
(a_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is

(\mathcal{G}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
-predictable and so is
\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge\tau_{u-}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})
.
We then get

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge\tau_{u}}^{x})_{s\geq 0}){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}\leq t\}}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\tilde{\varphi}_{2}(

\displaystyle b_{u},a_{u},k_{t-\tau_{u}}e)

\displaystyle\times\tilde{\varphi}_{5}(b_{u},a_{u},\theta_{t-\tau_{u}}e){\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\bigg].

Since
u\mapsto\tau_{u}
is a.s. strictly increasing by Lemma
42
, there is at most one
u
for which

\tau_{u}=t
and we can replace
{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}\leq t\}}
by
{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}<t\}}
in the above integral.
Now, for every fixed
u\geq 0
,
we apply the Markov property of
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}

at time
t-\tau_{u}
, see Lemma
29
. This is possible because

\tau_{u}<t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)
implies that
\ell(e)\geq\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)>t-\tau_{u}>0
.
Finally, we stress that
{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)\}}
is a function of
(
\tau_{u},b_{u},a_{u}
and)
k_{t-\tau_{u}}e
. It comes that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge\tau_{u}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\!\!\tilde{\varphi}_{2}(b_{u},a_{u},k_{t-\tau_{u}}e)\varphi_{6}(b_{u},a_{u},e(t-\tau_{u})){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}<t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\bigg],

where

\varphi_{6}(b,a,z)=\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\tilde{\varphi}_{5}(b,a,(Z_{r\wedge\ell(Z)})_{r\geq 0})\Big]

and where we recall that under
\mathbb{P}_{z}
,
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is an ISP
α,z
.
Recalling the definition (
54
) of the functions
\Psi
and
\mathcal{C}
, it should be clear that

H(b,a,e)=\Psi(b+ae)
and
g_{b}(a,e)=\mathcal{C}(b+ae)
, whence
\tilde{\varphi}_{5}(b,a,e)=\varphi_{5}(\Psi(b+ae),\mathcal{C}(b+ae))
. By translation and rotational invariance of the isotropic
stable process, we thus have

\varphi_{6}(b,a,z)=\mathbb{E}_{h_{b}(a,z)}[\varphi_{5}(\Psi(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z))],

whence

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge\tau_{u}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\int_{\mathcal{E}}\!\!\tilde{\varphi}_{2}(b_{u},a_{u},k_{t-\tau_{u}}e)\mathbb{E}_{h_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e(t-\tau_{u}))}

\displaystyle\Big[\varphi_{5}(\Psi(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z))\Big]

\displaystyle{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}<t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(a_{u},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\bigg].

We can then use the compensation formula in the reverse way to conclude that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{E}_{h_{b_{{L_{t}}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}}(t-\tau_{L_{t}-}))}[\varphi_{5}(\Psi(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z))]{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{E}_{R_{t}^{x}}[\varphi_{5}(\Psi(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z))]{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}\Big],

since
R^{x}_{t}=h_{b_{L_{t}-}}(a_{L_{t}},e_{L_{t}}(t-\tau_{L_{t}-}))
when
\tau_{L_{t}}>t
, see (
57
).
Recalling now (
55
) and the definition of
\varphi_{5}(w,x)=\varphi_{3}(w)\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi_{4}(X^{*})]
, we see that for any
z\in\mathcal{D}
,

\mathbb{E}_{z}[\varphi_{5}(\Psi(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z))]=\mathbb{E}_{z}[\varphi_{3}(\Psi(Z))\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{C}(Z)}[\varphi_{4}(X^{*})]]=\mathbb{Q}_{z}\Big[\varphi_{3}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big].

Since we work on
\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}
, we have
R_{t}^{x}\in\mathcal{D}
so that

\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{t}^{x}}\Big[\varphi_{3}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{L_{t}}>t\}}\Big]

Recalling now the expression (
58
) of
\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{1}
and that

\bm{\mathrm{I}}=\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{1}+\bm{\mathrm{I}}_{2}
, we finally get

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi_{1}((R_{s\wedge g_{t}}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((R_{(s+g_{t})\wedge t}^{x})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{R_{t}^{x}}\Big[\varphi_{3}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge g_{t}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((X_{(s+g_{t}^{*})\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}\Big[\varphi_{3}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]\Big],

which was our goal.

Step 3.
We finally show that (
53
) holds when
x\in\mathcal{D}
.
We first show that for any measurable bounded functions

\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{J}}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((X_{(s+t)\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0}){\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}>t\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}\big[\varphi_{2}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((X_{d_{0}^{*}+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\big]{\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}>t\}}\Big].

(59)

First, we see from (
55
) that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{J}}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((Z_{s\wedge t})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}(\Psi((Z_{(s+t)\wedge\tilde{\ell}(Z)})_{s\geq 0}))\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{C}(Z)}\big[\varphi_{3}(X^{*})\big]{\bm{1}}_{\{\tilde{\ell}(Z)>t\}}\Big].

Conditionally on
(Z_{s\wedge t})_{s\geq 0}
and
\{\tilde{\ell}(Z)>t\}
,

(Z_{t+s})_{s\geq 0}
is an ISP
{}_{\alpha,Z_{t}}
, whence

\bm{\mathrm{J}}=\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((Z_{s\wedge t})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{E}_{Z_{t}}\Big[\varphi_{2}(\Psi((Z_{s\wedge\tilde{\ell}(Z)})_{s\geq 0}))\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{C}(Z)}\big[\varphi_{3}(X^{*})\big]\Big]{\bm{1}}_{\{\tilde{\ell}(Z)>t\}}\Big],

and (
59
) follows from (
55
). Using a monotone class argument,
we deduce from (
59
) that for any bounded measurable
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\psi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\psi_{2}((X_{t+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0}){\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}>t\}}\Big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\psi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}[\psi_{2}(X^{*})]{\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}>t\}}\Big].

(60)

We next prove that for any measurable bounded functions

\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{K}}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((X_{(s+d_{0}^{*})\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((X_{t+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0}){\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}\leq t\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{2}((X_{(s+d_{0}^{*})\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}[\varphi_{3}(X^{*})]{\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}\leq t\}}\Big].

(61)

We first write
\bm{\mathrm{K}}=\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi_{1}((X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{4}(t-d_{0}^{*},(X_{s+d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0}){\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}\leq t\}}]
, where

\text{for all $r\geq 0$, all $w\in\Omega^{*}$},\quad\varphi_{4}(r,w)=\varphi_{2}(w((s\land r)_{r\geq 0})\varphi_{3}(w((s+r)_{s\geq 0}).

Since
d_{0}^{*}
is a function of
(X_{s\wedge d_{0}^{*}}^{*})_{s\geq 0}
, we find, using (
55
), that

\displaystyle\bm{\mathrm{K}}=\mathbb{E}_{x}\Big[\varphi_{1}(\Psi((Z_{s\wedge\tilde{\ell}(Z)})_{s\geq 0})))\varphi_{5}(t-\tilde{\ell}(Z),\mathcal{C}(Z)){\bm{1}}_{\{\tilde{\ell}(Z)\leq t\}}\Big],

where for
r\geq 0
and
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,

\varphi_{5}(r,z)=\mathbb{Q}_{z}\Big[\varphi_{2}((X_{s\land r}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\varphi_{3}((X_{r+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\Big]=\mathbb{Q}_{z}\Big[\varphi_{2}((X_{s\land r}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{r}^{*}}[\varphi_{3}(X^{*})]\Big]

by Step 2.
Finally, we use (
55
) again to conclude the proof of (
61
).
By a monotone class argument,
we deduce from (
61
) that for any bounded measurable
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}:\Omega^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\psi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\psi_{2}((X_{t+s}^{*})_{s\geq 0}){\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}\leq t\}}\Big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}\Big[\psi_{1}((X_{s\wedge t}^{*})_{s\geq 0})\mathbb{Q}_{X_{t}^{*}}[\psi_{2}(X^{*})]{\bm{1}}_{\{d_{0}^{*}\leq t\}}\Big].

Together with (
60
), this shows that (
53
) holds true.
The proof is complete.
∎

We can now give the

Proof of Theorem
9
.

By Proposition
46
, the family
(\mathbb{Q}_{x})_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}
defines a Markov process
on the canonical filtered probability space of càdlàg
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
-valued processes.

For any
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
it holds that
\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bm{1}}_{\{X^{*}_{t}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}t]=0
.
Indeed, recalling Definition
8
, it suffices to treat the case where
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
With the notation of the proof of Proposition
46
, it suffices that

\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bm{1}}_{\{R_{t}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}t=0
a.s. Since
\lim_{u\to\infty}\tau_{u}=\infty
a.s.
by Lemma
42
, it is enough that
\int_{0}^{\tau_{u}}{\bm{1}}_{\{R_{t}^{x}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}t=0
a.s. for all
u>0
.
Recalling (
56
), we thus need that
\int_{0}^{\tau_{u}}{\bm{1}}_{\{t\in\mathcal{Z}\}}\mathrm{d}t=0
for all
u>0
,

i.e.
that
\int_{0}^{\tau_{u}}{\bm{1}}_{\{t\in\mathcal{Z}^{c}\}}\mathrm{d}t=\tau_{u}
. This follows from the facts that

\mathcal{Z}^{c}\cap[0,\tau_{u})=\cup_{v\in\mathrm{J},v\leq u}(\tau_{v-},\tau_{v})
, see (
56
), and
that
\tau_{u}=\sum_{v\in\mathrm{J},v\leq u}\Delta\tau_{v}
.

We finally check
that this Markov process is Feller,
i.e.
that for any
t>0
, any
\varphi\in C_{b}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
,
we have
\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]\to\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]
if
x_{n}\to x
,
where
X^{*}
is the canonical process on
\Omega^{*}=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
.

Fix
t>0
,
\varphi\in C_{b}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
and
x_{n}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
such that
x_{n}\to x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
.
Let us introduce
\psi(r,z)=\mathbb{Q}_{z}[\varphi(X^{*}_{r})]
for
r\geq 0
and
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
We know from Theorem
7
-(c) that the map
(r,z)\mapsto\psi(r,z)
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\partial\mathcal{D}
.
By Definition
8
, recalling that
Z
is, under
\mathbb{P}_{0}
, an ISP
α,0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X_{t}^{*})]=\mathbb{E}_{0}[\varphi(x+Z_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{\sigma>t\}}]+\mathbb{E}_{0}[\psi(t-\sigma,\Lambda(x+Z_{\sigma-},x+Z_{\sigma})){\bm{1}}_{\{\sigma\leq t\}}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}[\varphi(X_{t}^{*})]=\mathbb{E}_{0}[\varphi(x_{n}+Z_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{\sigma_{n}>t\}}]+\mathbb{E}_{0}[\psi(t-\sigma_{n},\Lambda(x_{n}+Z_{\sigma_{n}-},x_{n}+Z_{\sigma_{n}})){\bm{1}}_{\{\sigma_{n}\leq t\}}],

where
\sigma=\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)
(
i.e.

\sigma=\inf\{t>0:x+Z_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}
)
and
\sigma_{n}=\tilde{\ell}(x_{n}+Z)
. Note here that
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\sigma=0)=1
when
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
(see Lemma
33
),
in which case the above identity is trivial.

Case 1:
x\in\mathcal{D}
.
By (
43
),
we a.s. have

\inf_{t\in[0,\sigma)}d(x+Z_{t},\mathcal{D}^{c})>0\quad\text{and}\quad x+Z_{\sigma}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}.

Thus a.s., for all
n
large enough,
\inf_{t\in[0,\sigma)}d(x_{n}+Z_{t},\mathcal{D}^{c})>0
and

x_{n}+Z_{\sigma}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}
, implying that
\sigma_{n}=\sigma
and thus that

\Lambda(x_{n}+Z_{\sigma_{n}-},x_{n}+Z_{\sigma_{n}})\to\Lambda(x+Z_{\sigma-},x+Z_{\sigma})
by Lemma
76
.
It follows by dominated
convergence that
\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]\to\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]
.

Case 2:
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
Then
\mathbb{P}_{0}(\sigma=0)=1
, so that
\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]=\psi(t,x)
.
By (
42
) and a scaling argument, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there is
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}(\sigma_{n}>\varepsilon)\leq c_{\varepsilon}d(x_{n},\partial\mathcal{D})^{\alpha/2}
, so that
\sigma_{n}\to 0
in probability.
This implies that in probability,
{\bm{1}}_{\{\sigma_{n}>t\}}\to 0
,
{\bm{1}}_{\{\sigma_{n}\leq t\}}\to 1
,
and
\Lambda(x_{n}+Z_{\sigma_{n}-},x_{n}+Z_{\sigma_{n}})\to x
(because

\lim_{n}Z_{\sigma_{n}-}=\lim_{n}Z_{\sigma_{n}}=0
in probability by right-continuity of
Z
at time
0
).
It follows by dominated convergence that
\mathbb{Q}_{x_{n}}[\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]\to\psi(t,x)
.
∎

Let us check Proposition
10
about the behavior of the process at the boundary.

Proof of Proposition
10
.

Recall Definitions
4
and
8
.
By the strong Markov property, we may assume that
R_{0}=x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
Also recall that
\mathcal{Z}={}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\{t\geq 0,\>R_{t}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}}
and that
\mathcal{Z}^{c}=\cup_{u\in\mathrm{J}}(\tau_{u-},\tau_{u})
,
see (
56
).
It remains to check that for any
u\in\mathrm{J}
, such that
\Delta\tau_{u}>0
, we have

R_{\tau_{u-}}=R_{(\tau_{u-})-}
if
\beta=*
and
R_{\tau_{u-}}\neq R_{(\tau_{u-})-}
if
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
.

Let thus
u\in\mathrm{J}
such that
\Delta\tau_{u}>0
. By Remark
5
, we have
u=L_{\tau_{u}}=L_{\tau_{u-}}
.
From the definition (
16
) of
R
, with
t=\tau_{u-}
, which satisfies
\tau_{L_{t}}=\tau_{u}>\tau_{u-}=t
,
we get

R_{\tau_{u-}}=h_{b_{u-}}(a_{u},e_{u}(t-\tau_{u-}))=b_{u-}+a_{u}e_{u}(0)=R_{(\tau_{u-})-}+a_{u}e_{u}(0).

We finally used that
R_{(\tau_{u-})-}=b_{u-}
