(\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon})_{u\geq 0}
, that
for all
n\geq 1
,

L_{t}^{\varepsilon}=T_{n-1}+c_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(t-\sigma_{n-1})\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in[\sigma_{n-1},\rho_{n})\quad\text{and}\quad L_{t}^{\varepsilon}=T_{n}\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in[\rho_{n},\sigma_{n}).

(81)

We have
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}=c_{\varepsilon}u
for
u\in[0,T_{1})
, whence
L^{\varepsilon}_{t}=c_{\varepsilon}^{-1}t=T_{0}+c_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(t-\sigma_{0})

for
t\in[0,c_{\varepsilon}T_{1})=[\sigma_{0},\rho_{1})
.

We have
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{T_{1}}=c_{\varepsilon}T_{1}+\sigma_{1}-\rho_{1}=\sigma_{1}
, whence
L^{\varepsilon}_{t}=T_{1}
for
t\in[\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})
.

We have
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}=c_{\varepsilon}u+\sigma_{1}-\rho_{1}
for
u\in[T_{1},T_{2})
, whence

L^{\varepsilon}_{t}=c_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(t-\sigma_{1}+\rho_{1})=T_{1}+c_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(t-\sigma_{1})
for

t\in[c_{\varepsilon}T_{1}+\sigma_{1}-\rho_{1},c_{\varepsilon}T_{2}+\sigma_{1}-\rho_{1})=[\sigma_{1},\rho_{2})
.

We have
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{T_{2}}=c_{\varepsilon}T_{2}+\sigma_{1}-\rho_{1}+\sigma_{2}-\rho_{2}=\sigma_{2}
, whence
L^{\varepsilon}_{t}=T_{2}

for
t\in[\rho_{2},\sigma_{2})
.

Etc.

Step 5.3.
We fix
t\geq 0
and check (
78
). If
t=0
,
then
L^{\varepsilon}_{0}=0\notin\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
and
R^{\varepsilon}_{0}=x=b^{\varepsilon}_{0}
, which agrees with the third line
of (
78
). If
t>0
, there is
n\geq 1

such that either
t\in(\sigma_{n-1},\rho_{n})
or
t\in[\rho_{n},\sigma_{n})
or
t=\sigma_{n}
.

\bullet
If
t\in(\sigma_{n-1},\rho_{n})
, then
R^{\varepsilon}_{t}=R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}
by definition of

\rho_{n}
. Moreover,
L_{t}^{\varepsilon}=T_{n-1}+c_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(t-\sigma_{n-1})\in(T_{n-1},T_{n})
by (
81
),
so that
L^{\varepsilon}_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
and

b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}=R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}
by (
80
). Thus
R^{\varepsilon}_{t}=b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}
, which
agrees with the third line
of (
78
).

\bullet
If
t\in[\rho_{n},\sigma_{n})
(which implies that
\sigma_{n}>\rho_{n}
),
then
L^{\varepsilon}_{t}=T_{n}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
by (
81
), so that (
80
) tells us that
b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}=R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}
,
that
\tau_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}=c_{\varepsilon}T_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(\sigma_{k}-\rho_{k})=\rho_{n}
and finally that

\tau_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}=c_{\varepsilon}T_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\sigma_{k}-\rho_{k})=\sigma_{n}
.
Thus
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}>t
, and to agree with the first line of (
78
),
we have to verify that
R^{\varepsilon}_{t}=h_{R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}}(A_{R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}},e_{n}(t-\rho_{n}))
. This has
been seen in Step 4.

\bullet
If
t=\sigma_{n}
, then as previously,
L^{\varepsilon}_{t}=T_{n}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
,
b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}=R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}
,

\tau_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}=\rho_{n}
and
\tau_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}=\sigma_{n}
. Thus
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}=t
, and to agree with the second line
of (
78
),
we have to verify that
R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n}}=g_{R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}}(A_{R^{\varepsilon}_{\sigma_{n-1}}},e_{n})
. This has
been seen in Step 4.
∎

9.2
Estimates on the excursion measures

Here we give some crucial estimates on the measure
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}
that will proved in Section
10
.
Recall that
\ell:\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
M:\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
were defined in Subsection
2.1
.
For
r>0
and
e\in\mathcal{E}
, we recall that
\ell_{r}(e)=\inf\{t>0:e(t)\notin B_{d}(r\mathbf{e}_{1},r)\}
.
The constant
\chi_{\mathrm{G}}
is introduced in Definition
70
when
\beta=*
and we set

\chi_{\mathrm{G}}=1/(2\kappa_{\mathrm{G}}\Gamma(\beta+1))
when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
,
with
\kappa_{\mathrm{G}}
defined in Assumption
22
-(b).

Proposition 59
.

Grant Assumption
18
with some
\alpha\in(0,2)
and with
\kappa_{\mathrm{F}}=1/\Gamma(\alpha+1)

and either Assumption
22
-(a), in which case set
\beta=*
,
c_{\varepsilon}=\chi_{\mathrm{G}}\,\varepsilon^{1/2}

and
\theta_{0}=1/2
, or Assumption
22
-(b)
with some
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
, in which case set
c_{\varepsilon}=\chi_{\mathrm{G}}\,\varepsilon^{1-\beta/\alpha}
and

\theta_{0}=\beta/\alpha
.
Consider
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}
as in Notation
57
and
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
as in Subsection
2.1
.
For
\delta\in(0,1]
, define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{*}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)={\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\delta\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)={\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>\delta\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e)\quad\text{under Assumption~\ref{assump:moments}-(a)},

(82)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)={\bm{1}}_{\{|e(0)|>\delta\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)={\bm{1}}_{\{|e(0)|>\delta\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e)\quad\text{under Assumption~\ref{assump:moments}-(b)}.

(83)

For any
\delta\in(0,1]
, we have

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathcal{E})+\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathcal{E})<\infty.

(84)

For any
\theta\in(0,\theta_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[M(e)\wedge 1+\ell(e)\wedge[\ell(e)]^{\theta}]\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e)<\infty,

(85)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\big[M(e)\wedge 1+\ell(e)\wedge 1\big](\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}-\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta})(\mathrm{d}e)=0.

(86)

For all
\delta>0
, all
\phi:\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}
bounded and continuous for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\phi(e)\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\phi(e)\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e).

(87)

If
\beta=*
, for all
\delta>0
, all
r>0
,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\ell_{r}<\eta)=0.

(88)

In any case (when
\beta=*
or
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
), for all
r>0
,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\ell_{r}>\eta)=\infty.

(89)

When
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
, for all
a>0
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\{e\in\mathcal{E}:|e(0)|\leq\delta,\ell(e)>a\})=0.

(90)

9.3
Some continuity properties

We first verify that the exit time does not charge points (except possibly
0
when
\beta\neq*
)
under the excursion measure.

Lemma 60
.

Grant the same assumptions and notations as in Proposition
59
and suppose
Assumption
1
.
For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, for all
t>0
, for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, for all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, we have

\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\{e\in\mathcal{E}:\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,e)=t\})=0
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}\text{Law}((Y^{\varepsilon}_{t\land\ell(Y^{\varepsilon})})_{t\geq 0})
,
with
(Y^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
introduced in
Notation
57
. Let
S_{\varepsilon}
be the set of jump times of the Poisson measure
\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}
.
Since
(Y^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is piecewise constant with set of jump times
S_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\bar{\ell}_{x}(A,(Y^{\varepsilon}_{t\land\ell(Y^{\varepsilon})})_{t\geq 0})\in\{0\}\cup S_{\varepsilon}
. But

\mathbb{P}(t\in S_{\varepsilon})=0
for all
t\geq 0
.
∎

We next check some continuity properties.

Lemma 61
.

Grant the same assumptions and notations as in Proposition
59
and suppose
Assumption
1
. Consider a measurable family
(A_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
such that

A_{y}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
for each
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.

(i) Fix
\delta>0
,
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and a sequence
(z_{n})_{n\geq 1}
such that

z_{n}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
\lim_{n}z_{n}=z
. For any continuous bounded function

\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}
, any sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\geq 1}
decreasing to
0
,

\lim_{n}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(g_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},e),\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},e))\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon_{n},\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(g_{z}(A_{z},e),\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},e))\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e).

(ii) Fix
\delta>0
,
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and a sequence
(z_{n})_{n\geq 1}
such that

z_{n}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
\lim_{n}z_{n}=z
. Consider
s\geq 0
,
t\geq 0
and
(s_{n})_{n\geq 1}
,
(t_{n})_{n\geq 1}

such that
\lim_{n}s_{n}=s
and
\lim_{n}t_{n}=t
. If
s\neq t
, then for
any sequence
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\geq 1}
decreasing to
0
, any continuous bounded function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(h_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},e(t_{n}-s_{n})))

\displaystyle{\bm{1}}_{\{s_{n}<t_{n}<s_{n}+\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon_{n},\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(h_{z}(A_{z},e(t-s))){\bm{1}}_{\{s<t<s+\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e).

(iii) Consider a process
(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
converging in law to an isotropic

\alpha
-stable process
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
issued from
z\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
For each
t>0
, as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

\big(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})\}},\Lambda(Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})-},Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})}),\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})\big)\;\;\text{goes in law to}\;\;\big(Z_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(Z)\}},\Lambda(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}),\tilde{\ell}(Z)\big),

where we recall that
\tilde{\ell}(Z)=\inf\{t>0:Z_{t}\notin\mathcal{D}\}
.

Proof.

We start with (i).
By invariance of
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}
by any isometry sending
\mathbf{e}_{1}
to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
,
the integrals do not depend on the choice of
(A_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
. We thus may assume that
y\mapsto A_{y}

continuous at
z
, see Lemma
77
, so that
A_{z_{n}}\to A_{z}
. We set
m_{n,\delta}=\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon_{n},\delta}(\mathcal{E})
and

m_{\delta}=\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathcal{E})
. By (
87
) with
\phi=1
, we have
\lim_{n}m_{n,\delta}=m_{\delta}
.
Still by (
87
), the sequence of probability measures
(m_{n,\delta}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon_{n},\delta})_{n\geq 1}
on
\mathcal{E}

converges weakly to
m_{\delta}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}
. By Skorokhod’s representation theorem, we can find

\mathbf{w}_{n}\sim m_{n,\delta}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon_{n},\delta}
a.s. converging, for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology, to some

\mathbf{w}\sim m_{\delta}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}
, and we are reduced to show that
\lim_{n}I_{n}=I
, where

I_{n}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(g_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n}),\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n}))\Big]\quad\text{and}\quad I=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(g_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}),\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\Big].

Consider a (random) sequence of time changes
\lambda_{n}
such that
||\lambda_{n}-I||_{\infty}\to 0

and such that
\mathbf{v}_{n}:=\mathbf{w}_{n}\circ\lambda_{n}
converges to
\mathbf{w}
uniformly on compact intervals.
We have
g_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})=g_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})
and
\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})=\lambda_{n}(\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n}))
.
Assume for a moment that
\lim_{n}q_{n}=0
, where

q_{n}:=\mathbb{P}(\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})\neq\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})).

Then
\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})
of course goes to
\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})
in probability,
and

\displaystyle g_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})=g_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})=\Lambda(z_{n}+A_{z_{n}}\mathbf{v}_{n}(\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})-),z_{n}+A_{z_{n}}\mathbf{v}_{n}(\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})))

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\to\Lambda(z+A_{z}\mathbf{w}(\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})-),z+A_{z}\mathbf{w}(\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})))=g_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})

in probability by continuity of
\Lambda
, see Lemma
76
. We conclude that
\lim_{n}I_{n}=I

as desired by dominated convergence.

We now check that
\lim_{n}q_{n}=0
when
\beta=*
. Consider
r>0
as in Remark
3
and recall that

\bar{\ell}_{y}(A,e)\geq\ell_{r}(e)
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
, all
e\in\mathcal{E}
.
For any
\eta>0
, we write

q_{n}\leq q_{n,1}(\eta)+q_{2}(\eta)+q_{n,3}(\eta)
, where

\displaystyle q_{n,1}(\eta)=\mathbb{P}(\ell_{r}(\mathbf{v}_{n})\leq\eta),\qquad q_{2}(\eta)=\mathbb{P}(\ell_{r}(\mathbf{w})\leq\eta)

\displaystyle q_{n,3}(\eta)=\mathbb{P}(\ell_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})>\eta,\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})>\eta,\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})\neq\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})).

By Remark
3
, we have
\lim_{\eta\to 0}q_{2}(\eta)=0
. By (
88
), we have

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{n}\mathbb{P}(\ell_{r}(\mathbf{w}_{n})\leq\eta)=0
, which implies that

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{n}q_{n,1}(\eta)=0
. It thus
suffices to prove that
for each
\eta>0
,
\lim_{n}q_{n,3}(\eta)=0
. By (
43
) and the Markov property, see Lemma
29
, we know
that a.s., on the event
\{\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})>\eta\}
,

\inf\{d(h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(t)),\mathcal{D}^{c}),t\in[\eta,\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\}>0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{w}(\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}.

Since
\mathbf{v}_{n}
a.s. converges locally uniformly to
\mathbf{w}
, we deduce that

h_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})
converges locally uniformly to
h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})
, so that
a.s. on
\{\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})>\eta\}
, for all
n
large enough,

\inf\{d(h_{z_{n}}(A_{z},\mathbf{v}_{n}(t)),\mathcal{D}^{c}),t\in[\eta,\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\}>0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{v}_{n}(\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}.

Thus a.s. on
\{\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})>\eta\}
, we have
\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})\in[0,\eta)\cup\{\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})\}

for all
n
large enough.
This implies that
\lim_{n}q_{n,3}(\eta)=0
.

We assume that
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
and check that
\lim_{n}q_{n}=0
, and more precisely that a.s.,

\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})=\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})
for all
n
large enough.
We recall from (
8
) that

\mathbf{w}(0)
has a density (namely,
\mathbf{w}(0)\sim m_{\delta}^{-1}{\bm{1}}_{\{x\in\mathbb{H},|x|>\delta\}}|x|^{-d-\beta}\mathrm{d}x
), so that

h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(0))\notin\partial\mathcal{D}
a.s.

On
\{h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(0))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}\}
, we a.s. have

h_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n}(0))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}
for all
n
large enough, so that

\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})=\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})=0
for all
n
large enough.

On
\{h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(0))\in\mathcal{D}\}
, we a.s. have, by (
43
), recalling (
8
) and that

\mathbf{w}\sim m_{\delta}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}
,

\inf\{d(h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(t)),\mathcal{D}^{c}),t\in[0,\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\}>0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{w}(\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}))\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c}.

We conclude as in the case
\beta=*
(but directly with
\eta=0
) that on
\{h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(0))\in\mathcal{D}\}
, a.s.,
for all
n
large enough,
\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{v}_{n})=\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})
.

For (ii), we use the same notation as previously: we have to check that

\lim_{n}J_{n}=J
, where

\displaystyle J_{n}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(h_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n}(t_{n}-s_{n}))){\bm{1}}_{\{s_{n}<t_{n}<s_{n}+\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})\}}\Big],

\displaystyle J=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(t-s))){\bm{1}}_{\{s<t<s+\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})\}}\Big].

We already have seen that
h_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})
a.s. converges to
h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})

for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology and that
\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})

converges to
\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})
in probability. Since
\mathbf{w}
a.s. has no jump at time
t-s
and since
t_{n}-s_{n}

goes to
t-s
, we deduce that
h_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n}(t_{n}-s_{n}))
goes to
h_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w}(t-s))
in probability.
Since
s\neq t
, we have
{\bm{1}}_{\{s_{n}<t_{n}\}}\to{\bm{1}}_{\{s<t\}}
. Since finally
\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})\neq t-s
a.s.
by Lemma
60
, we have
{\bm{1}}_{\{t_{n}<s_{n}+\bar{\ell}_{z_{n}}(A_{z_{n}},\mathbf{w}_{n})\}}\to{\bm{1}}_{\{t<s+\bar{\ell}_{z}(A_{z},\mathbf{w})\}}
in probability.
The conclusion follows from dominated convergence.

We check (iii) when
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. By Lemma
33
,

(Z_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(Z)\}},\Lambda(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}),\tilde{\ell}(Z))=(0,z,0)
.
By the Skorokhod representation theorem, we may assume that

(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
a.s. goes to
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
Let us now show that a.s.,
\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})\to 0
. Since
\mathcal{D}\subset\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d},(u-z)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{z}>0\}

by convexity, it suffices to show that
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\inf_{s\in[0,t]}(Z^{\varepsilon}_{s}-z)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{z}<0
a.s. for every
t>0
.
This follows from the facts that
(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
a.s. goes to
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology
and that for any
t>0
,
\inf_{s\in[0,t]}(Z_{s}-z)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{z}<0
, see Lemma
33
.
This implies that
{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})\}}\to 0
(because
t>0
)
and that both
Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})-}
and
Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})}
a.s. go to
z
, so that

\Lambda(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)})\to\Lambda(z,z)=z
by Lemma
76
.

We finally check (iii) when
z\in\mathcal{D}
. By the Skorokhod representation theorem, we may assume that

(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
a.s. goes to
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
Since
Z_{0}=z\in\mathcal{D}
, we know from (
43
) that

\inf\{d(Z_{t},\mathcal{D}^{c}),t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(Z))\}>0\quad\text{and}\quad Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}^{c},

from which we deduce as in the proof of (i) that a.s.,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})=\tilde{\ell}(Z)\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Lambda(Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})-},Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})})=\Lambda(Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)-},Z_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}).

Since
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\ell}(Z)=t)=0
by Bogdan, Jastrzȩbski, Kassmann, Kijaczko and
Popławski
[
18
, Theorem 1.3]
,
we conclude that
{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})\}}
a.s. goes to
{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(Z)\}}
. Finally,
Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}
a.s. goes to
Z_{t}
because
Z
a.s. has no jump at time
t
.
∎

9.4
Convergence of the boundary process

In this subsection, we prove the convergence in law of the process
(b_{t}^{\varepsilon},\tau_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
towards
the limiting boundary process
(b_{t},\tau_{t})_{t\geq 0}
, through
tightness/martingale problems arguments.

Proposition 62
.

Grant the same assumptions and notations as in Proposition
59
and suppose
Assumption
1
. Consider a measurable family
(A_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
such that

A_{y}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
for each
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
Consider for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
some
x_{\varepsilon}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and the solution
(b^{\varepsilon}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}

to (
76
)-(
77
) with
b^{\varepsilon}_{0}=x_{\varepsilon}
.
If
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}x_{\varepsilon}=x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
then
(b^{\varepsilon}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
converges in law to
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
in

\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology,
where
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is as in Definition
4
with
b_{0}=x
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into five steps.

Step 1.
We first show that the family of processes
((b^{\varepsilon}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0},\varepsilon\in(0,1])
is tight
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology
and that any limit point
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
satisfies
\Delta b_{u}=\Delta\tau_{u}=0
a.s. for every
deterministic
u\geq 0
. To this end,
we apply the Aldous criterion, see for instance
Jacod and Shiryaev
[
47
, Section VI, Thm. 4.5 and Remark 4.7]
.
It suffices that for all
T>0
,

(i)
\lim_{A\to\infty}\sup_{\varepsilon\in[0,1]}\mathbb{P}[\sup_{[0,T]}(|b^{\varepsilon}_{u}|+\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})>A]=0
,

(ii)
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\sup_{(S,S^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}_{T,\delta}}\mathbb{E}[(|b^{\varepsilon}_{S^{\prime}}-b^{\varepsilon}_{S}|+\tau^{\varepsilon}_{S^{\prime}}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{S})\land 1]=0
, where
\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}_{T,\delta}
is the set of pairs of stopping times
(S,S^{\prime})

in the filtration of
(b^{\varepsilon}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}

such that
0\leq S\leq S^{\prime}\leq S+\delta\leq T
.

We start with (i). Since for any
u\geq 0
,
b_{u}^{\varepsilon}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and since
\mathcal{D}
is bounded,
we immediately deduce that
\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{[0,T]}|b^{\varepsilon}_{u}|]<\infty
.
Next, recalling (
77
) and that
\bar{\ell}_{y}(A,e)\leq\ell(e)
, we find that

\sup_{[0,T]}\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}\leq c_{\varepsilon}T+I_{\varepsilon,T}+J_{\varepsilon,T}\leq\chi_{\mathrm{G}}T+I_{\varepsilon,T}+J_{\varepsilon,T}
, where

I_{\varepsilon,T}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell(e){\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)\leq 1\}}\Pi^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)\quad\text{and}\quad J_{\varepsilon,T}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell(e){\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>1\}}\Pi^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e).

Recall that
\theta_{0}\in(0,1/2]
was defined in Proposition
59
and fix

\theta\in(0,\theta_{0})
.
Using that
(a+b)^{\theta}\leq a^{\theta}+b^{\theta}
for all
a,b\geq 0
, we find

J_{\varepsilon,T}^{\theta}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[\ell(e)]^{\theta}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e)>1\}}\Pi^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e),\;\;\text{whence}\;\;I_{\varepsilon,T}+J_{\varepsilon,T}^{\theta}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{E}}(\ell(e)\land[\ell(e)]^{\theta})\Pi^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e).

Thus
\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\mathbb{E}[I_{\varepsilon,T}+J_{\varepsilon,T}^{\theta}]<\infty
by (
85
). All this proves (i).

We carry on with (ii). Using (
76
), (
77
), that
(a+b)\land 1\leq a\land 1+b\land 1

for all
a,b\geq 0
and that
|g_{y}(A,e)-y|\leq M(e)
and
\bar{\ell}_{y}(A,e)\leq\ell(e)
,
we get that for any
(S,S^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}_{T,\delta}
,

(|b_{S^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon}-b^{\varepsilon}_{S}|+\tau_{S^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{S})\land 1\leq\int_{S}^{S^{\prime}}\int_{\mathcal{E}}[M(e)\land 1+\ell(e)\land 1]\Pi_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e).

We conclude from (
85
) (and since
\ell(e)\land 1\leq\ell(e)\land[\ell(e)]^{\theta})

that

\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\mathbb{E}[(|b_{S^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon}-b^{\varepsilon}_{S}|+\tau_{S^{\prime}}^{\varepsilon}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{S})\land 1]\leq C\delta,

which implies (ii).

Step 2.
We consider a sequence
(b^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
converging in law to some

(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology, and show that
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}

solves (
14
)-(
15
) (with e.g.
a_{u}=A_{b_{u-}}
). Such a process being unique in law,
see Theorem
7
and Step 2 of its proof, this will complete the proof.

By the theory of martingale problems, it suffices that for any

\varphi\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

M_{u}^{\varphi}:=\varphi(b_{u},\tau_{u})-\varphi(x,0)-\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big[\varphi(g_{b_{v}}(A_{b_{v}},e),\tau_{v}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{v}}(A_{b_{v}},e))-\varphi(b_{v},\tau_{v})\Big]\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}v

is a martingale in the canonical filtration of
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
. In other words, we need that
for any
n\geq 1
, any
0\leq u_{1}<\cdots<u_{n}\leq v<w
, any

\varphi,\psi_{1},\cdots,\psi_{n}\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

\mathbb{E}[H((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})]=0,

(91)

where for
(y,r)\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

\displaystyle H(y,r)=

\displaystyle\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(y_{u_{i}},r_{u_{i}})\Big)\bigg(\varphi(y_{w},r_{w})-\varphi(y_{v},r_{v})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\int_{v}^{w}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big[\varphi(g_{y_{s}}(A_{y_{s}},e),r_{s}+\bar{\ell}_{y_{s}}(A_{y_{s}},e))-\varphi(y_{s},r_{s})\Big]\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}s\bigg).

We fix such a functional
H
, the rest of the proof is devoted to establish (
91
).

Step 3.
Since
(b^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
solves (
76
)-(
77
), we have

\mathbb{E}[K_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})]=0
, where

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}(y,r)=

\displaystyle\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(y_{u_{i}},r_{u_{i}})\Big)\bigg(\varphi(y_{w},r_{w})-\varphi(y_{v},r_{v})-\int_{v}^{w}c_{\varepsilon}\partial_{r}\varphi(y_{s},r_{s})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\int_{v}^{w}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big[\varphi(g_{y_{s}}(A_{b_{y_{s}}},e),r_{s}+\bar{\ell}_{y_{s}}(A_{b_{y_{s}}},e))-\varphi(y_{s},r_{s})\Big]\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}s\bigg).

We thus may write, for any
k\geq 1
and any
\delta\in(0,1]
,

\mathbb{E}[H((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})]=\mathbb{E}[H((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})]-\mathbb{E}[K_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})]=I_{1}^{\delta}+I_{2}^{k,\delta}+I_{3}^{k,\delta}+I_{4}^{k},

(92)

where, defining
H^{\delta}
(resp.
H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon}
,
H_{\varepsilon}
) as
H
replacing
\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}
by

\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}
(resp.
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}
,
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}
), see (
82
)-(
83
),

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\delta}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[H((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})]-\mathbb{E}[H^{\delta}((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})],

\displaystyle I_{2}^{k,\delta}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[H^{\delta}((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})]-\mathbb{E}[H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})],

\displaystyle I_{3}^{k,\delta}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})]-\mathbb{E}[H_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})],

\displaystyle I_{4}^{k}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[H_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})]-\mathbb{E}[K_{\varepsilon_{k}}((b_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\tau_{u}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{u\geq 0})].

We have
\sup_{(y,r)\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}|H_{\varepsilon}(y,r)-K_{\varepsilon}(y,r)|\leq Cc_{\varepsilon}

for some constant
C>0
, so that

\lim_{k\to\infty}I_{4}^{k}=0.

(93)

Step 4.
Here we prove that

\lim_{\delta\to 0}I_{1}^{\delta}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{k}|I_{3}^{k,\delta}|=0.

(94)

Since
\varphi
is bounded together with it first derivatives, we see that for all

(y,r)\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

|H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon}(y,r)-H_{\varepsilon}(y,r)|\leq C\int_{v}^{w}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big(|g_{y_{s}}(A_{b_{y_{s}}},e)-y_{s}|\land 1+\bar{\ell}_{y_{s}}(A_{b_{y_{s}}},e))\land 1\Big)(\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}-\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta})(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}s.

Recalling that
|g_{b}(A,e)-b|\leq M(e)
and
\bar{\ell}_{b}(A,e)\leq\ell(e)
, we end with

|H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon}(y,r)-H_{\varepsilon}(y,r)|\leq C(w-v)\int_{\mathcal{E}}\Big(M(e)\land 1+\ell(e)\land 1\Big)(\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}-\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta})(\mathrm{d}e).

It then directly follows from (
86
) that
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{k}|I_{3}^{k,\delta}|=0
. One
shows similarly that
\lim_{\delta\to 0}I_{1}^{\delta}=0
, using (
9
), which implies that

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\big[M(e)\wedge 1+\ell(e)\wedge 1\big](\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}-\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta})(\mathrm{d}e)=0.

Step 5.
Recall (
92
) and that our goal is to prove (
91
). By (
93
)-(
94
),
it suffices to check that for each
\delta\in(0,1]
,
\lim_{k\to\infty}I^{k,\delta}_{2}=0
.
Fix
\delta\in(0,1]
and assume for a moment that

(a)
\sup_{(y,r)\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}(|H^{\delta}(y,r)|+|H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon_{k}}(y,r)|)<\infty
,

(b) if
(y_{k},r_{k})\to(y,r)
in
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
for the the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology and if
(y,r)
has
no jump at times
u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v,w
, then

\lim_{k}H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon_{k}}(y_{k},r_{k})=H^{\delta}(y,r)
.

Then, we use the Skorokhod representation theorem to find

(\bar{b}^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u},\bar{\tau}^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u})_{u\geq 0})
(with the same law as
(b^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u})_{u\geq 0})
)
a.s. converging to
(\bar{b}_{u},\bar{\tau}_{u})_{u\geq 0})
(with the same law as
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0})
) and write

|I^{k,\delta}_{2}|=|\mathbb{E}[H^{\delta}((\bar{b}_{u},\bar{\tau}_{u})_{u\geq 0})]-\mathbb{E}[H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon_{k}}((\bar{b}^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u},\bar{\tau}^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u})_{u\geq 0}))]|\leq\mathbb{E}[|H^{\delta}((\bar{b}_{u},\bar{\tau}_{u})_{u\geq 0})-H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon_{k}}((\bar{b}^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u},\bar{\tau}^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{u})_{u\geq 0}))|].

This last quantity tends to
0
as
k\to\infty
by dominated convergence, thanks to points (a) and (b) and since

(\bar{b}_{u},\bar{\tau}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
a.s. has no jump at times
u_{1},\dots,u_{k},v,w
, see Step 1.

Point (a) follows from (
84
) and the fact that the functions involved in the
definition of
H^{\delta}
and
H^{\delta}_{\varepsilon}
are bounded.
For (b), the only difficulty is to check that for a.e.
s\in[v,w]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{k}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(g_{y^{k}_{s}}(A_{y^{k}_{s}},e),r_{s}^{k}+\bar{\ell}_{y^{k}_{s}}(A_{b_{y^{k}_{s}}},e))\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon_{k},\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(g_{y_{s}}(A_{y_{s}},e),r_{s}+\bar{\ell}_{y_{s}}(A_{y_{s}},e))\mathfrak{n}_{\beta}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e).

This follows from Lemma
61
-(i) and the fact that

\lim_{k}(y^{k}_{s},r^{k}_{s})=(y_{s},r_{s})
for all
s\in[v,w]
which is not a jump time of
(y,r)
,
which is the case for a.e.
s\in[v,w]
.
∎

9.5
Convergence of the Markov scattering process

We denote by
\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x}
the law of
(R_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
(the solution to (
75
))
issued from
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
and recall that

\mathbb{Q}_{x}
was introduced in Definitions
4
and
8
.
We also introduce the corresponding transition semigroups acting on continuous functions

\varphi:{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}
and defined for any
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
, any
t\geq 0
, as

\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi(x)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(X_{t}^{*})],\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\varphi(X_{t}^{*})],

where
(X_{t}^{*})_{t\geq 0}
is the canonical process on
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
.
We first show some tightness result.

Lemma 63
.

Adopt the same assumptions and notations as in Proposition
55
.
The family
(\mathbb{Q}_{x}^{\varepsilon},x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}},\varepsilon\in(0,1])

is tight, the set
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
being endowed with the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
Moreover,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}\mathbb{Q}_{x}^{\varepsilon}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|X^{*}_{t}-x|\Big]=0.

(95)

Proof.

We divide the proof in several steps.

Step 1.
Let us first show that (
95
) implies the tightness. Since
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
is compact,
it suffices, by the Aldous criterion, see
Jacod and Shiryaev
[
47
, Section VI, Thm. 4.5]
, that for all
T>0
,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\;\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\;\sup_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}\;\sup_{(S,S^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}_{T,\eta}}\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x}[|X^{*}_{S^{\prime}}-X^{*}_{S}|]=0.

(96)

Here
\mathcal{A}_{T,\eta}
is the set of all couples
(S,S^{\prime})
of stopping times (in the canonical filtration of
X^{*}
)
such that
0\leq S\leq S^{\prime}\leq S+\eta\leq T
. But for
(S,S^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}_{T,\eta}
, for any
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
,
using the strong Markov property at time
S
, we see that

\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x}[|X^{*}_{S^{\prime}}-X^{*}_{S}|]\leq\sup_{y\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{y}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|X^{*}_{t}-y|\Big].

Thus (
95
) implies (
96
) as desired.

Step 2.
For
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
and
\eta>0
, we set, recalling that
\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})=\inf\{t>0:X^{*}_{t}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}
,

\rho_{\varepsilon}(x,\eta)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}^{\varepsilon}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|X^{*}_{t}-x|\Big]\quad\hbox{and}\quad\rho^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(x,\eta)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}^{\varepsilon}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})]}|X^{*}_{t}-x|\Big].

By the strong Markov property at time
\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})
, for all
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
,

\rho_{\varepsilon}(x,\eta)\leq\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\eta)+\sup_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}\rho_{\varepsilon}(y,\eta)
.

Step 3.
Here we show that
\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\eta)=0
.

We recall that the solution
(R^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
to (
75
)
starting at
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
is
\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x}
-distributed, so that

\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(x,\eta)=\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|]
. We introduce
introduce the free process issued from
0

Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{H}}u\mathrm{M}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}v),

(97)

with the same Poisson measure
\mathrm{M}_{\varepsilon}
as in (
75
). One can verify that

R^{\varepsilon}_{t}=x+Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}
for all
t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon}))
and that

R^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})}=\Lambda(x+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})-},x+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})-})\in[x+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})-},x+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})}]
.
As a conclusion,

\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\leq\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}|.

Since moreover
\sup_{t\in[0,\eta\land\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\leq\text{Diam}(\mathcal{D})
,
we only have to check that

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)=0,\quad\text{where}\quad\rho_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}|\land 1\Big].

Using that
\int_{|u|\leq 1}u\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(u)\mathrm{d}u=0

by rotational invariance, we may write
Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}=Z^{\varepsilon,1}_{t}+Z^{\varepsilon,2}_{t}
, where

Z^{\varepsilon,1}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{H}}u{\bm{1}}_{\{|u|\leq 1\}}\tilde{\mathrm{M}}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}v)\quad\text{ and }\quad Z^{\varepsilon,2}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{H}}u{\bm{1}}_{\{|u|>1\}}\mathrm{M}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}v).

Let us first show that
\mathbb{E}[\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,1}_{t}|^{2}]\leq C\eta
.
By Doob’s inequality, recalling Definition
53
and Notation
52
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,1}_{t}|^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{4\eta}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}{\bm{1}}_{\{|u|\leq 1\}}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(u)\mathrm{d}u=\frac{4\eta}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\Big[|\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/\alpha}E^{\varepsilon}U|^{2}{\bm{1}}_{\{|\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/\alpha}E^{\varepsilon}U|\leq 1\}}\Big],

with
E^{\varepsilon}\sim\text{Exp}(\varepsilon^{-1})
and
U\sim\mathrm{F}(u)\mathrm{d}u
. Using Assumption
18
and
that
\varepsilon^{-1}E^{\varepsilon}\sim\text{Exp}(1)
, we get

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,1}_{t}|^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{4C_{\mathrm{F}}\eta}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z\int_{\{|u|\leq\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}z^{-1}\}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2/\alpha}z^{2}|u|^{2}\mathrm{d}u}{|u|^{d+\alpha}}.

A simple computation shows that this last quantity equals
C\eta
for some constant
C>0
.

Let us next check that
\mathbb{E}[\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,2}_{t}|\land 1]\leq C\eta
, which will end the step.
We write

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,2}_{t}|\land 1\Big]\!\leq\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,2}_{t}|>0\Big)\!\leq\!\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathrm{M}_{\varepsilon}([0,\eta]\times\{|u|>1\}\times\mathbb{H})>0\Big)\!=\!1-\exp(-\eta a_{\varepsilon})\!\leq\!a_{\varepsilon}\eta,

where

a_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{|u|>1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(u)\mathrm{d}u=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}(|\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/\alpha}E^{\varepsilon}U|>1)\leq\frac{C_{\mathrm{F}}}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-z}\mathrm{d}z\int_{\{|u|>\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}z^{-1}\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{|u|^{d+\alpha}}.

This last quantity is finite and does not depend on
\varepsilon
, so that

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{[0,\eta]}|Z^{\varepsilon,2}_{t}|\land 1]\leq C\eta
.

Step 4.
It only remains to verify that
\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in\partial\mathcal{D}}\rho_{\varepsilon}(x,\eta)=0
.
Here we use that
(R^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
defined in Lemma
58
, issued from
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
is
\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x}
-distributed.
For any
a\in(0,1]
,

\rho_{\varepsilon}(x,\eta)=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta)}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\Big]\leq D\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}>a)+\mathbb{E}\Big[{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\leq a\}}\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\Big],

(98)

where
D=\text{Diam}(\mathcal{D})
.
We have
R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x=R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-b^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t}^{\varepsilon}-}+b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}-x
and, by (
78
),

|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}|\leq M(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}}){\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\}},

where we recall that
M(e)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(e)]}|e(t)|
. Moreover,

|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-b^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t}-}|\leq D
, whence

\mathbb{E}\Big[{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\leq a\}}\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{u\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\cap[0,a]}(M(e^{\varepsilon}_{u})\land D)+\sup_{u\in[0,a]}|b^{\varepsilon}_{u}-x|\Big].

Recalling (
76
) and that
|g_{y}(A,e)-y|\leq M(e)\land D
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
and

e\in\mathcal{E}
, we find

\mathbb{E}\Big[{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\leq a\}}\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\Big]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{a}\int_{\mathcal{E}}(M(e)\land D)\Pi_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)\Big]=2a\int_{\mathcal{E}}(M(e)\land D)\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e).

By (
85
), there is a constant
C>0
such that for all
a\in(0,1]
,

\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\sup_{x\in\partial\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}\Big[{\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\leq a\}}\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|R^{\varepsilon}_{t}-x|\Big]\leq Ca.

(99)

Define
r>0
as in Remark
3
and recall that
\bar{\ell}_{y}(A,\cdot)\geq\ell_{r}(\cdot)
.
Recalling (
77
), we conclude that

\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}\geq\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\ell_{r}(e)\Pi^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}e)=:\tilde{\tau}_{u}^{\varepsilon}
(which does not depend on

x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
). Hence

\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}>a)=\mathbb{P}(\tau^{\varepsilon}_{a}<\eta)\leq\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\tau}^{\varepsilon}_{a}<\eta)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Pi_{\varepsilon}([0,a]\times\{\ell_{r}>\eta\})=0\Big)=\exp(-a\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\ell_{r}>\eta)).

Using (
89
), we conclude that for any
a>0
,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in\partial\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}>a)\leq\lim_{\eta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\exp(-a\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\ell_{r}>\eta))=0.

(100)

Gathering (
98
), (
99
) and (
100
) completes the step.
∎

We next show the convergence of the semigroups when starting from the boundary.

Lemma 64
.

Adopt the same assumptions and notations as in Proposition
55
.
Let
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
t\geq 0
and, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, let
x_{\varepsilon}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
t_{\varepsilon}\geq 0

be such that
x_{\varepsilon}\to x
and
t_{\varepsilon}\to t
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
For any continuous function
\varphi:{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}
,
it holds that
\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})\to\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Proof.

We classically may assume that
t>0
and that
\varphi
is Lipschitz continuous.

Step 1.
The set
S=\{t\geq 0:\mathbb{Q}_{x}(X^{*}_{t}\in\partial\mathcal{D})>0\}
being Lebesgue-null (because it holds that

\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bm{1}}_{\{X^{*}_{t}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}}\mathrm{d}t]=0
,
see Theorem
9
), it suffices to treat the case where
t\notin S
.

Indeed, when
t\in S
, let

\eta_{n}>0
be decreasing to
0
and such that
t+\eta_{n}\notin S
. We find

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}+\eta_{n}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})=\mathcal{P}_{t+\eta_{n}}\varphi(x)
for each
n
.
But
\lim_{n}\mathcal{P}_{t+\eta_{n}}\varphi(x)=\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x)
by right-continuity of
X^{*}
, and

\lim_{n}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}+\eta_{n}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})
,
because, using the Markov property at time
t_{\varepsilon}
and that
\varphi
is Lipschitz continuous,

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}|\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}+\eta_{n}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})|\leq\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{y\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}|\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta_{n}}\varphi(y)-\varphi(y)|\leq C\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{y\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{y}[|X^{*}_{\eta_{n}}-y|],

which tends to
0
as
n\to\infty
by (
95
).
Thus
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})=\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x)
as desired.

Step 2.
We consider
(R^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}\sim\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}
,
together with
\Pi^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}}\delta_{(u,e^{\varepsilon}_{u})}
,
(b^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
,

(\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and
(L^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
, as in Lemma
58
. We also consider

(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{x}
, together with
\Pi=\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}}\delta_{(u,e_{u})}
,
(b_{u})_{u\geq 0}
,

(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
and
(L_{t})_{t\geq 0}
, as in Definition
4
.
We have to prove that
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}})]=\mathbb{E}[\varphi(R_{t})]
if
t\notin S
.
We thus assume that
t\notin S
and observe that

L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}
a.s., because
R_{t}=b_{L_{t}}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
when
L_{t}\notin\mathrm{J}
,
see (
16
).

Step 2.1.
By Proposition
62
,
(b^{\varepsilon}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}

converges in law to
(b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
By Lemma
86
-(i), this implies that
((b^{\varepsilon}_{u},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0},(L^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0})
converges in law
to
((b_{u},\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0},(L_{t})_{t\geq 0})
in
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})\times\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
with
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
endowed with
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
and
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
endowed with the
topology of the uniform convergence on compact time intervals.

Step 2.2.
Exactly as in Step 3.5 of the proof of Proposition
45
, making use of Lemma
60
,
one can check that we have both

\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}-}=t_{\varepsilon})=\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}=t_{\varepsilon})=0

and
\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}-}=t)=\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\tau_{L_{t}}=t)=0
.

Step 2.3.
We now show that

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq\delta)=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e_{L_{t}})\leq\delta)=0

By Step 2.2 and since
t\notin S
, we a.s. have
L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}
and
\tau_{L_{t}-}<t<\tau_{L_{t}}
.
By Lemma
86
-(ii) and Step 2.1, we conclude that

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\Delta\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}\leq\delta)\leq\mathbb{P}(\Delta\tau_{L_{t}}\leq\delta)=\mathbb{P}(0<\Delta\tau_{L_{t}}\leq\delta).

Since
\cap_{\delta>0}\{0<\Delta\tau_{L_{t}}\leq\delta\}=\emptyset
up to a negligible set, we end with

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\Delta\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}\leq\delta)=0.

The conclusion follows, since
\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in J_{\varepsilon},\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq\delta\}\subset\{\Delta\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}\leq\delta\}
: indeed,
\Delta\tau^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}=\bar{\ell}_{b^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}-}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}-}},e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})
.
Similarly,
\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e_{L_{t}})\leq\delta)\leq\mathbb{P}(0<\Delta\tau_{L_{t}}\leq\delta)
tends to
0

as
\delta\to 0
.

Step 2.4.
We have
\lim_{A\to\infty}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\geq A)=\lim_{A\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\geq A)=0
.

Indeed, we deduce from Step 2.1 that
L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}
goes in law to
L_{t}
, whence

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\geq A)\leq\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\geq A)
, which tends to
0
as
A\to\infty

since
L_{t}
is a.s. finite.

Step 2.5.
We now verify that if
\beta=*
, then for any
\delta>0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}){\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta\}}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e_{L_{t}})>\delta\}}\Big].

Using that
L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
if and only if there is
u\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
such that
\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u-}<t_{\varepsilon}<\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}

(we replaced broad inequalities by strict ones using Step 2.2) and that in such a case

R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}=h_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}},e^{\varepsilon}_{u})
, see (
78
) and

\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}=\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u-}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}-}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{L_{t_{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}-}},e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})
, we write,
for any
A>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}){\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\cap[0,A],\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta\}}\Big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\cap[0,A]}\varphi(h_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}},e^{\varepsilon}_{u})){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u-}<t_{\varepsilon}<\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{u})>\delta\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(h_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}},e)){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}<t_{\varepsilon}<\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big]

by the compensation formula. Recall that
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}
was introduced in Proposition
59
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\cap[0,A],\ell(e_{L_{t}})>\delta\}}\Big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\varphi(h_{b_{u}}(A_{b_{u}},e)){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}<t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(A_{b_{u}},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\delta}_{*}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}u\Big].

By the Skorokhod representation theorem, we may assume that
(\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u},b^{\varepsilon}_{u})_{u\geq 0}
a.s. converges to

(\tau_{u},b_{u})_{u\geq 0}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology. This implies that for a.e.
u\geq 0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}=\tau_{u}
and
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}b^{\varepsilon}_{u}=b_{u}
. Moreover, we have
\tau_{u}\neq t
for a.e.
u\geq 0

(since
(\tau_{u})_{u\geq 0}
is strictly increasing, there is at most one
u
such that
\tau_{u}=t
).
By Lemma
61
-(ii), we conclude that a.s., for a.e.
u\geq 0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathcal{E}}\!\!\varphi(h_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}},e)){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}<t_{\varepsilon}<\tau^{\varepsilon}_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}}(A_{b^{\varepsilon}_{u}},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)=\int_{\mathcal{E}}\!\!\varphi(h_{b_{u}}(A_{b_{u}},e)){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{u}<t<\tau_{u}+\bar{\ell}_{b_{u}}(A_{b_{u}},e)\}}\mathfrak{n}_{*}^{\delta}(\mathrm{d}e).

But
K:=\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathcal{E})<\infty
by (
84
). Hence
by dominated convergence, for all
A>0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}){\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\cap[0,A],\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta\}}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\cap[0,A],\ell(e_{L_{t}})>\delta\}}\Big].

To complete the step,
it only remains to observe that
\lim_{A\to\infty}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}I_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,A}=0
,
where

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,A}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\cap(A,\infty],\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta)+\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}\cap(A,\infty),\ell(e_{L_{t}})>\delta).

Since
I_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,A}\leq\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}>A)+\mathbb{P}(L_{t}>A)
, this follows from Step 2.4.

Step 2.6.
We next prove that if
\beta=*
, then
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon})=1
.

Since
t\notin S
, we have
\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J})=1
(see the very beginning of Step 2), whence

\Delta_{\varepsilon}:=|\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon})-1|=|\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon})-\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J})|\leq\Delta_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,1}+\Delta_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,2},

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,1}:=|\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq\delta)-\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e_{L_{t}})\leq\delta)|,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,2}:=|\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta)-\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e_{L_{t}})>\delta)|.

The conclusion follows, since we
know from Step 2.5 (with
\varphi=1
) that for each
\delta>0
,
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Delta_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,2}=0

and from Step 2.3
that
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Delta_{\varepsilon}^{\delta,1}=0
.

Step 2.7.
We conclude the proof when
\beta=*
.
We write

\Gamma_{\varepsilon}:=|\mathbb{E}[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}})]-\mathbb{E}[\varphi(R_{t})]|\leq\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta}+2||\varphi||_{\infty}\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{2,\delta},

where (recall that
L_{t}\in\mathrm{J}
a.s. because
t\notin S
)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta}=\Big|\mathbb{E}[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}){\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta\}}]-\mathbb{E}[\varphi(R_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e)>\delta\}}]\Big|,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{2,\delta}=\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\notin\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon})+\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq\delta)+\mathbb{P}(L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},\ell(e_{L_{t}})\leq\delta).

We have
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta}=0
for each
\delta>0
by Step 2.5 and

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{2,\delta}=0
by Steps 2.6 and 2.3.

Step 2.8.
We now prove that when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},|e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}(0)|\leq\delta)=0.

We write
\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},|e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}(0)|\leq\delta)\leq\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta,a,A}+\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{2,a}+\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{3,A}
,
where

\displaystyle\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta,a,A}=\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\cap[0,A],\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>a,|e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}(0)|\leq\delta),

\displaystyle\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{2,a}=\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq a)\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{3,A}=\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}>A).

But
\lim_{a\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{2,a}=0
by Step 2.3 and

\lim_{A\to\infty}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{3,A}=0
by Step 2.4. Hence we only have to check that
for each
a>0
, each
A>0
,
\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta,a,A}=0
.
But

\displaystyle\Theta_{\varepsilon}^{1,\delta,a,A}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Pi_{\varepsilon}([0,A]\times\{e\in\mathcal{E}:|e(0)|\leq\delta,\ell(e)>a\})>0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\exp(-A\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\{e\in\mathcal{E}:|e(0)|\leq\delta,\ell(e)>a\})).

The conclusion follows from (
90
).

Step 2.9.
We conclude when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
. Proceeding exactly as in Step 2.5
(recall that when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
,
\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon,\delta}(\mathrm{d}e)={\bm{1}}_{\{|e(0)|>\delta\}}\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e)
),
one can check that for any
\delta>0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}){\bm{1}}_{\{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon},|e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}(0)|>\delta\}}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(R_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{L_{t}\in\mathrm{J},|e_{L_{t}}(0)|>\delta\}}\Big].

Replacing systematically
\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})>\delta
by
|e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}(0)|>\delta
,

\ell(e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}})\leq\delta
by
|e^{\varepsilon}_{L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}}(0)|\leq\delta
,
\ell(e_{L_{t}})>\delta
by

|e_{L_{t}}(0)|>\delta
and
\ell(e_{L_{t}})\leq\delta
by
|e_{L_{t}}(0)|\leq\delta
, we
prove as in Step 2.6 that
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{P}(L^{\varepsilon}_{t_{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon})=1
and
conclude as in Step 2.7.
∎

We now extend the previous result to any starting point in
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
.

Lemma 65
.

Adopt the same assumptions and notations as in Proposition
55
.
Let
x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
,
t\geq 0
and, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, let
x_{\varepsilon}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}

be such that
x_{\varepsilon}\to x
.
Then for any continuous function
\varphi:{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}
,
it holds that
\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})\to\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Proof.

We recall that
\tilde{\ell}(r)=\inf\{t>0:r(t)\notin\mathcal{D}\}
for all
r\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
The case
t=0
being obvious, we suppose that
t>0
. We classically may assume that

\varphi
is Lipschitz continuous.

Step 1.
The set
S_{1}=\{t\geq 0:\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)>0\}
is Lebesgue-null. Indeed,
write
S_{1}=\cup_{n\geq 1}S_{1}^{n}
, where
S_{1}^{n}=\{t\geq 0:\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)\geq 1/n\}
,
recall that for any
y\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
, the set
j(y)=\{t\geq 0:\Delta y(t)\neq 0\}
is at most countable,
and notice that

\text{Leb}(S_{1}^{n})=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bm{1}}_{\{\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)\geq 1/n\}}\mathrm{d}t\leq n\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\Delta X^{*}_{t}\neq 0)\mathrm{d}t=n\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\text{Leb}(j(X^{*})))=0.

Moreover, the set
S_{2}=\{t\geq 0:\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})=t)>0\}
is at most countable,
because any probability measure
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
(here
\mathbb{Q}_{x}(\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\in\mathrm{d}t)
) has at most a countable number of atoms.
Exactly as in Step 1 of the proof of Lemma
64
, we may assume that

t\notin S:=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
.

Step 2.
We consider the free process

(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{H}}u\mathrm{M}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}v))_{t\geq 0}
defined in (
97
).
We recall that for
(R^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
the solution of (
75
) starting from
x_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{D}
,
we have
\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})=\tilde{\ell}(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon})
and
R^{\varepsilon}_{t}=x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}
for all
t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon}))
and

R^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(R^{\varepsilon})}=\Lambda(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})-},x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(Z^{\varepsilon})})
.

Step 3.

We show that the pure jump Lévy process
(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
converges, for the local uniform topology
(whence for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology),
to the ISP
α,0
. This stronger convergence will be useful in Section
10
.
The Lévy measure of
(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is

\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(z)\mathrm{d}z
. Recalling Notation
52
, we find

\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(z)=\varepsilon^{-1-d/\alpha}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-r}\mathrm{F}(\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}r^{-1}z)r^{-d}\mathrm{d}r.

Using Assumption
18
, one easily checks that, since
\kappa_{\mathrm{F}}=1/\Gamma(\alpha+1)

and setting
M=C_{\mathrm{F}}\Gamma(\alpha+1)
,

\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(z)\leq M|z|^{-d-\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(z)\sim|z|^{-d-\alpha}\quad\text{as $\varepsilon\to 0$.}

We now introduce
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)=|z|^{d+\alpha}\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(z)
, which is bounded by
M
and
tends to
1
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We consider a Poisson measure
\mathrm{K}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[0,M]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}

with intensity
\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}u|z|^{-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z
, as well as

\displaystyle U^{\varepsilon}_{t}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{M}\int_{\{|z|<1\}}z{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\}}\tilde{\mathrm{K}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}z)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{M}\int_{\{|z|\geq 1\}}z{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\}}\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}z),

\displaystyle Z_{t}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{M}\int_{\{|z|<1\}}z{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq 1\}}\tilde{\mathrm{K}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}z)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{M}\int_{\{|z|\geq 1\}}z{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq 1\}}\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}z).

It holds that
(U^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and that
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}

is an ISP
α,0
. We now prove that
\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}=\sup_{[0,T]}|U^{\varepsilon}_{t}-Z_{t}|\to 0
in probability and this will
complete the step. We write
\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}\leq\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{1}+\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{1}=

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{M}\int_{\{|z|<1\}}z({\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\}}-{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq 1\}})\tilde{\mathrm{K}}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}z)\Big|,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{M}\int_{\{|z|\geq 1\}}|z||{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\}}-{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq 1\}}|\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}z).

First, By Doob’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{1})^{2}]\leq

\displaystyle 4T\int_{\{|z|<1\}}\int_{0}^{M}|z|^{2}|{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\}}-{\bm{1}}_{\{u\leq 1\}}|\frac{\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}=4T\int_{\{|z|<1\}}\frac{|\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)-1|\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha-2}},

which tends to
0
by dominated convergence. Next, we have
\mathbb{P}(\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{2}\neq 0)\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{K}(A_{\varepsilon,T})>0)
,
where

A_{\varepsilon,T}=\{(t,u,z)\in[0,T]\times[0,M]\times\{|z|\geq 1\}:u\in[\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\land 1,\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\lor 1]\}
.
As a consequence,
\mathbb{P}(\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{2}\neq 0)\leq 1-e^{-\lambda_{\varepsilon,T}}
, with

\lambda_{\varepsilon,T}=T\int_{\{|z|\geq 1\}}\int_{0}^{M}{\bm{1}}_{\{u\in[\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\land 1,\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)\lor 1]\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}=T\int_{\{|z|\geq 1\}}\frac{|\varphi_{\varepsilon}(z)-1|\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

which tends to
0
by dominated convergence. Thus
\mathbb{P}(\Delta_{\varepsilon,T}^{2}\neq 0)\to 0
.

Step 4.
We show that the law of
(X^{*}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}},X^{*}_{\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})},\tilde{\ell}(X^{*}))

under
\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}
converges to the law of
(X^{*}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}},X^{*}_{\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})},\tilde{\ell}(X^{*}))

under
\mathbb{Q}_{x}
.

Recall Definition
8
: let
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be an
\alpha
-stable process issued from
0
,
set
R_{t}=x+Z_{t}
for all

t\in[0,\tilde{\ell}(x+Z))
and
R_{\tilde{\ell}(Z)}=\Lambda(x+Z_{\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)-},x+Z_{\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)})
. Then
\tilde{\ell}(R)=\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)
and

(R_{t\land\tilde{\ell}(R)})_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
(X^{*}_{t\land\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})})_{t\geq 0}
under
\mathbb{Q}_{x}
.
Recalling Step 2, it suffices to show that
((x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon})\}},\Lambda(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon})-},x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{\ell}(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon})}),\tilde{\ell}(x_{\varepsilon}+Z^{\varepsilon}))
goes in law
to
((x+Z_{t}){\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)\}},\Lambda(x+Z_{\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)-},x+Z_{\tilde{\ell}(x+Z)}),\tilde{\ell}(x+Z))
. This follows from Step 3 and
Lemma
61
-(iii).

Step 5.
Using the Markov property at time
\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}[{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}}\varphi(X^{*}_{t})]+\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}[{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t-\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})}\varphi(X^{*}_{\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}[\Psi_{\varepsilon}(X^{*}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}},X^{*}_{\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})},\tilde{\ell}(X^{*}))],

where for
(y,z,s)\in({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\cup\{0\})\times\partial\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\Psi_{\varepsilon}(y,z,s)={\bm{1}}_{\{t<s\}}\varphi(y)+{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq s\}}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t-s}\varphi(z)
.
Similarly, with
\Psi(y,z,s)={\bm{1}}_{\{t<s\}}\varphi(y)+{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq s\}}\mathcal{P}_{t-s}\varphi(z)
,

\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x)=\mathbb{Q}_{x}[\Psi(X^{*}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}},X^{*}_{\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})},\tilde{\ell}(X^{*}))].

By Step 4 and
Skorokhod’s representation theorem, we may consider
(Y_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},\rho_{\varepsilon})
(resp.
(Y,H,\rho)
)
distributed as
(X^{*}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})\}},X^{*}_{\tilde{\ell}(X^{*})},\tilde{\ell}(X^{*}))
under
\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}
(resp. under
\mathbb{Q}_{x}
)
such that almost surely,
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}(Y_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},\rho_{\varepsilon})=(Y,H,\rho)
.
Since
\mathbb{P}(\rho=t)=0
(because
t\notin S_{2}
), we a.s. have
{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\rho_{\varepsilon}\}}\to{\bm{1}}_{\{t<\rho\}}

and
{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}\}}\to{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq\rho\}}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Moreover, we deduce from Lemma
64

that
{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq\rho_{\varepsilon}\}}\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t-\rho_{\varepsilon}}\varphi(H_{\varepsilon})\to{\bm{1}}_{\{t\geq\rho\}}\mathcal{P}_{t-\rho}\varphi(H)
a.s.
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. All in all,
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(Y_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},\rho_{\varepsilon})\to\Psi(Y,H,\rho)
a.s. and, since

\Psi_{\varepsilon}
is bounded by
||\varphi||_{\infty}
, we conclude by dominated convergence that

\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\varphi(x_{\varepsilon})=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\varepsilon}(Y_{\varepsilon},H_{\varepsilon},\rho_{\varepsilon})]\to\mathbb{E}[\Psi(Y,H,\rho)]=\mathcal{P}_{t}\varphi(x).

∎

We are now ready to give the

Proof of Proposition
55
.

We consider
x_{\varepsilon}\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
such that
x_{\varepsilon}\to x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
,
consider a process
(R^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
with law
\mathbb{Q}^{\varepsilon}_{x_{\varepsilon}}
and
a process
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
with law
\mathbb{Q}_{x}
. Thanks to the tightness proved in Lemma
63
,
it suffices to show that the finite-dimensional distributions of
(R_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
converge
to those of
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
: for each
n\geq 1
, any
0\leq t_{1}<\ldots<t_{n}
and any
continuous bounded functions
\varphi_{1},\cdots,\varphi_{n}:{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}}^{\varepsilon})\bigg]\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}})\bigg]\quad\text{as }\varepsilon\to 0.

(101)

We work by induction on
n
.
When
n=1
, (
101
)
follows from Lemma
65
. Assume next that (
101
) holds true
for some
n\geq 1
and consider
0\leq t_{1}<\ldots<t_{n+1}
and some continuous bounded functions

\varphi_{1},\cdots,\varphi_{n+1}:{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\to\mathbb{R}
. By the Markov property
applied at time
t_{n}
, we get

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{n+1}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}}^{\varepsilon})\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{P}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})\prod_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}}^{\varepsilon})\bigg],

where
u=t_{n+1}-t_{n}>0
. A similar equality holds for
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and we write

\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{n+1}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}}^{\varepsilon})\bigg]-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{n+1}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}})\bigg]\bigg|\leq A_{\varepsilon}+B_{\varepsilon},

where

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\mathcal{P}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})\Big)\prod_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}}^{\varepsilon})\bigg]\bigg|,

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})\prod_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}}^{\varepsilon})\bigg]-\mathbb{E}_{x}\bigg[\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}})\prod_{k=1}^{n}\varphi_{k}(R_{t_{k}})\bigg]\bigg|.

Since the limiting process
(R_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is Feller, see Theorem
9
,
the function
\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}

is continuous so that we can use the induction hypothesis and apply (
101
)
with the functions

\varphi_{1},\cdots\varphi_{n-1}
and
\varphi_{n}\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}
, which implies that
B_{\varepsilon}\to 0

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Next, setting
C=\prod_{k=1}^{n}||\varphi_{k}||_{\infty}
, we write

A_{\varepsilon}\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{P}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})\big|\Big].

By Lemma
65
, it holds that

R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon}
converges in law to
R_{t_{n}}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
and therefore, by Skorokhod’s representation
theorem, there exist a family
\bar{R}_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon}
(with same law as
R_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon}
) a.s. converging to some

\bar{R}_{t_{n}}
(with same law as
R_{t_{n}}
). Thus

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{P}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{n+1}(\bar{R}_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(\bar{R}_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{P}_{u}^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{n+1}(\bar{R}_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(\bar{R}_{t_{n}})\big|\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(\bar{R}_{t_{n}}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}(\bar{R}_{t_{n}})\big|\Big].

The first term tends to
0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
by Lemma
65
and dominated convergence,
as well as the second one by continuity of
\mathcal{P}_{u}\varphi_{n+1}
and dominated convergence.
∎

10
Convergence of the excursion measures and related estimates

In this section, we first introduce some notations related to the discrete excursion measures.
Then we establish some estimates on random walks in the half-space. We next prove
Proposition
59
under Assumption
22
-(b) and then under
Assumption
22
-(a) separately, the case of (a) being more delicate.
Finally, we prove Lemma
56
.

10.1
Notation

For
w\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we set as usual

\ell(w)=\inf\{t>0:w(t)\notin\mathbb{H}\}\quad\text{and}\quad M(w)=\sup_{t\in[0,\ell(w)]}|w(t)|.

We also recall some notations introduced before, see Notation
57
and Proposition
59
.
With
\zeta=1/2
if
\beta=*
and
\zeta=\beta/\alpha
if
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
, we have, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}=\chi_{\mathrm{G}}\varepsilon^{-\zeta}\mathcal{L}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{t\land\ell(Y^{\varepsilon})}),

(102)

where
Y_{t}^{\varepsilon}=O_{\varepsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u)
,
with
O_{\varepsilon}\sim\mathrm{G}_{\varepsilon}(v)\mathrm{d}v
independent of the Poisson measure
\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}
on

\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
with intensity
\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}(u)\mathrm{d}u
.
Recall that
\mathrm{F}_{\varepsilon}
and
\mathrm{G}_{\varepsilon}
were introduced in Notation
52
.
Let us observe at once that since
(Y^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
(\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}Y^{1}_{t/\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
,
it holds that

\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-\zeta}(\Phi_{\varepsilon}\#\mathfrak{n}^{1}),\quad\text{where}\quad\Phi_{\varepsilon}(w)=(\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}w(t/\varepsilon))_{t\geq 0}.

(103)

For
w\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have
\ell(\Phi_{\varepsilon}(w))=\varepsilon\ell(w)
and
M(\Phi_{\varepsilon}(w))=\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}M(w)
.
For
x\in\mathbb{H}
, we introduce
Y^{\varepsilon,x}_{t}=x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u\mathrm{K}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}u)
.
Then
(Y^{\varepsilon,x}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
has the same law as
(\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}Y^{1,\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}x}_{t/\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
,
whence in particular, for all
x\in\mathbb{H}
, all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\ell(Y^{\varepsilon,x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\varepsilon\ell(Y^{1,\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}x})\quad\text{and}\quad M(Y^{\varepsilon,x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}M(Y^{1,\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}x}).

(104)

By (
102
), for any measurable
\phi:\mathcal{E}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, since
\mathrm{G}_{\varepsilon}(y)=\varepsilon^{-d/\alpha}\mathrm{G}_{1}(\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}y)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{E}}\phi(e)\mathfrak{n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}e)=

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathrm{G}}\varepsilon^{-\zeta-d/\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\big(\big(Y_{t\wedge\ell(Y^{\varepsilon,y})}^{\varepsilon,y}\big)_{t\geq 0}\big)\Big]\mathrm{G}_{1}(\varepsilon^{-1/\alpha}y)\mathrm{d}y

(105)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathrm{G}}\varepsilon^{-\zeta}\int_{\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\big(\big(Y_{t\wedge\ell(Y^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}x})}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}x}\big)_{t\geq 0}\big)\Big]\mathrm{G}_{1}(x)\mathrm{d}x.

(106)

Finally, we will use that for all
x\in\mathbb{H}
, for
(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}

an ISP
α,x
under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}\Big(\inf_{t\in[0,\ell(Z))}d(Z_{t},\mathbb{H}^{c})>0,Z_{\ell(Z)}\in\bar{\mathbb{H}}^{c}\Big)=1.

(107)

This is nothing but (
43
) when
\mathcal{D}=\mathbb{H}
. By (
107
) and the Markov property of
\mathfrak{n}_{*}
,
see Lemma
29
, we deduce that for all
\eta>0
,

\text{for $\mathfrak{n}_{*}$-a.e. $e\in\mathcal{E}$ such that $\ell(e)>\eta$,}\quad\inf_{t\in[\eta,\ell(e))}d(e(t),\mathbb{H}^{c})>0\quad\text{and}\quad e(\ell(e))\in\bar{\mathbb{H}}^{c}.

(108)

10.2
Estimates on random walks

Recall that
\mathrm{F}_{1}
denotes the law of
EU
where
E\sim\mathrm{Exp}(1)
and
U\sim\mathrm{F}(v)\mathrm{d}v
,
see Notation
52
.

Lemma 66
.

Grant Assumption
18
with
\kappa_{F}=1/\Gamma(\alpha+1)
.
Let
(\mathbf{S}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
be a random walk with incremental law

\mathrm{F}_{1}
starting at
0
.
There is
\mathcal{V}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{H}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\check{\ell}(x+\mathbf{S})>n)\sim\mathcal{V}(x_{1})n^{-1/2}\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(109)

where
\check{\ell}(x+\mathbf{S})=\min\{n\geq 1:(x+\mathbf{S}_{n})\notin\mathbb{H}\}
.
Moreover, there is a constant
C
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{H}
, any
n\geq 0
, any
y>0
,
setting
\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})=\max_{i=1,\dots,\check{\ell}(x+\mathbf{S})}|x+\mathbf{S}_{i}|
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\check{\ell}(x+\mathbf{S})>n)\leq C\mathcal{V}(x_{1})(n+1)^{-1/2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{V}(x_{1})\leq C(1+x_{1}^{\alpha/2}),

(110)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})>y)\leq C(1+|x|^{\alpha/2})y^{-\alpha/2}.

(111)

These estimates are more or less well-known, except maybe (
111
).

Remark 67
.

We will use (
111
) only under Assumption
22
-(b).
Its proof relies on the fact that

\text{there is $C>0$ such that for all $y>0$,}\quad\mathbb{P}(\check{M}(\mathbf{S})>y)\leq Cy^{-\alpha/2}.

(112)

This has been proved for a one-dimensional random walk by Doney
[
36
, Corollary 3]
,
and his arguments can be extended to the multidimensional case. We will check (
112
)
while proving Proposition
59
under Assumption
22
-(a), namely just after
the proof of Lemma
74
.

Proof of Lemma
66
.

We set
U_{n}=\mathbf{S}_{n}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}
which is a one-dimensional symmetric random walk.
We define its
decreasing ladder heights and epochs

(\gamma_{n},H_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
by setting

H_{0}=0
,
\gamma_{0}=0
and for any
n\geq 1
,
\gamma_{n}=\min\{k>\gamma_{n-1}:U_{k}<H_{n-1}\}
and

H_{n}=U_{\gamma_{n}}
. By the strong Markov property,

(\gamma_{n},-H_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a bivariate random walk, and each
component is increasing. We now introduce the renewal function
\mathcal{W}:(0,\infty)\to[1,\infty)

of
(H_{n})_{n\geq 0}
defined by
\mathcal{W}(y)=\mathbb{E}[T_{y}]
,
where
T_{y}=\min\{n\geq 1:y+H_{n}<0\}
. Then (
109
), with
\mathcal{V}(y)=a\mathcal{W}(y)
for some
constant
a>0
, is a rather classical fact,
see for instance Denisov-Wachtel
[
69
, Theorem 1]
. The fact that there is no slowly
varying function in the asymptotics follows from the symmetry of the random walk, see for
instance
[
7
]
.
The first bound in (
110
) follows again from
[
69
, Theorem 1]
.
The second bound in (
110
) follows from Doney
[
37
, Lemma 7]
, which tells us that

\mathcal{V}(y)\sim cy^{\alpha/2}
as
y\to\infty
, and from the fact that

\mathcal{V}
locally bounded on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
(because it is nondecreasing).

We finally show (
111
). Note that we may (and will)
assume that
y>2|x|
. It holds that

\check{\ell}(x+\mathbf{S})=\inf\{k\geq 1:x+U_{k}<0\}=\gamma_{T_{x_{1}}}
. We set

Z_{k}=\max_{i\in\{\gamma_{k-1},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\}}|\mathbf{S}_{i}-\mathbf{S}_{\gamma_{k-1}}|
and write

\displaystyle\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})=\max_{k\in\{1,\ldots,T_{x_{1}}\}}\max_{i\in\{\gamma_{k-1},\ldots,\gamma_{k}\}}|x+\mathbf{S}_{i}|\leq|x|+\max_{k\in\{1,\ldots,T_{x_{1}}\}}|\mathbf{S}_{\gamma_{k-1}}|+\max_{k\in\{1,\ldots,T_{x_{1}}\}}Z_{k}.

Since
\max_{k\in\{1,\ldots,T_{x_{1}}\}}|\mathbf{S}_{\gamma_{k-1}}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}|\mathbf{S}_{\gamma_{k}}-\mathbf{S}_{\gamma_{k-1}}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}Z_{k}
, we find

\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})\leq|x|+2\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}Z_{k}.

Thus, since
y>2|x|
,

\mathbb{P}(\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})>y)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}Z_{k}\geq\frac{y-|x|}{2}\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}Z_{k}\geq\frac{y}{4}\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}\Big(Z_{k}\land\frac{y}{4}\Big)\geq\frac{y}{4}\Big).

The trick used in the last inequality is borrowed from Denisov-Wachtel
[
69
]
.
Thus

\mathbb{P}(\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})>y)\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{T_{x_{1}}}\Big(\frac{4Z_{k}}{y}\land 1\Big)\Big]=\mathbb{E}[T_{x_{1}}]\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{4Z_{1}}{y}\land 1\Big]

by Wald’s identity: the sequence
(Z_{k})_{k\geq 1}
is i.i.d., with
Z_{k}
being
\mathcal{F}_{\gamma_{k}}
-measurable and

T_{x_{1}}
is an
(\mathcal{F}_{\gamma_{k}})_{k\geq 0}
-stopping time, because it is a hitting time of
(H_{k}=U_{\gamma_{k}})_{k\geq 0}
.
But we have seen that
\mathbb{E}[T_{x_{1}}]=\mathcal{W}(x_{1})\leq C(1+x_{1}^{\alpha/2})\leq C(1+|x|^{\alpha/2})
.
Moreover, recalling that
Z_{1}=\max_{i\in\{0,\dots,\gamma_{1}\}}|\mathbf{S}_{i}|
and that

\gamma_{1}=\check{\ell}(\mathbf{S})
,
we see that
Z_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\check{M}(\mathbf{S})
. Thus
\mathbb{P}(Z_{1}>z)\leq Cz^{-\alpha/2}
by (
112
) and

\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{4Z_{1}}{y}\land 1\Big]=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}\Big(Z_{1}\geq\frac{yz}{4}\Big)\mathrm{d}z\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{(yz)^{\alpha/2}}\leq\frac{C}{y^{\alpha/2}}.

We have shown that
\mathbb{P}(\check{M}(x+\mathbf{S})\geq y)\leq C(1+|x|^{\alpha/2})y^{-\alpha/2}
, which was our goal.
∎

We now deduce similar estimates for continuous-time random walks.

Lemma 68
.

Grant Assumption
18
and recall that
