y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
\mathcal{I}_{y}
stands for the set of linear isometries
sending
\mathbf{e}_{1}
to
\mathbf{n}_{y}
.

Lemma 77
.

Suppose Assumption
1
.

(i) If
d=2
, there exists a family
(A_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
such that

A_{y}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and such that
y\mapsto A_{y}
is globally Lipschitz continuous
on
\partial\mathcal{D}
.

(ii) If
d\geq 3
, for any
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, there exists a family
(A_{y}^{z})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
such that

A_{y}^{z}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
for all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and such that
y\mapsto A_{y}^{z}
is locally Lipschitz continuous
on
\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
.

Proof.

When
d=2
, we define
A_{y}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}

by
A_{y}\mathbf{e}_{1}=\mathbf{n}_{y}
and
A_{y}\mathbf{e}_{2}=-(\mathbf{n}_{y}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{2})\mathbf{e}_{1}+(\mathbf{n}_{y}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1})\mathbf{e}_{2}
.
The map
y\mapsto A_{y}
has the same regularity as
y\mapsto\mathbf{n}_{y}
,

i.e.

C^{2}
under Assumption
1
.

When
d\geq 3
, we fix
z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and consider a
C^{3}
-diffeomorphism
f:\mathbb{S}_{d-1}\to\partial\mathcal{D}

(e.g. the restriction
to
\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
of
\Phi^{-1}
defined in Lemma
78
). We set
p=f^{-1}(z)

and consider a
C^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism
g:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{S}_{d-1}\setminus\{p\}
.
Thus
h=f\circ g:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\to\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
is a
C^{3}
-diffeomorphism.
We consider the canonical basis
(\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\prime},\dots,\mathbf{e}^{\prime}_{d})
of
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
, and define, for

y\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
and
k=2,\dots,d
,

\mathbf{f}_{k}(y)=d_{h^{-1}(y)}h(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\prime})
. For each
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
,
the family
(\mathbf{f}_{2}(y),\dots,\mathbf{f}_{d}(y))
is a basis of the tangent space
T_{y}
at
y
to
\partial\mathcal{D}

and for each
k=2,\dots,d
, the map
y\to\mathbf{f}_{k}(y)
is of class
C^{2}
on
\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
.
We now use the Gram-Schmidt procedure
to get, for each
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
, an orthonormal basis
(\mathbf{g}_{2}(y),\dots,\mathbf{g}_{d}(y))
of
T_{y}
.
For each
k=2,\dots,d
, the map
y\to\mathbf{g}_{k}(y)
is still of class
C^{2}
on
\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
(because

(\mathbf{f}_{2}(y),\dots,\mathbf{f}_{d}(y))
are locally uniformly linearly independent for
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
).
We define

A_{y}^{z}
by
A_{y}^{z}\mathbf{e}_{1}=\mathbf{n}_{y}
and
A_{y}^{z}\mathbf{e}_{k}=\mathbf{g}_{k}(y)
for
k=2,\dots,d
. We have
A_{y}^{z}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
for each

y\in\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
, and
y\mapsto A_{y}^{z}
is of class
C^{2}
on
\partial\mathcal{D}\setminus\{z\}
.
Finally, we choose
A_{z}^{z}\in\mathcal{I}_{z}
arbitrarily.
∎

A.3
Some parameterization lemmas

The following lemma may be standard, but we found no precise reference.

Lemma 78
.

Grant Assumption
1
.
There is a
C^{3}
-diffeomorphism
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with both
D\Phi
and
D\Phi^{-1}
bounded on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
such that
\Phi(\mathcal{D})={B}_{d}(0,1)
.
Consequently, there is
\kappa>0

such that for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\kappa^{-1}|x-y|\leq|\Phi(x)-\Phi(y)|\leq\kappa|x-y|
.

Proof.

We assume without loss of generality that
0\in\mathcal{D}
and split the proof into three steps.

Step 1.
Let
r:\mathbb{S}_{d-1}\to(0,\infty)
be uniquely defined, for
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
, by
the fact that
r(\sigma)\sigma\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. We then have
\mathcal{D}=\{u\sigma:\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1},u\in[0,r(\sigma))\}
.
Let us show that
r
is of class
C^{3}
.

We fix
\sigma_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}

and set
x_{0}=r(\sigma_{0})\sigma_{0}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. Since
\partial\mathcal{D}
is of class
C^{3}
, there exists a neighborhood
U
of

x_{0}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a
C^{3}
function
F:U\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\nabla F
does not vanish and

\partial\mathcal{D}\cap U=\{u\in U:F(u)=0\}
.
Moreover,
\nabla F(x_{0})
is colinear to
\mathbf{n}_{x_{0}}
.
Let
\psi(t,\sigma)=F(t\sigma)
, which is defined and
C^{3}
on a neighborhood
V
of
(r(\sigma_{0}),\sigma_{0})
in

(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
. We have
\psi(r(\sigma_{0}),\sigma_{0})=F(x_{0})=0
and

\partial_{t}\psi(r(\sigma_{0}),\sigma_{0})=\sigma_{0}\cdot\nabla F(x_{0})\neq 0
(since
0\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
since
\nabla F(x_{0})
is colinear to
\mathbf{n}_{x_{0}}
and since for all
a\in\mathcal{D}
, all
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,

(a-y)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{y}>0
by convexity of
\mathcal{D}
).
By the implicit function theorem, there is a
C^{3}
function
g
, defined on a neighborhood
W
of
\sigma_{0}

in
\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
, such that
\psi(g(\sigma),\sigma)=0
(whence
g(\sigma)\sigma\in\partial\mathcal{D}
) for all
\sigma\in W
.
We have
g(\sigma)=r(\sigma)

for all
\sigma\in W
, so that
r
is
C^{3}
on
W
.

Step 2.
We introduce

r_{0}=\min_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}r,\quad r_{1}=\max_{\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}r,\quad\eta=\frac{r_{0}\land 1}{2},\quad m_{0}=\frac{r_{0}-\eta}{1-\eta}\quad\text{and}\quad m_{1}=\frac{r_{1}-\eta}{1-\eta}.

Consider some smooth

\varphi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to[0,1]
such that
\varphi(t)=0
for
t\in[0,\eta/2]\cup[2,\infty)
and such that

\int_{0}^{1}\varphi(t)\mathrm{d}t=1-\eta
. We set

\displaystyle q(t,m)=\int_{0}^{t}(1+\varphi(s)(m-1))\mathrm{d}s\geq 0\quad\text{ for $t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}$, $m\in[m_{0},m_{1}]$,}

\displaystyle{\bar{m}}(\rho)=\frac{\rho-\eta}{1-\eta}\in[m_{0},m_{1}]\quad\text{ for $\rho\in[r_{0},r_{1}]$.}

We have the following properties.

(i)
q(t,m)=t
for all
t\in[0,\eta/2]
, all
m\in[m_{0},m_{1}]
,

(ii)
q(1,m)=(1-\eta)m+\eta
for all
m\in[m_{0},m_{1}]
,

(iii)
q(1,{\bar{m}}(\rho))=\rho
for all
\rho\in[r_{0},r_{1}]
,

(iv)
\partial_{t}q(t,m)=1+\varphi(t)(m-1)\in[\eta,1+m_{1}]
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, all

m\in[m_{0},m_{1}]
,

(v)
\partial_{m}q(t,m)=\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s)\mathrm{d}s
,
so that
\partial_{m}q(t,m)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[m_{0},m_{1}]
,

(vi) for all
t\geq 0
, all
m\in[m_{0},m_{1}]
,
\eta t\leq q(t,m)\leq(1+m_{1})t
.

Points (i), (ii), (iii) and (v) are straightforward. To show that
1+\varphi(t)(m-1)\geq\eta

in (iv), we use that
m_{0}\geq\eta
. Point (vi) uses that by (i) and (iv), for every
t\geq 0
,
every
m\in[m_{0},m_{1}]
,

q(t,m)=q(0,m)+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}q(s,m)\mathrm{d}s\in[\eta t,(1+m_{1})t].

Step 3.
We now show that
H(x)=q(|x|,{\bar{m}}(r(\frac{x}{|x|})))\frac{x}{|x|}

is a
C^{3}
-diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
into
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with
DH
and
DH^{-1}
bounded
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and that
H(B_{d}(0,1))=\mathcal{D}
. Setting
\Phi=H^{-1}
will complete the proof.

By Step 1,
\mathcal{D}=\{u\sigma,\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1},u\in[0,r(\sigma))\}
.
For each
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
,
t\mapsto q(t,{\bar{m}}(r(\sigma)))
continuously increases
from
0
(at
t=0
) to
q(1,{\bar{m}}(r(\sigma)))=r(\sigma)
(at
t=1
) to
\infty
(at
t=\infty
).
Thus
H(B_{d}(0,1))=\mathcal{D}
and
H
is a bijection from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
into
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Since
H(x)=x
for all
x\in B_{d}(0,\eta/2)
, we only have to show that
H
is
C^{3}

on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\eta/4)
, with
DH
bounded from above and from below
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\eta/4)
.

First,

H
is of class
C^{3}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\eta/4)
, because
q
is smooth on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times[m_{0},m_{1}]
,

x\mapsto\frac{x}{|x|}
is smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\eta/4)
,
r
is
C^{3}
on
\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
and
{\bar{m}}

is smooth on
[m_{0},m_{1}]
.

For
x=t\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
and
t\geq\eta/4
, and for
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we write

y=a\sigma+b\tau
, with
\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
orthogonal to
\sigma
and
a^{2}+b^{2}=|y|^{2}
.
We find that

DH(t\sigma)(y)=[a\rho_{1}(t,\sigma)+b\rho_{2}(t,\sigma,\tau)]\sigma+b\rho_{3}(t,\sigma)\tau,

where
\rho_{1}(t,\sigma)=\partial_{t}q(t,{\bar{m}}(r(\sigma)))
,

\rho_{2}(t,\sigma,\tau)=\frac{\partial_{m}q(t,{\bar{m}}(r(\sigma)){\bar{m}}^{\prime}(r(\sigma))Dr(\sigma)(\tau)}{t}\quad\text{and}\quad\rho_{3}(t,\sigma)=\frac{q(t,{\bar{m}}(r(\sigma)))}{t}.

Since
\rho_{1}
,
\rho_{2}
,
\rho_{3}
are bounded (for
t>\eta/4
) by Steps 1 and 2, we conclude that

|DH(t\sigma)(y)|\leq C|y|
, showing that
DH
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\eta/4)
.

Next, we recall that
\rho_{1}\geq\eta
, that
\rho_{3}\geq\eta
, and call

A=\sup_{t\geq\eta/4,\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1},\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}}|\rho_{2}(t,\sigma,\tau)|
.
If first
|b|\leq\frac{|a|\eta}{2A}
, then

|DH(t\sigma)(y)|\geq|a\rho_{1}(t,\sigma)+b\rho_{2}(t,\sigma,\tau)|\geq|a|\eta-|b|A\geq\frac{|a|\eta}{2}=\frac{|a|\eta}{2}\lor(A|b|).

If next
b\geq\frac{|a|\eta}{2A}
, then

|DH(t\sigma)(y)|\geq|b\rho_{3}(t,\sigma)|\geq\eta|b|=(\eta|b|)\lor\frac{\eta^{2}|a|}{2A}).

Hence there is
c>0
such that in any case,
|DH(t\sigma)(y)|\geq c(|a|\lor|b|)\geq\frac{c}{2}|y|
.
This shows that
DH
is uniformly bounded from below on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{d}(0,\eta/4)
.
∎

The next lemma relies more deeply on Assumption
1
, see Remark
2
.

Lemma 79
.

Grant Assumption
1
.
For
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, let
\mathcal{D}_{x,A}={\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}\}}
.
There are
\varepsilon_{1}>0
,
\eta>0
,
C>0
such that the following properties hold true.

(i) For all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
there is a
C^{3}
function
\psi_{x,A}:B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that

\psi_{x,A}(0)=0
,
\nabla\psi_{x,A}(0)=0
and

\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})=\{u\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1}):u_{1}>\psi_{x,A}(u_{2},\dots,u_{d})\},

(124)

such that
{\rm Hess}\;\psi_{x,A}(v)\geq\eta I_{d-1}
and

|D^{2}\psi_{x,A}(v)|+|D^{3}\psi_{x,A}(v)|\leq C
for all
v\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
.

(ii) For all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, all
u\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
,

\frac{1}{2}|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{2},\dots,u_{d})|\leq d(u,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{2},\dots,u_{d})|.

(iii) For all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, all
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
, setting

\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}=|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||
,

|\psi_{x,A}(v)-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}|v|^{2}\qquad\hbox{for all $v\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})$.}

(125)

Proof.

We first prove (i) with
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{0}
defined in Remark
2
.
Observe that (
13
) and (
124
) are equivalent.
Hence by Remark
2
, a function
\psi_{x,A}
satisfying all the requirements of (i) exists for
a some
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
.
For another
B\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,B}
if and only if
A^{-1}By\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
so that

\psi_{x,B}
defined on
B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
by

\psi_{x,B}(u):=\psi_{x,A}(A^{-1}Bu)
(here we identify
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
to
\sum_{i=2}^{d}u_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i+1}\in\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\perp}

and observe that
A^{-1}B
is an isometry from
\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\perp}
into itself) is suitable.

Let us check (ii), for
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
small enough.
For
u=(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we use the shortened notation
u_{0}=(u_{2},\dots,u_{d})
.
For
u\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, it holds that
v:=(\psi_{x,A}(u_{0}),u_{0})\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, so that

d(u,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq|u-v|=|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|
. We next consider
\vartheta>0
such that

\sup_{x\in\partial\mathcal{D},A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}}\sup_{u\in B_{d-1}(0,\vartheta)}|\nabla\psi_{x,A}(u)|\leq 1
and set
\varepsilon_{1}=\frac{\vartheta\land\varepsilon_{0}}{2}
. We fix
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}

and
u\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})

and show that for all
v\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
|u-v|\geq\frac{1}{2}|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|
.

\bullet
If
v\notin B_{d}(0,2\varepsilon_{1})
, then
|u-v|\geq|v|-|u|\geq\varepsilon_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}(|u_{1}|+|\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|)\geq\frac{1}{2}|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|
.
We used that
|u_{1}|\leq\varepsilon_{1}
and
|\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|\leq\varepsilon_{1}

(since
\psi_{x,A}(0)=0
and
u_{0}\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
, on which
|\nabla\psi_{x,A}|
is bounded by
1
).

\bullet
If
v\in B_{d}(0,2\varepsilon_{1})
,
we write, using that
v_{1}=\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})
(because
v\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0})\cap\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
),

\displaystyle|u\!-\!v|\!\geq\!\frac{1}{2}(|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})|+|u_{0}-v_{0}|)\geq\frac{1}{2}(|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|-|\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|+|u_{0}-v_{0}|).

But
|\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|\leq|u_{0}-v_{0}|
because
u_{0},v_{0}\in B_{d-1}(0,\vartheta)
.
Hence
|u-v|\geq\frac{1}{2}|u_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(u_{0})|
.

Let us finally verify (iii) for
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
small enough.
By (i), there is
C>0
such that for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
, all
v\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,

|D^{2}\psi_{x,A}(v)|+|D^{3}\psi_{x,A}(v)|\leq C,\quad|D\psi_{x,A}(v)|\leq C|v|\quad\hbox{and}\quad|\psi_{x,A}(v)|\leq C|v|^{2}.

(126)

It is thus enough to show that there
are
\kappa\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)
,
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)
and
C>0
such that
|\psi_{x,A}(v)-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}|v|^{2}

when
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\kappa
and
v\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
:
when
|x-x^{\prime}|>\kappa
(and
v\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
), we simply write

|\psi_{x,A}(v)-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)|\leq 2C|v|^{2}\leq\frac{2C}{\kappa}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}|v|^{2}.

Thus, let us fix
\kappa\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)

and
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)
to be chosen later.

Fix
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that
|x-x^{\prime}|\leq\kappa
, as well as
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}

and observe that
y=A^{-1}(x^{\prime}-x)\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
(because
h_{x}(A,y)=x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and

|y|=|x^{\prime}-x|\leq\kappa<\varepsilon_{0}
).
By (
124
), there is
a\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
such that
y=(\psi_{x,A}(a),a_{1},\dots,a_{d-1})
.
Let us observe that

|x-x^{\prime}|=|y|\geq|a|.

The inward unit normal vector
\sigma
to
\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
at
y
is

\sigma=\frac{\mathbf{e}_{1}-\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}\partial_{i}\psi_{x,A}(a)\mathbf{e}_{i+1}}{r}\quad\hbox{where}\quad r=\sqrt{1+|\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)|^{2}}\quad\hbox{and we have}\quad\sigma=\Theta\mathbf{e}_{1},

with
\Theta:=A^{\prime}A^{-1}
.
Moreover, the tangent space
T
to
\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
at
y
is given by

T=\Big\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:u_{1}=\psi_{x,A}(a)+\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}\partial_{i}\psi_{x,A}(a)(u_{i+1}-a_{i})\Big\}.

We now fix
v\in B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{1})

and use the notation
{\bar{v}}=\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}v_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i+1}\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{1})
.

Figure 1:
Here
O=h_{x}^{-1}(A,x)
and
y=h_{x}^{-1}(A,x^{\prime})
.

Note that
y+\Theta{\bar{v}}\in T
, because

\Theta{\bar{v}}\cdot\sigma=\Theta{\bar{v}}\cdot\Theta\mathbf{e}_{1}={\bar{v}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=0
.
Then, the value of
\gamma:=\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)\geq 0
is determined by
the following fact: it is the smallest positive
\gamma
such that
z:=y+\Theta{\bar{v}}+\gamma\sigma\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
.
Observe that
\gamma=\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)\leq C|v|^{2}
by (
126
).
Note that we have

|z|\leq|y|+|{\bar{v}}|+\gamma\leq|x-x^{\prime}|+|v|+C|v|^{2}\leq\kappa+\varepsilon_{1}+C\varepsilon_{1}^{2},

and therefore,
z\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0})
if
\kappa
and
\varepsilon_{1}
are small enough. Thus, the condition

z\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
can be rewritten as
z\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=\psi_{x,A}(z\cdot\mathbf{e}_{2},\dots,z\cdot\mathbf{e}_{d})
.
In other words, since
y\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=\psi_{x,A}(a)
and
z\cdot\mathbf{e}_{i}=a_{i-1}
for
i=2,\dots,d
and since

\sigma\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=r^{-1}
and
\sigma\cdot\mathbf{e}_{i}=-r^{-1}\partial_{i-1}\psi_{x,A}(a)
for
i=2,\dots,d
,

\psi_{x,A}(a)+\Theta{\bar{v}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}+\frac{\gamma}{r}=\psi_{x,A}(b-\gamma c),

(127)

where
b_{i}=a_{i}+\Theta{\bar{v}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{i+1}
and
c_{i}=r^{-1}\partial_{i}\psi_{x,A}(a)

for
i=1,\dots,d-1
. Recalling the equation of
T
and that
y+\Theta{\bar{v}}\in T
, we get

\Theta{\bar{v}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{d-1}\partial_{i}\psi_{x,A}(a)\Theta{\bar{v}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{i+1}=(b-a)\cdot\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a).

This formula, inserted in (
127
), gives us

\frac{\gamma}{r}=\psi_{x,A}(b-\gamma c)-\psi_{x,A}(a)-(b-a)\cdot\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a).

(128)

Since
\gamma=\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)
, we conclude that

|\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v)-\psi_{x,A}(v)|=|r[\psi_{x,A}(b-\gamma c)-\psi_{x,A}(a)-(b-a)\cdot\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)]-\psi_{x,A}(v)|\leq I+J+K,

where

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle r|\psi_{x,A}(b-\gamma c)-\psi_{x,A}(b)|,

\displaystyle J=

\displaystyle(r-1)|\psi_{x,A}(b)-\psi_{x,A}(a)-(b-a)\cdot\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)|,

\displaystyle K=

\displaystyle|\psi_{x,A}(b)-\psi_{x,A}(a)-(b-a)\cdot\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)-\psi_{x,A}(v)|.

Observe that
1\leq r=\sqrt{1+|\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)|^{2}}\leq 1+|\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)|^{2}\leq 1+C|a|^{2}

by (
126
), whence
1\leq r\leq 1+C|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}\leq 1+C\kappa^{2}
, so that

r\in[1,2]
if
\kappa
is small enough.
Since
\gamma\leq C|v|^{2}
and
|c|\leq|\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)|\leq C|a|\leq C|x-x^{\prime}|
,

I\leq 2||D\psi_{x,A}||_{\infty}\gamma|c|\leq C|x-x^{\prime}||v|^{2}.

Next, since
r-1\leq C|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}\leq C|x-x^{\prime}|
and
|b-a|=|\bar{v}|=|v|
,

J\leq C|x-x^{\prime}|||D^{2}\psi_{x,A}||_{\infty}|b-a|^{2}\leq C|x-x^{\prime}||v|^{2}.

To treat
K
, we use
the Taylor formula to write, recalling that
\psi_{x,A}(0)=0
and
\nabla\psi_{x,A}(0)=0
,

\displaystyle\psi_{x,A}(b)-\psi_{x,A}(a)-(b-a)\cdot\nabla\psi_{x,A}(a)=(b-a)\cdot M(b-a)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\psi_{x,A}(v)=v\cdot M^{\prime}v,

\displaystyle\hbox{where}\quad M=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{Hess}\,\psi_{x,A}(a+t(b-a))(1-t)\mathrm{d}t\quad\hbox{and}\quad M^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{Hess}\,\psi_{x,A}(tv)(1-t)\mathrm{d}t.

Consequently,

K\leq||M|||b-a-v|(|b-a|+|v|)+||M-M^{\prime}|||v|^{2}\leq C|b-a-v||v|+||M-M^{\prime}|||v|^{2},

since
||M||\leq||D^{2}\psi_{x,A}||_{\infty}
and
|b-a|=|v|
. Moreover,

|b-a-v|=|\Theta{\bar{v}}-{\bar{v}}|\leq||\Theta-I|||{\bar{v}}|=||A-A^{\prime}|||v|,

because
||\Theta-I||=||A^{\prime}A^{-1}-I||=||A^{\prime}-A||
. Finally,

||M-M^{\prime}||\leq||D^{3}\psi_{x,A}||_{\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|a+t(b-a-v)|\leq C(|a|+|b-a-v|),

whence
||M-M^{\prime}||\leq C(|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||)
. All in all,

K\leq C(|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||)|v|^{2}
.
∎

A.4
Proof of the geometric inequalities

Our goal is to prove Proposition
39
. We recall that for
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

h_{x}(A,y)=x+Ay
,
\delta(y)=d(y,\partial\mathcal{D})
and

\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=\Lambda(h_{x}(A,y),h_{x}(A,z))
. When
h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
and
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,

\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=[h_{x}(A,y),h_{x}(A,z)]\cap\partial\mathcal{D}
.
We start with a consequence
of the Thales theorem.

Lemma 80
.

Consider a
C^{1}
open convex domain
\Sigma\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For all
y\in\Sigma
, all

z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\Sigma
, setting
a=[y,z]\cap\partial\Sigma
, we have

|a-z|\leq\frac{|y-z|d(z,\partial\Sigma)}{d(y,\partial\Sigma)}.

Proof.

Introduce the tangent space
P_{a}
to
\partial\Sigma
at
a
, denote by
b
(resp.
c
)
the orthogonal projection
of
y
(resp.
z
) on
P_{a}
. By the Thales theorem, we have

\frac{|a-z|}{|y-a|}=\frac{|c-z|}{|y-b|},\quad\text{{\it i.e.}}\quad|a-z|=\frac{|y-a||c-z|}{|y-b|}.

Figure 2:
Illustration of the proof of Lemma
80
.

But
|y-a|\leq|y-z|
,
|c-z|\leq d(z,\partial\Sigma)
and
|y-b|\geq d(y,\partial\Sigma)
.
∎

The next lemma is the main difficulty of the section.

Lemma 81
.

Grant Assumption
1
. There is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that
for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
, setting
\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}=|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||
,

(a) for all
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that both
h_{x}(A,y)
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)
belong to
\mathcal{D}
while
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}

and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathcal{D}
, if
|\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-h_{x}(A,y)|\leq|\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)|
, then

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|y|\land 1+|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\Big),

(b) for all
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
h_{x}(A,y)
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)
belong to
\mathcal{D}

but
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|y|\land 1+|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\Big).

Proof.

Let
D
be the diameter of
\mathcal{D}
and let
K=D\lor 1
.
We fix
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
.
We introduce
\mathcal{D}_{x,A}=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x}(A,u)\in\mathcal{D}\}
and
\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},u)\in\mathcal{D}\}

and we recall that for all
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\delta(h_{x}(A,u))=d(u,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})
.

Step 1.
Here we show that it is sufficient to prove that for all
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}
,
all
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, for some constant
M
depending only on
\mathcal{D}
, setting

a(x,y,v)=[y,v]\cap\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,

|a(x,y,v)-v|\leq M\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y||y-v|}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}.

(129)

Figure 3:
Illustration of (
129
).

Let us prove that this implies (a). For
y,z
as in the statement, we have
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}

and
z\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}^{c}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}^{c}
. We have

\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=h_{x}(A,a(x,y,z))=x+Aa(x,y,z)
and
\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)=x^{\prime}+A^{\prime}a(x^{\prime},y,z)
, so that

\displaystyle\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq

\displaystyle|Aa(x,y,z)-A^{\prime}a(x^{\prime},y,z)|\leq\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2},

where
\Delta_{1}=||A^{\prime}-A|||a(x,y,z)|
and
\Delta_{2}=|A^{\prime}a(x,y,z)-A^{\prime}a(x^{\prime},y,z)|
.
Since
|a(x,y,z)|\leq(|y|+|z|)\land D

(because
a(x,y,z)\in[y,z]
, because
0\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
and
a(x,y,z)\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
), we find

\Delta_{1}\leq||A-A^{\prime}||[(|y|+|z|)\land D]\leq K\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}(|y|\land 1+|z|\land 1).

Next, notice that the condition
|\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-h_{x}(A,y)|\leq|\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)|
rewrites

|a(x,y,z)-y|\leq|a(x^{\prime},y,z)-y|,

(130)

since
\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=h_{x}(A,a(x,y,z))
and since
h_{x}(A,\cdot)
is an isometry.
Introduce now
v=a(x^{\prime},y,z)\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}
, which satisfies
a(x^{\prime},y,v)=v=a(x^{\prime},y,z)
.
Since moreover
v\notin\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
by (
130
), (
129
) tells us that

\Delta_{2}=|a(x,y,z)-a(x^{\prime},y,z)|=|a(x,y,z)-v|\leq M\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y||y-v|}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}.

The conclusion follows, since
|y-v|\leq|z-y|\land D
, because

v\in[y,z]
and
v,y\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}
.

Let us next show that (
129
) implies (b). For
y,z
as in the statement, we have

y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}

and
z\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
. We have

\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=x+Aa(x,y,z)
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)=x^{\prime}+A^{\prime}z
, so that

\displaystyle\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq

\displaystyle|Aa(x,y,z)-A^{\prime}z|\leq\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{3},

where
\Delta_{1}=||A^{\prime}-A|||a(x,y,z)|
and
\Delta_{3}=|A^{\prime}a(x,y,z)-A^{\prime}z|
. Of course,

\Delta_{1}
is controlled as previously, while (
129
) gives
(we have
|y-z|=|y-z|\land D
because
y,z\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}
)

\Delta_{3}=|a(x,y,z)-z|\leq M\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y||y-z|}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\leq M\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land D)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}.

Step 2.
There is a constant
C
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, any
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}

such that
|y|\geq\varepsilon
and any

v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, (
129
) holds true with the constant
M=\frac{C}{\varepsilon}
.

Indeed, by Lemma
80
with the convex set
\Sigma=\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, with
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
and with

v\in D_{x,A}^{c}
,

|a(x,y,v)-v|\leq\frac{|y-v|d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\frac{|y||y-v|d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y||y-v|}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})},

where we finally used Lemma
35
-(ii): since
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
we have
d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
.

Step 3.
We next show that there exist
\varepsilon_{2}>0
and

h:(0,\varepsilon_{2}]\to(0,\infty)
with
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}h(\varepsilon)=0

such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2}]
,
there is a constant
C_{\varepsilon}
such that for all
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}

such that
|y|\leq\varepsilon
and all
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
such that
|v|\geq h(\varepsilon)
,
(
129
) holds true with the constant
M=C_{\varepsilon}
.

By Assumption
1
, there exist
a>\gamma>0
such that

U_{a}:=B_{d}(\mathbf{e}_{1}/a,1/a)\subset\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cup\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\subset B_{d}(\mathbf{e}_{1}/\gamma,1/\gamma)=:U_{\gamma},

and we e.g. assume that
a=1
. We fix
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}

such that
|y|\leq\varepsilon
and
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
such that
|v|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}
,
where
\varepsilon^{\prime}=h(\varepsilon)=\frac{8\varepsilon^{1/2}}{\gamma^{1/2}}
.
For all
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{2}-\varepsilon\geq\frac{\gamma(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{4},\quad\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{256}-2\varepsilon^{2}\geq\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{300}\quad\text{and}\quad\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon\geq\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}.

Actually the two first inequalities hold true for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, and
the second one uses that
8^{4}/256-2>8^{4}/300
and that
\gamma>1
.
We choose
\varepsilon_{2}>0
such that
\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\leq 1
and the above inequalities hold true
for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{2}]
.

Step 3.1.
We have
v_{1}\geq\gamma(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}/2
, because
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\subset{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0muU}_{\gamma}
, whence

|v-\mathbf{e}_{1}/\gamma|^{2}\leq 1/\gamma^{2}
,
i.e.

2v_{1}/\gamma\geq|v|^{2}\geq(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}
.
Moreover,
|v-y|\leq
diam
(\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}})\leq 2/\gamma
, so that

\frac{v_{1}-y_{1}}{|v-y|}\geq\frac{\gamma(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}/2-\varepsilon}{2/\gamma}\geq\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{8}.

Step 3.2.
Let
p=y+\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\frac{v-y}{|v-y|}
. We show here that

p\in[y,v]\cap U_{1}
and

d(p,\partial U_{1})\geq\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{600}
.

We write
\frac{v-y}{|v-y|}=\sin\theta\mathbf{e}_{1}+\cos\theta\mathbf{e}_{0}
, with
\mathbf{e}_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
orthogonal to

\mathbf{e}_{1}
and
\sin\theta\geq\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{8}
by Step 3.1.
We also write
y=y_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}+y_{0}\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}
,
for some
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}_{d-1}
orthogonal to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
, so that

\displaystyle|p-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}=

\displaystyle\Big(y_{1}+\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\sin\theta-1\Big)^{2}+\Big|y_{0}\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}+\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\cos\theta\mathbf{e}_{0}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle y_{1}^{2}+2y_{1}\Big(\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\sin\theta-1\Big)+\Big(\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\sin\theta-1\Big)^{2}+2y_{0}^{2}+2\Big(\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\cos\theta\Big)^{2}.

The second term is nonpositive and we can bound
y_{1}^{2}+2y_{0}^{2}\leq 2|y|^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}
, whence

\displaystyle|p-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}+1-2\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}\sin\theta+\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{256}+\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{128}.

Recalling that
\sin\theta\geq\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{8}
, we end with

|p-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2}\leq 1+2\varepsilon^{2}-\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{256}\leq 1-\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{300}.

This implies that
p\in U_{1}
and

d(p,\partial U_{1})=1-|p-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{2}(1-|p-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{2})\geq\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{600}
.

Finally, we have
p\in[y,v]
because
\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16|v-y|}\in[0,1]
, recall that
we are in the situation where
|v|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}
and
|y|\leq\varepsilon
, whence

|v-y|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon\geq\frac{\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{16}.

Step 3.3.
By Step 3.2 and since
U_{1}\subset\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
we have
a(x,y,v)=a(x,p,v)
, so that by Lemma
80
,

|a(x,y,v)-v|\leq\frac{|p-v|d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}{d(p,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}\leq\frac{|y-v|d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}{d(p,\partial U_{1})}.

By Lemma
35
-(ii), we have
d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
,
because
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
.
By Step 3.2,

d(p,\partial U_{1})\geq\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{600}\geq\frac{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}{600}\frac{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}{|y|},

because
d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq|y|
since
0\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
.
All this shows that, as desired,

|a(x,y,v)-v|\leq\frac{600C}{\gamma^{4}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{4}}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y||y-v|}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}.

Step 4.
We now fix
\varepsilon_{4}\in(0,1)
such that
\varepsilon_{4}\leq\varepsilon_{3}
and
h(\varepsilon_{4})\leq\varepsilon_{3}
, with

\varepsilon_{3}=\varepsilon_{1}\land\varepsilon_{2}\land 1\land\frac{\eta}{24C\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D},A\in\mathcal{I}_{x},A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}}

(131)

where
\varepsilon_{1}
,
\eta
and
C
were defined in Lemma
79
(
C
is the constant
appearing in (
125
)) and
\varepsilon_{2}
was defined in Step 3.
By Steps 2 and 3, we know that (
129
) holds true for any
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x,A}\cap\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}
and

v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
such that
|y|\geq\varepsilon_{4}
or (
|y|\leq\varepsilon_{4}
and
|v|\geq h(\varepsilon_{4})
),
and it only remains to study the case where
|y|<\varepsilon_{4}
and
|v|<h(\varepsilon_{4})
,
which implies that
|y|<\varepsilon_{3}
and
|v|<\varepsilon_{3}
. Since
\varepsilon_{3}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
,
we can use Lemma
79
. We introduce
y_{0}=(y_{2},\dots,y_{d})
and
v_{0}=(v_{2},\dots,v_{d})
.

Step 4.1.
If first
|v_{0}|^{2}\leq 2|y|
, then we use Lemma
80
as in Step 2 to write

|a(x,y,v)-v|\leq\frac{|y-v|d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}.

Since
v\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,
we have
v_{1}\in[\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v_{0}),\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})]
by (
124
), so that by
Lemma
79
,

d(v,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})\leq|v_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})|\leq|\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v_{0})-\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}|v_{0}|^{2}\leq 2C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}|y|,

whence

|a(x,y,v)-v|\leq 2C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y||y-v|}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}.

Step 4.2.
If next
|v_{0}|^{2}>2|y|
, we will show that

a(x,y,v)=ty+(1-t)v\quad\text{for some }t\in\Big(0,\frac{12C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{3}}{\eta}\Big].

(132)

Using moreover that
|y|=|y-0|\geq d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})
because
0\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
, this will imply (
129
), since

|a(x,y,v)-v|=t|y-v|\leq\frac{12C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{3}}{\eta}|y-v|\leq\frac{12C\varepsilon_{3}}{\eta}\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\frac{|y-v||y|}{d(y,\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A})}.

To prove (
132
), it is enough to verify that, setting
s=\frac{12C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{3}}{\eta}\in(0,1/2)

(by (
131
)),

sy_{1}+(1-s)v_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(sy_{0}+(1-s)v_{0})\geq 0.

Indeed,
a(x,y,v)=ty+(1-t)v
, with
t\in(0,1)
determined by the fact that
a(x,y,v)\in\partial\mathcal{D}_{x,A}
,

i.e.

ty_{1}+(1-t)v_{1}-\psi_{x,A}(ty_{0}+(1-t)v_{0})=0
.

But, as in Step 4.1,
v_{1}\in[\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v_{0}),\psi_{x,A}(v_{0})]
. Moreover,

y_{1}\geq\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(y_{0})
because
y\in\mathcal{D}_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}
.
It thus suffices to check that

r:=s\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(y_{0})+(1-s)\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v_{0})-\psi_{x,A}(sy_{0}+(1-s)v_{0})\geq 0.

(133)

By (
125
), we have

\displaystyle r\geq

\displaystyle s\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(y_{0})+(1-s)\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v_{0})-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(sy_{0}+(1-s)v_{0})-C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}|sy_{0}+(1-s)v_{0}|^{2}.

Since
|y_{0}|<\varepsilon_{3}\leq 1
and
|v_{0}|<\varepsilon_{3}
and since
2|y_{0}|<|v_{0}|^{2}
,
we have

|sy_{0}+(1-s)v_{0}|^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{3}(|y_{0}|+|v_{0}|)\leq\varepsilon_{3}(|v_{0}|^{2}/2+|v_{0}|)\leq\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon_{3}|v_{0}|.

Since Hess
\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}\geq\eta I_{d-1}
, we classically have

s\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(y_{0})+(1-s)\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(v_{0})-\psi_{x^{\prime},A^{\prime}}(sy_{0}+(1-s)v_{0})\geq\frac{\eta}{2}s(1-s)|v_{0}-y_{0}|.

Moreover,
|v_{0}-y_{0}|\geq|v_{0}|-|y_{0}|\geq|v_{0}|-\frac{|v_{0}|^{2}}{2}\geq\frac{|v_{0}|}{2}
.
All in all,

r\geq\frac{\eta}{4}s(1-s)|v_{0}|-\frac{3}{2}C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{3}|v_{0}|\geq\frac{\eta}{8}s|v_{0}|-\frac{3}{2}C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{3}|v_{0}|,

because
s\in[0,1/2]
as already seen. This last quantity equals
0
by definition of
s
.
∎

We can finally handle the

Proof of Proposition
39
.

We fix
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
.

For (i), assume that
h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
and

h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathcal{D}
. Since we either have

|\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-h_{x}(A,y)|\leq|\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)|
or the converse inequality,
we deduce from Lemma
81
-(a) that

\displaystyle\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)\!-\!\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|\!-\!|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|y|\land 1+|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))}\Big).

For the second inequality, note that

|y|\geq\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))
(since
|y|={|h_{x}(A,y)-x|}\geq\delta(h_{x}(A,y))

and
|y|=|h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)-x^{\prime}|\geq\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))
because
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
) and write

|y|\land 1\leq|z|\land 1+|y-z|\land 1\leq|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))}.

For (ii), assume that
h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
and

h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\in\mathcal{D}
. By Lemma
81
-(b),

\displaystyle\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)\!-\!h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)|\!-\!|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|y|\land 1+|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}\Big).

We used that
|y|\geq\delta(h_{x}(A,y))
, whence
|y|\land 1\leq|z|\land 1+|y-z|\land 1\leq|z|\land 1+\frac{|y|(|y-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))}
.

For (iii), we assume that
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathcal{D}
. Consider
\varepsilon>0

small enough so that
h_{x}(A,\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1})\in\mathcal{D}
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1})\in\mathcal{D}
. Then we can apply (i)
to find

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1},z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1},z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|z|\land 1+\frac{|\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}|(|\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}-z|\land 1)}{\delta(h_{x}(A,\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}))}\Big).

But if
\varepsilon>0
is small enough, we have
\delta(h_{x}(A,\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}))=d(x+\varepsilon\mathbf{n}_{x},\partial\mathcal{D})=\varepsilon
, recall that

A\mathbf{e}_{1}=\mathbf{n}_{x}
, and
\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}))=\varepsilon
. We end with

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1},z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1},z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}\Big(|z|\land 1+|\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{1}-z|\land 1)\leq 2C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}.

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
, we find

||\bar{g}_{x}(A,0,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},0,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}||\leq 2C\rho_{x,x^{\prime},A,A^{\prime}}
as desired.

Point (iv) is obvious, since
\bar{g}_{x}(A,0,z)
,
\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},0,z)
,
x
,
x^{\prime}
belong to

{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
, which is bounded.
∎

We conclude this appendix with the following strange observation: we could treat the
flat case
\mathcal{D}=\mathbb{H}
(with some small difficulties since
\mathbb{H}
is not bounded, but with many
huge simplifications), but we could not treat, with our method,
general convex domains that are not strongly convex.

Remark 82
.

(a) The following version of Lemma
81
-(a) holds true in the flat case where
\mathcal{D}=\mathbb{H}
:
for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathbb{H}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
,
all
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
y\in\mathbb{H}
and
z\notin{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathbb{H}}
,
(so that
h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathbb{H}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)\in\mathbb{H}
,
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathbb{H}
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathbb{H}
),

\Big||\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\Big|\leq||A-A^{\prime}||(|y|+|z|).

(b) Consider a (non-strongly) convex open bounded domain
\mathcal{D}
of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that

\mathcal{D}\cap B_{2}(0,1)=\{u=(u_{1},u_{2})\in B_{2}(0,1):u_{1}>u_{2}^{4}\}.

There does not exist any constant
M\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
A\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
,
all
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
, all
y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that

h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}
,
h_{x}(A,z)\notin\mathcal{D}
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)\notin\mathcal{D}
,

|\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\!\leq\!M(|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||)\Big(|y|+|z|+\frac{|y||y-z|}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))}\Big).

Proof.

For point (a), it suffices to note that
\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=x+A\bar{g}_{0}(I,y,z)
, where
I
is the identity matrix,
and the result follows from
the fact that
|\bar{g}_{0}(I,y,z)|\leq|y|+|z|
, because
\bar{g}_{0}(I,y,z)=[y,z]\cap\partial\mathbb{H}\in[y,z]
.

For point (b), we choose
x=(0,0)
,
x^{\prime}=(a^{4},a)
,
y=(\alpha,0)
and
z=(\alpha,-\alpha^{1/4})
,
with
a>0
small and
\alpha=\frac{a^{4}(6a^{2}-4a^{3}+a^{4})}{\sqrt{1+16a^{2}}}>0
. We have
x,x^{\prime},z\in\partial\mathcal{D}
.
We naturally choose
A=I\in\mathcal{I}_{x}
(because
\mathbf{n}_{x}=\mathbf{e}_{1}
) and
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{x^{\prime}}
defined by

A^{\prime}(u_{1},u_{2})=u_{1}\sigma+u_{2}\tau
, where

\tau=\frac{(4a^{3},1)}{\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma=\frac{(1,-4a^{3})}{\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}}

are the tangent and normal unit vectors to
\partial\mathcal{D}
at
x^{\prime}
(we have
\sigma=\mathbf{n}_{x^{\prime}}
).
For all
u=(u_{1},u_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

h_{x}(u)=u
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(u)=x^{\prime}+u_{1}\sigma+u_{2}\tau
.

Figure 4:
Illustration of Remark
82
-(b).

Moreover, since
a>0
is small,
y=h_{x}(A,y)\in\mathcal{D}
and
h_{x^{\prime}}(A,y)=x^{\prime}+\alpha\sigma\in\mathcal{D}
.
Observe also that
h_{x^{\prime}}(A,z)=x^{\prime}+\alpha\sigma-\alpha^{1/4}\tau.

We have

\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)=z
, and it holds that
\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)=((a-a^{2})^{4},a-a^{2})
:
it suffices that there exists
\theta\in(0,1)
such that

((a-a^{2})^{4},a-a^{2})=h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y)+\theta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},z)-h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))
,

i.e.

((a-a^{2})^{4},a-a^{2})=x^{\prime}+\alpha\sigma-\theta\alpha^{1/4}\tau
, which rewrites

(a-a^{2})^{4}=a^{4}+\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}}-\frac{4\theta\alpha^{1/4}a^{3}}{\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}}\quad\text{and}\quad a-a^{2}=a-\frac{4\alpha a^{3}}{\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}}-\frac{\theta\alpha^{1/4}}{\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}}.

A computation shows that
(the second equality uses the definition of
\alpha
)

\theta=\frac{a^{2}\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}-4\alpha a^{3}}{\alpha^{1/4}}=\frac{\alpha+\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}(a^{4}-(a-a^{2})^{4})}{4\alpha^{1/4}a^{3}}

is suitable, and indeed
\theta\in(0,1)
if
a>0
is small because
\alpha\sim 6a^{6}
as
a\to 0
.

One then observes that, as
a\to 0
,

|\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|\geq|a-a^{2}+\alpha^{1/4}|=a+6^{1/4}a^{3/2}+o(a^{3/2}),

while
|x-x^{\prime}|=\sqrt{a^{8}+a^{2}}=a+o(a^{6})
, so that

|\bar{g}_{x}(A,y,z)-\bar{g}_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y,z)|-|x-x^{\prime}|\geq 6^{1/4}a^{3/2}+o(a^{3/2}).

Next, for all
a>0
small, we have

||A-A^{\prime}||\leq|\sigma-\mathbf{e}_{1}|+|\tau-\mathbf{e}_{2}|\leq 2\Big(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+16a^{2}}}+\frac{4a^{3}}{\sqrt{1+16a^{2}}}\Big)\leq 2(\sqrt{1+16a^{6}}-1+4a^{3}),

so that
||A-A^{\prime}||\leq 8a^{3}+o(a^{3})
. Moreover,

|y|=\alpha\leq 6a^{6}
,
|z|\leq 2\alpha^{1/4}\leq 4a^{3/2}
and
|y-z|\leq|y|+|z|\leq 10a^{3/2}
.
Also, we have
\delta(h_{x}(A,y))=\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))=|y|
.
Thus, for
a>0
small,

\displaystyle(|x-x^{\prime}|+||A-A^{\prime}||)\Big(|y|+|z|+\frac{|y||y-z|}{\delta(h_{x}(A,y))\land\delta(h_{x^{\prime}}(A^{\prime},y))}\Big)\leq

\displaystyle(a+o(a))(14a^{3/2}+o(a^{3/2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 14a^{5/2}+o(a^{5/2}).

Hence if there would exist
M
as in the statement, we would have, for all
a>0
small,

6^{1/4}a^{3/2}+o(a^{3/2})\leq M(14a^{5/2}+o(a^{5/2}))
,
which is not possible.
∎

Appendix B
About test functions

The goal of this appendix is to prove Remarks
13
and
17
, as well as the following
result.

Remark 83
.

Recall that
\mathcal{L}\varphi(x)
was introduced in Definition
12
for
\varphi\in C({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})\cap C^{2}(\mathcal{D})
and

x\in\mathcal{D}
. It is well-defined and continuous on
\mathcal{D}
. Moreover, we have

\mathcal{L}\varphi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)-z\cdot\nabla\varphi(x){\bm{1}}_{\{|z|<a\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},\qquad x\in\mathcal{D},

(134)

for any
a\in(0,\infty)
and we can choose
a=0
when
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
a=\infty
when
\alpha\in(1,2)
.

Proof.

We recall that
\Lambda
is continuous by Lemma
76
and write

\mathcal{L}\varphi=\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi+\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi+\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|\geq 1\}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1\}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)-(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1\}}(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}.

Since
\varphi\in C({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
,
\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi
is
well-defined and continuous on
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}
.
For
\varepsilon>0
, we introduce the set

\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}=\{x\in\mathcal{D},\>d(x,\partial\mathcal{D})\geq\varepsilon\}
.
Since
\varphi\in C^{2}(\mathcal{D})
, there is a constant
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

|\varphi(y)-\varphi(x)-(y-x)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)|\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y-x|^{2}
for any
x,y\in\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}
.
Therefore, for any
x\in\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}
and any
|z|\leq 1
, we have

\big|\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)-(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)\big|\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}|z|^{2}{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|\leq\varepsilon/2\}}+\tilde{C}_{\varepsilon}{\bm{1}}_{\{\varepsilon/2\leq|z|\leq 1\}},

where
\tilde{C}_{\varepsilon}=2\sup_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}|\varphi(x)|+\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla\varphi(x)|
.
We used that
|\Lambda(x,x+z)-x|\leq|z|\leq 1
. This bound is integrable with respect to

|z|^{-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z
so that we conclude by dominated convergence
that
\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi
is well-defined and continuous on
\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}
, for any
\varepsilon>0
.
Finally
observe that
\Lambda(x,x+z)=x+z
if
|z|\leq d(x,\partial\mathcal{D})
, so that for all
x\in\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}
, all
|z|\leq 1
,

|(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)|\leq{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|>\varepsilon\}}\sup_{x\in{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}}|\varphi(x)|.

This bound being integrable with respect to
|z|^{-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z
, we conclude
that
\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi
is well-defined and continuous on
\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}
for all
\varepsilon>0
.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}z{\bm{1}}_{\{a<|z|<1\}}|z|^{-d-\alpha}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}z{\bm{1}}_{\{1<|z|<b\}}|z|^{-d-\alpha}=0

for any
a<1<b
, (
134
) holds for any
a\in(0,\infty)
.
When
\alpha\in(1,2)
,
\int_{\{|z|>1\}}|z|^{1-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z<\infty

and
\int_{\{|z|>1\}}z|z|^{d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z=0
so that (
134
) holds with

a=\infty
. When
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we have

\int_{\{|z|<1\}}|z|^{1-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z<\infty
and
\int_{\{|z|<1\}}z|z|^{d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z=0

so that (
134
) holds with
a=0
.
∎

Proof of Remark
13
.

We start with (a): we fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1-\alpha]

and assume that
\varphi\in C^{2}(\mathcal{D})\cap C^{\alpha+\varepsilon}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
,
so that for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
, all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, using that
\Lambda(x,x+z)\in[x,x+z]
,

|\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)|\leq C(|\Lambda(x,x+z)-x|^{\alpha+\varepsilon}\land 1)\leq C(|z|^{\alpha+\varepsilon}\land 1).

Using (
134
) with
a=0
, we conclude that
\mathcal{L}\varphi
is bounded on
\mathcal{D}
as desired.

We carry on with (b): we fix
\alpha\in[1,2)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,2-\alpha]

and assume that
\varphi\in C^{2}(\mathcal{D})\cap C^{\alpha+\varepsilon}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
, with
\nabla\varphi(x)\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}=0
for
all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. We have to prove that
\mathcal{L}\varphi
is bounded on
\mathcal{D}
.
We use (
134
) with
a=\varepsilon_{0}/2
,
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
being defined in Remark
2
:
we write

\mathcal{L}\varphi(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{4}\mathcal{L}_{k}\varphi(x)
, where,
introducing
{\tilde{x}}\in\partial\mathcal{D}
such that
d(x,\partial\mathcal{D})=|x-{\tilde{x}}|
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|\geq\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)-(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z)\cdot(\nabla\varphi(x)-\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}}))\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{4}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

Since
\varphi
is bounded,
\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi
is obviously bounded.

Since
\varphi\in C^{\alpha+\varepsilon}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
,
there is
C>0
such that for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
, all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

|\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)-(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x)|\leq C|\Lambda(x,x+z)-x|^{\alpha+\varepsilon}\leq C|z|^{\alpha+\varepsilon},

because
\Lambda(x,x+z)\in[x,x+z]
. Hence
\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi
is bounded.

Using that

|\nabla\varphi(x)-\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})|\leq C|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{\alpha+\varepsilon-1}
, that

|\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z|\leq z
and that
\Lambda(x,x+z)=x+z

if
|z|<d(x,\partial\mathcal{D})=|x-{\tilde{x}}|
, we see that when
\alpha\in(1,2)
,

|\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x)|\leq C|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{\alpha+\varepsilon-1}\int_{\{|z|\geq|x-{\tilde{x}}|\}}\frac{|z|\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}=C|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{\alpha+\varepsilon-1}|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{1-\alpha}=C|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{\varepsilon},

which is bounded since
\mathcal{D}
is bounded. When
\alpha=1
, we write

|\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x)|\leq C|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{\varepsilon}\int_{\{|x-{\tilde{x}}|\leq|z|\leq\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}\frac{|z|\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+1}}\leq C|x-{\tilde{x}}|^{\varepsilon}|\log|x-{\tilde{x}}||\leq C.

We now study
\mathcal{L}_{4}
. If first
|x-{\tilde{x}}|\geq\varepsilon_{0}/2
,
then
x+z\in\mathcal{D}
for all
z\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)
, whence
\mathcal{L}_{4}\varphi(x)=0
.
We next treat the case where
|x-{\tilde{x}}|<\varepsilon_{0}/2
. Using a suitable isometry, we may assume that

{\tilde{x}}=0
and
\mathbf{n}_{\tilde{x}}=\mathbf{e}_{1}
(whence
0\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,

\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{H}
and
\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=0
). Necessarily,
x-{\tilde{x}}
is colinear to

\mathbf{n}_{\tilde{x}}
so that
x=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}
, with
x_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)
(because
0<|x-{\tilde{x}}|<\varepsilon_{0}/2
).
Using that
\Lambda(x,x+z)=x+z
if
x+z\in\mathcal{D}
, write

\mathcal{L}_{4}\varphi(x)=\mathcal{L}_{41}\varphi(x)+\mathcal{L}_{42}\varphi(x)+\mathcal{L}_{43}\varphi(x)
,
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{41}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{x+z\in\mathbb{H}\setminus\mathcal{D}\}}(\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{42}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{x+z\notin\mathbb{H}\}}(\Lambda(x,x+z)-\Gamma(x,z))\cdot\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{43}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{x+z\notin\mathbb{H}\}}(\Gamma(x,z)-x-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

and where, for
z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
x+z\notin\mathbb{H}
, we have set

\Gamma(x,z)=-\frac{x_{1}}{z_{1}}\sum_{i=2}^{d}z_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}.

By Remark
2
(with
x=0
and
A=I
),

\mathcal{D}\cap B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0})=\{u\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0}):u_{1}>\psi(u_{2},\dots,u_{d})\}
,
for some
C^{3}
convex function
\psi:B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{0})\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

such that
\psi(0)=0
,
\nabla\psi(0)=0
, and
||D^{2}\psi||_{\infty}\leq C
(with
C
not depending on
{\tilde{x}}
).
This implies that for
|z|<\varepsilon_{0}/2
(recall that
x=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}
with
x_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)
),
x+z\in\mathbb{H}\setminus\mathcal{D}

if and only if
x_{1}+z_{1}\in(0,\psi(z_{2},\dots,z_{d})]
. Thus, bounding roughly
|\Lambda(x,x+z)-x-z|\leq|z|
,
writing
z=(z_{1},v)
and using that
|z|\geq|v|
,

|\mathcal{L}_{41}\varphi(x)|\leq||\nabla\varphi||_{\infty}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)}\!\!\mathrm{d}v\int_{-x_{1}}^{\psi(v)-x_{1}}\!\!|z|^{1-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq||\nabla\varphi||_{\infty}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)}\!\!|v|^{1-d-\alpha}\psi(v)\mathrm{d}v.

Since
\psi(v)\leq C|v|^{2}
and since
3-d-\alpha>-(d-1)
, we conclude that
\mathcal{L}_{41}\varphi
is bounded.

Since
\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}=0
,
we may write
\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})=\sum_{i=2}^{d}\rho_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}
.
Moreover, we also have
\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})\cdot x=0
so that

(\Gamma(x,z)-x-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi({\tilde{x}})=-(\frac{x_{1}}{z_{1}}+1)\sum_{i=2}^{d}\rho_{i}z_{i}
,
and we find that

\mathcal{L}_{43}\varphi(x)=-\sum_{i=2}^{d}\rho_{i}\int_{\{z\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2)\>:\>x+z\notin\mathbb{H}\}}\Big(\frac{x_{1}}{z_{1}}+1\Big)z_{i}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}=0

by a symmetry argument,
using the substitution
(z_{1},z_{2},\dots,z_{d})\mapsto(z_{1},-z_{2},\dots,-z_{d})
, which leaves invariant the
set
\{z\in B_{d}(0,\varepsilon_{0}/2):x+z\notin\mathbb{H}\}
(recall that
x=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}
).

Next, assume for a moment that for some constant
K>0
,

\text{for all $z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$ such that $x+z\notin\mathbb{H}$,}\quad|\Gamma(x,z)-\Lambda(x,x+z)|\leq K\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{|z_{1}|^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|z_{i}|^{3}.

(135)

Recalling that
x+z\notin\mathbb{H}
if and only if
x_{1}+z_{1}\leq 0
and that
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{42}\varphi(x)|\leq

\displaystyle K||\nabla\varphi||_{\infty}x_{1}^{2}\int_{-1}^{-x_{1}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,1)}\Big(1+\frac{|z_{2}|^{3}+\cdot+|z_{d}|^{3}}{|z_{1}|^{3}}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}\cdots\mathrm{d}z_{d}}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K^{\prime}x_{1}^{2}\int_{-1}^{-x_{1}}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,1)}\Big(1+\frac{|z_{2}|^{3}}{|z_{1}|^{3}}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}\cdots\mathrm{d}z_{d}}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K^{\prime}x_{1}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{1}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(1+\frac{z_{2}^{3}}{z_{1}^{3}}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}+z_{2})^{2+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K^{\prime}x_{1}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{1}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\Big[\int_{0}^{z_{1}}\Big(1+\frac{z_{2}^{3}}{z_{1}^{3}}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{z_{1}^{2+\alpha}}+\int_{z_{1}}^{1}\Big(1+\frac{z_{2}^{3}}{z_{1}^{3}}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{z_{2}^{2+\alpha}}\Big],

for some constant
K^{\prime}
, allowed to vary from line to line.
Using that
\alpha\in[1,2)
, we conclude that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{42}\varphi(x)|\leq K^{\prime}x_{1}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{1}(z_{1}^{-1-\alpha}+z_{1}^{-3})\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq K^{\prime}x_{1}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{1}z_{1}^{-3}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq K^{\prime}.

It remains to check (
135
). We set
\gamma=\Gamma(x,z)
and observe that

\gamma=x+\theta_{1}z
, where

\theta_{1}=\frac{x_{1}}{|z_{1}|}.

Indeed, recall that
z_{1}<-x_{1}<0
(since
x+z\notin\mathbb{H}
), that
x=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}
, and note that
x+\theta_{1}z=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}-\frac{x_{1}}{z_{1}}z=-\frac{x_{1}}{z_{1}}\sum_{i=2}^{d}z_{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}
.
We then set
\lambda=\Lambda(x,x+z)=x+\theta z
, with
\theta\in(0,1)
determined by the fact that

\lambda\in\partial\mathcal{D}
. It holds that
\theta\leq\theta_{1}
: it suffices to prove that
\gamma\notin\mathcal{D}
,
which is obvious since
\gamma\notin\mathbb{H}
(because
\gamma_{1}=0
).
We also have
\theta\geq\theta_{0}
, where,
for
C
the constant such that
\psi(v)\leq C|v|^{2}
,

\theta_{0}=\Big(1-\frac{Cx_{1}|z|^{2}}{|z_{1}|^{2}}\Big)\theta_{1}.

Indeed, it suffices to treat the case where
\theta_{0}>0
and to check that

\lambda^{0}:=x+\theta_{0}z\in\mathcal{D}
,
i.e.
that

\lambda^{0}_{1}\geq\psi(\lambda^{0}_{2},\dots,\lambda^{0}_{d})
. To this end, it suffices to note that

\lambda^{0}_{1}=x_{1}+\frac{x_{1}}{|z_{1}|}\Big(1-\frac{Cx_{1}|z|^{2}}{|z_{1}|^{2}}\Big)z_{1}=\frac{Cx_{1}^{2}|z|^{2}}{|z_{1}|^{2}},

while, recalling that
x=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}
and that
\theta_{0}\leq\theta_{1}
(since we are in the case
\theta_{0}>0
),

\psi(\lambda^{0}_{2},\dots,\lambda^{0}_{d})\leq C\sum_{i=2}^{d}(\lambda_{i}^{0})^{2}=C\theta_{0}^{2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}z_{i}^{2}\leq C\theta_{0}^{2}|z|^{2}\leq C\theta_{1}^{2}|z|^{2}=C\frac{x_{1}^{2}|z|^{2}}{z_{1}^{2}}.

We have shown that
\Gamma(x,z)=x+\theta_{1}z
and
\Lambda(x,x+z)=x+\theta z
with
\theta\in[\theta_{0},\theta_{1}]
,
so that

|\Gamma(x,z)-\Lambda(x,x+z)|\leq(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0})||z|=\frac{Cx_{1}^{2}|z|^{3}}{|z_{1}|^{3}},

from which (
135
) follows.
∎

Proof of Remark
17
.

Point (a) relies on a rather noticeable observation, see Step 8,
while points (b) and (c) are not difficult once (a) is checked (because
\mathcal{D}=B_{d}(0,1)
).

Step 1.
Let
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
and
\varphi\in C^{\alpha/2}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
be radially symmetric
and satisfy
\varphi|_{\partial\mathcal{D}}=0
. Then

\varphi\in H_{\beta}
if and only if
\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi(\mathbf{e}_{1})=0
, where

\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi(\mathbf{e}_{1}):=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\varphi(y)\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d+\beta}}.

Indeed, using that
\varphi|_{\partial\mathcal{D}}=0
and the symmetry of
\varphi
and of the domain
\mathcal{D}=B_{d}(0,1)

we see that

\text{for all $x\in\partial\mathcal{D}$, all $\varepsilon>0$,}\quad\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\varepsilon}\varphi(x)=\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\varepsilon}\varphi(\mathbf{e}_{1})=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\varphi(y){\bm{1}}_{\{|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|\geq\varepsilon\}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{d+\beta}},

which converges to
\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi(\mathbf{e}_{1})
, since
|\varphi(y)|=|\varphi(y)-\varphi(\mathbf{e}_{1})|\leq C|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{\alpha/2}
and since
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
.

Step 2.
Let
\varphi_{1}\in C({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
such that

\varphi_{1}(x)=[d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})]^{\alpha/2}
when
d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})\leq 1/2

and let
\varphi_{2}\in C_{c}(\mathcal{D})
. Then
\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}\notin H_{*}
.
Indeed, for any
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and for
h=(h_{1},0)\in B_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1},1)
with
h_{1}>0
small enough, we have,
since
\mathbf{n}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}}=-\mathbf{e}_{1}
,

\mathcal{H}_{*}\varphi_{0}(x,h)=\mathcal{H}_{*}\varphi_{0}(\mathbf{e}_{1},h)=h_{1}^{-\alpha/2}[d((1-h_{1})\mathbf{e}_{1},D^{c})]^{\alpha/2}=1.

Hence
\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{h\in B_{2}(0,\varepsilon)\cap B_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1},1)}\mathcal{H}_{*}\varphi_{0}(x,h)\geq 1\neq 0
.

Step 3.
For any
\varphi\in C^{1}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
, we have
\varphi\in H_{*}
, because

|\mathcal{H}_{*}\varphi(x,h)|\leq|h|^{1-\alpha/2}||\nabla\varphi||_{\infty}
for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
h\in B_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1},1)
.

Step 4.
Point (b) follows from Step 1, since
\varphi_{1}-a\varphi_{2}\in C^{\alpha/2}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
for any
a>0
,
and since choosing
a=\frac{\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}{\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}
, we find

\mathcal{H}_{\beta}(\varphi_{1}-a\varphi_{2})(\mathbf{e}_{1})=0
.

Step 5.
Assuming that (a) holds true, we show (c) when
\beta\in(0,\alpha/2)
and
\beta^{\prime}=*
.
Consider some nonnegative radially symmetric

\varphi_{1}\in C^{2}(\mathcal{D})
such that

\varphi_{1}(x)=[d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})]^{\alpha/2}
as soon as
d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})\leq 1/2
.
Then
\varphi_{1}\in D_{\alpha}
by (a) and
\varphi_{1}\in C^{\alpha/2}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
.
Let now
\varphi_{2}\in C^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{D})
be radially symmetric,
nonnegative and positive on
B_{d}(0,1/2)
. By Remark
13
,
\varphi_{2}\in D_{\alpha}
.
Thus
\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{1}-a\varphi_{2}\in D_{\alpha}
for all
a>0
.
By (b), there is
a>0
such that
\varphi_{0}\in H_{\beta}
.
But
\varphi_{0}\notin H_{*}
by Step 2 and since
\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{1}
near the boundary.

Step 6.
We now show (c) when
\beta=*
and
\beta^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha/2)
. We consider some nonnegative
radially symmetric
\varphi\in C^{2}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
such that

\varphi(x)=[d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})]^{2}
as soon as
d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})\leq 1/2
.
Then
\nabla\varphi(x)=0
for all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, so that
\varphi\in D_{\alpha}
by Remark
13
.
Moreover,
\varphi\in C^{1}({}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}})
, whence
\varphi\in H_{*}
by Step 3.
But
\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi(\mathbf{e}_{1})>0
, so that
\varphi\notin H_{\beta^{\prime}}
by Step 1.

Step 7.
We next prove (c) when
0<\beta<\beta^{\prime}<\alpha/2
(a similar argument works
when
{0<\beta^{\prime}<\beta<\alpha/2}
). Consider some radially symmetric probability density
\varphi_{1}\in C^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{D})

such that
\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})>1
. Such a function
\varphi_{1}

is easily built, since
\int_{\mathcal{D}}|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{-d-\beta}\mathrm{d}y=\infty
. By the Jensen inequality,

\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})\geq[\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})]^{(d+\beta^{\prime})/(d+\beta)}>\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})
.
Let
\eta=\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})-\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})
and let
\varphi_{2}\in C^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{D})

be radially symmetric and so that

|\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})-1|+|\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})-1|\leq\frac{1}{2}\land\frac{\eta}{4\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}
.
Such a function
is easily built, since
\int_{\mathcal{D}}|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{-d-\beta}\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}y)=\int_{\mathcal{D}}|y-\mathbf{e}_{1}|^{-d-\beta^{\prime}}\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}y)=1
. Consider now

a=\frac{\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}{\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}
,
so that
\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{0}(\mathbf{e}_{1})=0
, where

\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{1}-a\varphi_{2}\in H_{\beta}
by Step 1. Also,
\varphi_{0}\in C^{2}_{c}(\mathcal{D})
,
so that
\varphi_{0}\in D_{\alpha}
by Remark
13
. But
\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})\geq 1/2
, so that

a\leq 2\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})
, and
|\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})-\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})|\leq\frac{\eta}{4\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}
, whence

\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{0}(\mathbf{e}_{1})=\eta+\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})-a\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})=\eta+a(\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1})-\mathcal{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}\varphi_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1}))\geq\eta-\frac{a\eta}{4\mathcal{H}_{\beta}\varphi_{1}(\mathbf{e}_{1})}\geq\frac{\eta}{2}.

Thus
\varphi_{0}\notin H_{\beta^{\prime}}
by Step 1.

Step 8.
For
\psi\in C^{2}((0,\infty))\cap C([0,\infty))
and
a\in(0,\infty)
, we set

\mathcal{K}\psi(a)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[\psi((a+b)_{+})-\psi(a)-\psi^{\prime}(a)b{\bm{1}}_{\{|b|<1\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{1+\alpha}}.

Setting
\psi_{*}(a)=a^{\alpha/2}
, we have
\mathcal{K}\psi_{*}(a)=0
for all
a\in(0,\infty)
.

When
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we have

\mathcal{K}\psi_{*}(a)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(a+b)_{+}^{\alpha/2}-a^{\alpha/2}]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{1+\alpha}}=a^{-\alpha/2}A
, where

A=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(1+b)_{+}^{\alpha/2}-1]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{1+\alpha}}=A_{1}+A_{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha},

A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,
-\frac{1}{\alpha}
corresponding to the integrals on
(0,\infty)
,
(-1,0)
,
(-\infty,-1)
.
The values of
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are given in
[
42
, Proof of Lemma 9, page 143]
(with

\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}
). We find
A=0
.

When
\alpha\in(1,2)
, we have
\mathcal{K}\psi_{*}(a)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(a+b)_{+}^{\alpha/2}-a^{\alpha/2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}a^{\alpha/2-1}b]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{1+\alpha}}=a^{-\alpha/2}B
, where

B=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(1+b)_{+}^{\alpha/2}-1-\frac{\alpha}{2}b]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{1+\alpha}}=B_{1}+B_{3}+\frac{\alpha}{2(\alpha-1)}-\frac{1}{\alpha},

B_{1}
,
B_{3}
,
\frac{\alpha}{2(\alpha-1)}-\frac{1}{\alpha}
corresponding to the integrals on

(0,\infty)
,
(-1,0)
,
(-\infty,-1)
. The values of
B_{1}
and
B_{3}
are given
in
[
42
, Proof of Lemma 9, pages 144-145]
(with
\beta=\frac{\alpha}{2}
): we find
B=0
.

When
\alpha=1
,
\mathcal{K}\psi_{*}(a)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(a+b)_{+}^{1/2}-a^{1/2}-\frac{1}{2a^{1/2}}b{\bm{1}}_{\{|b|<1\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{2}}=a^{-1/2}C
, with

C=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(1+b)_{+}^{1/2}-1-\frac{1}{2}b{\bm{1}}_{\{|b|<1/a\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(1+b)_{+}^{1/2}-1-\frac{1}{2}b{\bm{1}}_{\{|b|<1\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}b}{|b|^{2}}=C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{4},

corresponding to the integral on

(1,\infty)
,
(0,1)
,
(-1,0)
and
(-\infty,-1)
.
One can show that

\displaystyle C_{1}=\sqrt{2}+\log(1+\sqrt{2})-1,\;\;C_{2}=\frac{3}{2}-\sqrt{2}-\log(1+\sqrt{2})+\log 2,\;\;C_{3}=\frac{1}{2}-\log 2,\;\;C_{4}=-1,

so that
C=0
.

Step 9.
It remains to prove (a). We consider
\varphi\in C^{2}(\mathcal{D})
such that

\varphi(x)=[d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})]^{\alpha/2}
as soon as
d(x,\mathcal{D}^{c})\leq 1/2
,
i.e.

\varphi(x)=(1-|x|)^{\alpha/2}
as soon as
|x|\geq 1/2
. We
only study the case
\alpha\in(1,2)
, the cases
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\alpha=1

being treated similarly, with some variations.
We have to show that
\mathcal{L}\varphi
is bounded on
\mathcal{D}
.
Since
\mathcal{L}\varphi\in C(\mathcal{D})
by Remark
83
,
it suffices that
\mathcal{L}\varphi
is bounded on

K=\{x\in\mathcal{D}:d(x,D^{c})\leq 1/4\}
and, by symmetry, on
K_{1}=\{x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}:x_{1}\in[3/4,1)\}
.
For
x\in K_{1}
, we have
\varphi(x)=(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2}
,

\nabla\varphi(x)=-\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}\mathbf{e}_{1}

and
d(x+z,\mathcal{D}^{c})\leq 1/2
if
|z|<1/4
, so that, using (
134
) with
a=1/4
,

\mathcal{L}\varphi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z))-\varphi(x)-z\cdot\nabla\varphi(x){\bm{1}}_{\{|z|<1/4\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi(x)+\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi(x)+\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x),

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|\geq 1/4\}}[\varphi(\Lambda(x,x+z)))-\varphi(x)]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1/4\}}[(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}^{\alpha/2}-(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}z_{1}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1/4\}}[d(\Lambda(x,x+z),\mathcal{D}^{c})^{\alpha/2}-(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}^{\alpha/2}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}.

First,
\mathcal{L}_{1}\varphi
is bounded because
\varphi
is bounded. Next,

\mathcal{L}_{2}\varphi(x)=\mathcal{L}_{21}\varphi(x)-\mathcal{L}_{22}\varphi(x)
, with

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{21}\varphi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}^{\alpha/2}-(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}z_{1}{\bm{1}}_{\{|z|<1/4\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{22}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|\geq 1/4\}}[(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}^{\alpha/2}-(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}.

Integrating in
z_{2},\dots,z_{d}
, setting
y=1-x_{1}>0
and using the substitution
u=-z_{1}
, we find that
for some constant
c>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{21}\varphi(x)=

\displaystyle c\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}^{\alpha/2}-(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}z_{1}{\bm{1}}_{\{|z_{1}|<1/4\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{|z_{1}|^{1+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\int_{\mathbb{R}}[(y+u)_{+}^{\alpha/2}-y^{\alpha/2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}y^{\alpha/2-1}u{\bm{1}}_{\{|u|<1/4\}}]\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{|u|^{1+\alpha}}=0

by Step 7. Moreover,
\mathcal{L}_{22}\varphi(x)
is bounded, because

|(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}^{\alpha/2}-(1-x_{1})^{\alpha/2}|\leq|z_{1}|^{\alpha/2}
, which is integrable against
|z|^{-\alpha-d}
.

Set
\mathbb{H}_{1}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:y_{1}<1\}
, which contains
\mathcal{D}=B_{d}(0,1)
.
Observe that if
x+z\notin\mathbb{H}_{1}
, then
d(\Lambda(x,x+z),\mathcal{D}^{c})=0
and
(1-x_{1}-z_{1})_{+}=d(x+z,\mathbb{H}_{1}^{c})=0
.
We thus may write
\mathcal{L}_{3}\varphi(x)=-\mathcal{L}_{31}\varphi(x)-\mathcal{L}_{32}\varphi(x)
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{31}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1/4\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{x+z\in\mathbb{H}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{D}\}}[(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2}-d(\Lambda(x,x+z),\mathcal{D}^{c})^{\alpha/2}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{32}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1/4\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{x+z\in\mathcal{D}\}}[(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2}-d(\Lambda(x,x+z),\mathcal{D}^{c})^{\alpha/2}]\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}.

If
x+z\in\mathbb{H}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{D}
,
d(\Lambda(x,x+z),\mathcal{D}^{c})=0
, so that

\mathcal{L}_{31}\varphi(x)=\int_{\{|z|<1/4\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{x+z\in\mathbb{H}_{1}\setminus B_{d}(0,1)\}}(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}.

Recalling that
x=x_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}
and using the notation
z=(z_{1},v)
, so that
x+z=(x_{1}+z_{1},v)
,
we have
1-|v|^{2}\leq x_{1}+z_{1}<1
as soon as
|z|<1/4
and
x+z\in\mathbb{H}_{1}\setminus B_{d}(0,1)
: indeed,

|x+z|^{2}\geq 1
tells us that
(x_{1}+z_{1})^{2}+|v|^{2}\geq 1
, while
x_{1}+z_{1}<1
since
x+z\in\mathbb{H}_{1}

and
x_{1}+z_{1}\geq 0
since
x_{1}\geq 3/4
and
|z|<1/4
, so that
x_{1}+z_{1}\geq(x_{1}+z_{1})^{2}\geq 1-|v|^{2}
.
Since finally
|z|\geq|v|
,

0\leq\mathcal{L}_{31}\varphi(x)\leq\int_{B_{d-1}(0,1/4)}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\alpha}}\int_{1-x_{1}-|v|^{2}}^{1-x_{1}}\!\!\!(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}z_{1}=\frac{1}{\alpha/2+1}\int_{B_{d-1}(0,1/4)}\frac{|v|^{2+\alpha}\mathrm{d}v}{|v|^{d+\alpha}},

and
\mathcal{L}_{31}\varphi
is bounded, because
2+\alpha-d-\alpha>1-d
.

To treat
\mathcal{L}_{32}
, we observe that
x+z\in B_{d}(0,1)
, whence

\Delta(x,z):=(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2}-d(\Lambda(x,x+z),D^{c})^{\alpha/2}=(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2}-(1-|x+z|)^{\alpha/2}\geq 0,

Moreover,
x+z\in B_{d}(0,1)
and
|z|<1/4
imply that
x_{1}+z_{1}\in(1/2,1)
(because
x_{1}\in[3/4,1)
)
and that, still with the notation
z=(z_{1},v)
, so that
x+z=(x_{1}+z_{1},v)
,

\displaystyle\Delta(x,z)\leq

\displaystyle(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}[|x+z|-x_{1}-z_{1}]\leq(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}|v|^{2}.

We used that
a^{\alpha/2}-b^{\alpha/2}\leq a^{\alpha/2-1}(a-b)
for
a\geq b\geq 0

and that, since
\sqrt{1+u}\leq 1+\frac{u}{2}
for all
u\geq 0
, it holds that

0\leq|x+z|-x_{1}-z_{1}=(x_{1}+z_{1})\Big(\sqrt{1+\frac{|v|^{2}}{(x_{1}+z_{1})^{2}}}-1\Big)\leq\frac{|v|^{2}}{2(x_{1}+z_{1})}\leq|v|^{2}.

All this shows that

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathcal{L}_{32}\varphi(x)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\{|z|<1/4\}}{\bm{1}}_{\{1/2<x_{1}+z_{1}<1\}}(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}|v|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{d+\alpha}}.

We now set
\theta=d+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha}{4}
and use that
|z|^{-d-\alpha}\leq[\max(|v|,|z_{1}|)]^{-d-\alpha}\leq|v|^{-\theta}|z_{1}|^{\theta-d-\alpha}
(we have
\theta\in[0,d+\alpha]
since
\alpha>1\geq\frac{2}{3}
) to get,
for some constant
C
depending only on
\alpha
and
d
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{32}\varphi(x)|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\{|v|<1/4\}}|v|^{2-\theta}\mathrm{d}v\int_{1/2-x_{1}}^{1-x_{1}}(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}|z_{1}|^{\theta-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{-1}^{1-x_{1}}(1-x_{1}-z_{1})^{\alpha/2-1}|z_{1}|^{\theta-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}z_{1},

since
2-\theta>1-d
(because
\alpha<2
) and
x_{1}<1
. Substituting
z_{1}=-(1-x_{1})u
, we find

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{32}\varphi(x)|\leq

\displaystyle C(1-x_{1})^{\theta-d-\alpha/2}\int_{-1}^{1/(1-x_{1})}(1+u)^{\alpha/2-1}|u|^{\theta-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1-x_{1})^{\theta-d-\alpha/2}\Big[\int_{-1}^{1}(1+u)^{\alpha/2-1}|u|^{\theta-d-\alpha}\mathrm{d}u+\int_{1}^{1/(1-x_{1})}u^{\theta-1-d-\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}u\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1-x_{1})^{\theta-d-\alpha/2}[1+(1-x_{1})^{-(\theta-d-\alpha/2)}].

We finally used that
\alpha/2-1>-1
, that
\theta-d-\alpha>-1
(since
\alpha<2
), and that

\theta-d-\alpha/2>0
(since
\alpha<2
). Recalling that
x_{1}\in(3/4,1)
, we conclude that
\mathcal{L}_{32}\varphi

is bounded.
∎

Appendix C
Skorokhod topologies

For
x_{k},x\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we say that
x_{k}\to x
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology,
see Jacod and Shiryaev
[
47
, Chapter VI]
, if
there exists a family of continuous increasing functions
\lambda_{k}=\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
\lambda_{k}(0)=0
such that

\lim_{k}||\lambda_{k}-I||_{\infty}=0\quad\text{and for all $T>0$,}\quad\lim_{k}||x_{k}\circ\lambda_{k}-x||_{\infty,T}=0,

where
||\lambda_{k}-I||_{\infty}=\sup_{t\geq 0}|\lambda_{k}(t)-t|
and

||x_{k}\circ\lambda_{k}-x||_{\infty,T}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|x_{k}(\lambda_{k}(t))-x(t)|
.

We now recall the notion of convergence in the
{\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{1}}
-topology, see Whitt
[
73
, Chapter 12]
.
For
x\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we define the completed graph
\Gamma_{x}
of
x
as

\Gamma_{x}=\big\{(t,y)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\>y\in[x(t-),x(t)]\big\}.

A parametric representation of
x
is a continuous function
(u,r)
mapping
\mathbb{R}_{+}

onto
\Gamma_{x}
such that
for any
0\leq s\leq t
, either (i)
u(s)<u(t)
, or
(ii)
u(s)=u(t)
and for any
i=1,\cdots,d
,

|x_{i}(u(s)-)-r_{i}(s)|\leq|x_{i}(u(t)-)-r_{i}(t)|
.
Let
\Pi_{x}
be the set of parametric representations of
x
.

For
x_{k},x\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we say that
x_{k}\to x
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{1}}
-topology if
there exists a sequence
(u_{k},r_{k})\in\Pi_{x_{k}}
and
(u,r)\in\Pi_{x}
such that

\text{for all $T>0$,}\quad\lim_{k}(||u_{k}-u||_{\infty,T}+||r_{k}-r||_{\infty,T})=0.

The following lemma must be standard, but we found no precise reference.

Lemma 84
.

Consider a family of continuous piecewise affine elements
(x_{k})_{k\geq 1}
of
C(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
: for some
0=t_{0}^{k}<t_{1}^{k}<\dots
such that
\lim_{n}t^{k}_{n}=\infty
, we have, for all
n\geq 0
,

x_{k}(t)=x_{k}(t^{k}_{n})+\frac{t-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}(x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}))\quad\text{for all $t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})$.}

(136)

For each
k\geq 1
, define

\bar{x}_{k}(t)=\sum_{n\geq 0}x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}){\bm{1}}_{\{t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})\}}
and, for
t\geq 0
, set

m^{k}_{t}=\sum_{n\geq 1}{\bm{1}}_{\{t^{k}_{n}\leq t\}}
.
If
\bar{x}_{k}\to x\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})

for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology and if for all
T>0
,

\lim_{k}\sup_{n=0,\dots,m^{k}_{T}}(t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n})=0,

then
x_{k}\to x
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{1}}
-topology.

Proof.

Since the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology is stronger than the
{\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{1}}
-topology, we have
\bar{x}_{k}\to x
for
the
{\bm{\mathrm{M}}_{1}}
-topology.
Hence it suffices to show that there exist
(u_{k},r_{k})\in\Pi_{x_{k}}
and

(\bar{u}_{k},\bar{r}_{k})\in\Pi_{\bar{x}_{k}}
such that for all
T>0
,

\lim_{k}(||u_{k}-\bar{u}_{k}||_{\infty,T}+||r_{k}-\bar{r}_{k}||_{\infty,T})=0
.

Since
x_{k}
is continuous, we naturally
define
(u_{k},r_{k})\in\Pi_{x_{k}}
by setting
(u_{k}(t),r_{k}(t))=(t,x_{k}(t))
.

We then define
(\bar{u}_{k},\bar{r}_{k})
:
for all
n\geq 0
, we set

A^{k}_{n}=k|x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k}(t^{k}_{n})|+2\quad\text{and}\quad s^{k}_{n}=t^{k}_{n}+\frac{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}{A^{k}_{n}}\in(t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1}),

and, for
t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})
,

(\bar{u}_{k}(t),\bar{r}_{k}(t))=\begin{cases}\Big(t^{k}_{n}+A^{k}_{n}(t-t^{k}_{n}),x_{k}(t^{k}_{n})\Big)&\text{if $t\in[t^{k}_{n},s^{k}_{n}]$},\\[10.0pt]
\Big(t^{k}_{n+1},x_{k}(t^{k}_{n})+\frac{t-s^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-s^{k}_{n}}(x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k}(t^{k}_{n})\Big)&\text{if $t\in(s^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})$}.\end{cases}

Let us check that
(\bar{u}_{k},\bar{r}_{k})\in\Pi_{\bar{x}_{k}}
.
One easily checks that
(\bar{u}_{k},\bar{r}_{k})
is a continuous bijection from
\mathbb{R}_{+}
onto

\Gamma_{\bar{x}_{k}}=\cup_{n\geq 0}\Big[\Big([t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})\times\{x_{k}(t_{n}^{k})\}\Big)\cup\Big(\{t^{k}_{n+1}\}\times[x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}),x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1}))]\}\Big)\Big].

Next for
0\leq s<t
, if
\bar{u}_{k}(s)=\bar{u}_{k}(t)
, then there is
n\geq 0
such that

s^{k}_{n}\leq s<t\leq t^{k}_{n+1}
, so that
\bar{u}_{k}(s)=\bar{u}_{k}(t)=t^{k}_{n+1}
, whence

\bar{x}_{k}(\bar{u}_{k}(s)-)=x_{k}(t^{k}_{n})
. Thus for all
i=1,\dots,d
,

\displaystyle|\bar{x}_{k,i}(\bar{u}_{k}(s)-)-\bar{r}_{k,i}(s)|=

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{s-s^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-s^{k}_{n}}\Big||x_{k,i}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k,i}(t^{k}_{n})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{t-s^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-s^{k}_{n}}\Big||x_{k,i}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k,i}(t^{k}_{n})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\bar{x}_{k,i}(\bar{u}_{k}(t)-)-\bar{r}_{k,i}(t)|.

For each

n\geq 0
, each
t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})
, both
\bar{u}_{k}(t)
and
u_{k}(t)
belong to
[t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1}]
,
so that

\text{for all $T>0$,}\quad||u_{k}-\bar{u}_{k}||_{\infty,T}\leq\sup_{n=0,\dots,m^{k}_{T}}(t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}),

which tends to
0
by assumption.

We now show that
||r_{k}-\bar{r}_{k}||_{\infty}\leq 1/k
and this well complete the proof.
Fix
t\geq 0
and let
n\geq 0
such that
t\in[t^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})
. Recall the definition of
A^{k}_{n}
,
that
r_{k}=x_{k}
and (
136
). If first
t\in[t^{k}_{n},s^{k}_{n}]
, then

|r_{k}(t)-\bar{r}_{k}(t)|=\Big|\frac{t-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}(x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}))\Big|\leq\Big|\frac{s^{k}_{n}-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}(x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}))\Big|\leq\frac{1}{k},

while if
t\in[s^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1})
, then

|r_{k}(t)\!-\!\bar{r}_{k}(t)|\!=\!\Big|\Big(\frac{t-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}-\frac{t-s^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-s^{k}_{n}}\Big)(x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})-x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}))\Big|\!\leq\!\Big|\frac{s^{k}_{n}-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}(x_{k}(t^{k}_{n+1})\!-\!x_{k}(t^{k}_{n}))\Big|\!\leq\!\frac{1}{k}.

We used that the function
t\mapsto\frac{t-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}-\frac{t-s^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-s^{k}_{n}}
is
decreasing on
[s^{k}_{n},t^{k}_{n+1}]
, equals
\frac{s^{k}_{n}-t^{k}_{n}}{t^{k}_{n+1}-t^{k}_{n}}
when
t=s^{k}_{n}

and equals
0
when
t=t^{k}_{n+1}
.
∎

Lemma 85
.

Consider a family
(x_{k})_{k\geq 1}
of elements of
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
x_{k}\to x\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})

for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology, and a family
(\rho_{k})_{k\geq 1}
of nondecreasing elements of
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})

such that
\sup_{[0,T]}|\rho_{k}(t)-t|=0
for all
T>0
. Then
x_{k}\circ\rho_{k}\to x
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.

Proof.

We denote by
\mathbb{D}^{\uparrow}
the set of nondecreasing càdlàg functions from

\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
. We have that
\rho_{k}\to\rho=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}

in
\mathbb{D}^{\uparrow}
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology
and that
x_{k}\to x
in
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
Since
\rho
is continuous, we conclude by Whitt
[
72
, Theorem 3.1]

that
x_{k}\circ\rho_{k}\to x
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology.
∎

Lemma 86
.

Consider a family
((z_{n}(u)_{u\geq 0})_{n\geq 1}
of increasing elements of
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
such that
z_{n}(0)=0
and

\lim_{u\to\infty}z_{n}(u)=\infty
. Assume that
((z_{n}(u))_{u\geq 0})_{n\geq 1}

converges to some strictly increasing
(z(u))_{u\geq 0}\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R})
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology, with

\lim_{u\to\infty}z(u)=\infty
.
For
t\geq 0
, set
y(t)=\inf\{u\geq 0:z(u)>t\}
and
y_{n}(t)=\inf\{u\geq 0:z_{n}(u)>t\}
.

(i) For all
T>0
,
