\lim_{n}\sup_{[0,T]}|y_{n}(t)-y(t)|=0
.

(ii) If
\lim_{n}t_{n}=t>0
, and
z(y(t)-)<t<z(y(t))
, then
\lim_{n}\Delta z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n}))=\Delta z(y(t))
.

Proof.

For (i), it suffices, by Dini’s theorem and since
y
is continuous (because
z
is increasing)
to show that
\lim_{n}y_{n}(t)=y(t)
for all fixed
t>0
. Let us e.g. show that
\limsup_{n}y_{n}(t)\leq y(t)
,
the proof that
\liminf_{n}y_{n}(t)\geq y(t)
being similar (but slightly easier). Fix
\varepsilon>0

and
u
a continuity point of
z
such that
y(t+\varepsilon)<u
. This implies that
t+\varepsilon\leq z(u)
, so that (since

\lim_{n}z_{n}(u)=z(u)
because
u
is a continuity point of
z
)
t<z_{n}(u)
for all
n
large enough.
Consequently,
y_{n}(t)\leq u
for all
n
large enough, whence
\limsup_{n}y_{n}(t)\leq u
. Since we can choose

u
arbitrarily close to
y(t+\varepsilon)
(because the set of jump times of
z
is at most countable),
we conclude that
\limsup_{n}y_{n}(t)\leq y(t+\varepsilon)
. It only remains to let

\varepsilon\to 0
, using that
y
is continuous.

For (ii), we e.g. show that
z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n}))\to z(y(t))
, the proof that
z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n})-)\to z(y(t)-)
being similar.
Then sequence
z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n}))
being compact in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
(since
z_{n}
is locally uniformly bounded
and since
y_{n}(t_{n})\to y(t)
by (i)), it suffices to show that the only adherent point of

z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n}))
is
z(y(t))
.

We first show that if
s_{n}\to s
, then the only possible
adherent points of
z_{n}(s_{n})
are
z(s-)
and
z(s)
. To this end, consider a time change
\lambda_{n}

such that
||\lambda_{n}-I||_{\infty}\to 0

and
||z_{n}-z\circ\lambda_{n}||_{\infty,T}\to 0
(with e.g.
T=s+1
), write
z_{n}(s_{n})=z_{n}(s_{n})-z(\lambda_{n}(s_{n}))+z(\lambda_{n}(s_{n}))
, use that
z_{n}(s_{n})-z(\lambda_{n}(s_{n}))\to 0
and that the only possible
adherent points of
z(\lambda_{n}(s_{n}))
are
z(s-)
and
z(s)
.

Since
y_{n}(t_{n})\to y(t)
by (i), we deduce that the only possible adherent points of

z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n}))
are
z(y(t)-)
and
z(y(t))
.
But by definition, we have
z_{n}(y(t_{n}))\geq t_{n}\to t
. Since
z(y(t)-)<t
,
we conclude that the only adherent point of
z_{n}(y_{n}(t_{n}))
is
z(y(t))
.
∎

Finally, we check the following easy fact.

Lemma 87
.

Assume that
x_{n},x\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
t_{n},t\geq 0
are such that
x_{n}\to x
for the
{\bm{\mathrm{J}}_{1}}
-topology and

t_{n}\to t
. We take the convention that
x_{n}(0-)=x_{n}(0)
and
x(0-)=x(0)
.

(a) There exists
n_{k}\to\infty
such that
x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})\to x(t)
or

x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})\to x(t-)
.

(b) There exists
n_{k}\to\infty
such that
x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}}-)\to x(t)
or
x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}}-)\to x(t-)
.

Proof.

We consider
\lambda_{n}=\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
continuous increasing, with
\lambda_{n}(0)=0
,

\lim_{n}||\lambda_{n}-I||_{\infty}=0
and
\lim_{n}||y_{n}-x||_{\infty,T}=0
for all
T>0
, where
y_{n}=x_{n}\circ\lambda_{n}
.
We set
s_{n}=\lambda_{n}^{-1}(t_{n})
, so that
x_{n}(t_{n})=y_{n}(s_{n})
and
x_{n}(t_{n}-)=y_{n}(s_{n}-)
. We have
\lim_{n}s_{n}=t
.
We can find a subsequence
n_{k}
such that either (i)
s_{n_{k}}
is nonincreasing or (ii)
s_{n_{k}}
is strictly
increasing. In the first case,
\lim x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})=x(t)
, because (for
k
large enough)

|x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-x(t)|=|y_{n_{k}}(s_{n_{k}})-x(t)|\leq||y_{n_{k}}-x||_{\infty,t+1}+|x(s_{n_{k}})-x(t)|\to 0

since
x
is càd. In the second case,
\lim x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})=x(t-)
, because (for
k
large enough)

|x_{n_{k}}(t_{n_{k}})-x(t)|=|y_{n_{k}}(s_{n_{k}})-x(t)|\leq||y_{n_{k}}-x||_{\infty,t+1}+|x(s_{n_{k}})-x(t-)|\to 0.

This proves (a) and one can check (b) similarly.
∎

Appendix D
From the scattering process to the scattering P.D.E.

Here we show the link between the scattering process introduced in Definition
19

and the scattering P.D.E. (
19
).
We first provide a notion of weak solutions to (
19
),
see Jabir and Profeta
[
46
, Theorem 4.2.1]
and Bernou and Fournier
[
8
, Definition 4]

for similar considerations.
We introduce the sets
F_{+}=\{(x,v)\in\partial\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}:v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}>0\}
and

F_{-}=\{(x,v)\in\partial\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}:v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}<0\}
.

Definition 88
.

We say that a family
(f^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
of probability measures on
{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a weak solution
to (
19
) if there exist some measures
\nu_{+}
on

\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{+}
and
\nu_{-}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{-}
such that

\nu_{+}([0,T]\times F_{+})+\nu_{-}([0,T]\times F_{-})<\infty
for all
T>0
and
such that for all
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(0,x,v)f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{t}\varphi(t,x,v)f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi(t,x,v)f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(t,x,w)\mathrm{F}(w)\mathrm{d}w-\varphi(t,x,v)\Big)f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{+}}\varphi(t,x,v)\nu_{+}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{-}}\varphi(t,x,v)\nu_{-}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)

(137)

and

\nu_{+}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=2\mathrm{G}(v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}>0\}}\nu_{-}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathbb{R}^{d})\mathrm{d}v.

(138)

The following remark explains this definition.

Remark 89
.

If
(f^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a smooth weak solution to (
19
), then it
solves (
19
).

Proof.

We assume that
f^{\varepsilon}_{t}
has a smooth density that we still denote by
f^{\varepsilon}_{t}
.
An integration by parts shows that the first line of (
137
) equals

-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(t,x,v)\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t.

The Green formula implies that the second line of (
137
) equals

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(t,x,v)v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\partial\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(t,x,v)(v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x})f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t.

Finally, the third line of (
137
) equals

\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(t,x,v)\Big(\mathrm{F}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,w)\mathrm{d}w-f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\Big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t.

All in all, since (
137
) holds true for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})

(with
\mathcal{D}
open so that
\varphi
vanishes on
\partial\mathcal{D}
), we find
that for all
(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathrm{F}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,w)\mathrm{d}w-f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\Big).

Hence
(f^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
solves the first line of (
19
). Moreover, once this is seen,
(
137
) rewrites, for all
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\int_{\partial\mathcal{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\varphi(t,x,v)(v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x})f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{+}}\varphi(t,x,v)\nu_{+}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{-}}\varphi(t,x,v)\nu_{-}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=0

Thus
\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}(v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x})f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t=(\nu_{+}-\nu_{-})(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)
,
so that necessarily

\displaystyle\nu_{+}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}(v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x})f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}>0\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\nu_{-}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}|v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}|f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}<0\}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t.

Hence (
138
) tells us that for all
t\geq 0
, all
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
, all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

(v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x})f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}>0\}}=2\mathrm{G}(v)\int_{w\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}<0}|w\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}|f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x,w)\mathrm{d}w,

so that
(f^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
solves the second line of (
19
).
∎

We end the paper with the

Proof of Remark
21
.

We fix
(x_{0},v_{0})\in\bm{E}
(recall (
20
)),
consider a measurable family

(A_{y})_{y\in\partial\mathcal{D}}
such that
A_{y}\in\mathcal{I}_{y}
for each
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and divide the proof into 3 steps.

Step 1.
Consider a Poisson measure
\mathrm{N}_{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\delta_{(S^{\varepsilon}_{k},Y^{\varepsilon}_{k})}

on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
with intensity measure

\varepsilon^{-1}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{d}v)
, independent of a collection of i.i.d.
\mathrm{G}_{+}
-distributed
random variables
(W_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, recall that
\mathrm{G}_{+}(v)=2\mathrm{G}(v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}>0\}}
.
Consider the càdlàg process
(\bm{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon},\bm{V}_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
solving

\left\{\begin{aligned} \bm{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}&=x_{0}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{t}\bm{V}_{s}^{\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}s,\\
\bm{V}_{t}^{\varepsilon}&=v_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(z-\bm{V}_{s-}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\mathrm{N}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}z)+\sum_{n\geq 1}\Big(A_{\bm{X}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}W_{n}-\bm{V}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}-}^{\varepsilon}\Big){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau_{n}\leq t\}},\\
\tau_{0}^{\varepsilon}&=0\quad\text{and}\quad\tau_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}=\inf\{t>\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon},\>\bm{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\in\partial\mathcal{D}\}.\end{aligned}\right.

(139)

The intensity of the Poisson measure
\mathrm{N}_{\varepsilon}

being finite on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
for all
T>0
, (
139
)
has a pathwise unique solution, which is an
\varepsilon
-scattering process starting from
(x,v)
, see
Definition
19
.

Indeed, (
139
) tells us that the position process
\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}
moves according to its velocity

\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/\alpha}\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}
; that the velocity process
\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}
is refreshed at rate

\varepsilon^{-1}
, its new value being chosen according to
\mathrm{F}
; and that when the position process reaches the boundary

\partial\mathcal{D}
(at some time
\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}
), it is restarted with a
\mathrm{G}_{\bm{X}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}^{\varepsilon}}
-distributed
velocity (recall that for
y\in\partial\mathcal{D}
and
W\sim\mathrm{G}_{+}(v)\mathrm{d}v
,
A_{y}W\sim\mathrm{G}_{y}(v)\mathrm{d}v
).

The process built in Definition
19
has exactly the same dynamics.

Step 2.
Here we observe that
\mathbb{E}[M^{\varepsilon}_{T}]<\infty
for all
T>0
, where

M^{\varepsilon}_{T}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\bm{1}}_{\{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{n}\leq T\}}
. Since
M^{\varepsilon}_{T}\leq\bm{M}^{\varepsilon}_{T}
, where
\bm{M}^{\varepsilon}_{T}
stands
for the total number of jumps of

(\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
during
[0,T]
, this follows from Remark
20
.

Step 3.
For
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and for
T>0
,
we deduce from (
139
) that

\displaystyle\varphi(T,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{T},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{T})=

\displaystyle\varphi(0,x_{0},v_{0})+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\partial_{t}\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t})+\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t})\Big)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big(\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},z)-\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t-})\Big)\mathrm{N}_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geq 1}\Big(\varphi(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon},\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}},A_{\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}}W_{n})-\varphi(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon},\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}-})\Big){\bm{1}}_{\{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{n}\leq T\}}.

Taking expectations and choosing
T
such that Supp
\varphi\subset[0,T]\times{}\mkern 3.0mu\overline{\mkern-3.0mu\mathcal{D}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
we get

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\varphi(0,x_{0},v_{0})+\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\partial_{t}\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t})+\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t})\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},w)\mathrm{F}(w)\mathrm{d}w-\varphi(t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t})\Big)\mathrm{d}t\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{n\geq 1}\Big(\varphi(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon},\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}},A_{\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}}W_{n})-\varphi(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon},\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}-})\Big)\Big]

Denoting by
f^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\in\mathrm{d}x,\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\in\mathrm{d}v)
, we see that

(f^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
satisfies (
137
) with
f_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\delta_{(x_{0},v_{0})}
and with the measures

\displaystyle\nu_{+}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathrm{d}t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{d}x,A_{\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}}W_{n}\in\mathrm{d}v\Big),

\displaystyle\nu_{-}(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}v)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathrm{d}t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{d}x,\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}-}\in\mathrm{d}v\Big),

which are respectively carried by
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{+}
and
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times F_{-}
. By Step 2, we have

\nu_{+}([0,T]\times F_{+})=\nu_{-}([0,T]\times F_{-})=\mathbb{E}[M^{\varepsilon}_{T}]<\infty
, and this moreover allows us to justify
the previous computations of the present step.
It remains to check (
138
). But for each
n\geq 1
, since
W_{n}
is independent
of
(\tau^{\varepsilon}_{n},\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{n}},\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{n}-})
and

A_{x}W_{n}\sim 2\mathrm{G}(v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}>0\}}\mathrm{d}v
for each
x\in\partial\mathcal{D}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathrm{d}t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{d}x,A_{\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}}W_{n}\in\mathrm{d}v\Big)=2\mathrm{G}(v){\bm{1}}_{\{v\cdot\mathbf{n}_{x}>0\}}\mathrm{d}v\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathrm{d}t,\bm{X}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{n}^{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{d}x,\bm{V}^{\varepsilon}_{\tau^{\varepsilon}_{n}-}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\Big).

Summing this identity on
n\geq 1
precisely gives (
138
).
∎
